It claims neither monotonicity of the actual F under deletion nor
preservation of its backward P,Q. That distinction is justified by(23),
which already holds for every actual source instance before deletion.

## 7. All-count source upper comparison, including the first factor

Assume temporarily either

    (I) C>=r_h, or (II) A>=2C M_max.                   (27)

Section 8 proves that every parameter pair satisfies this dichotomy.
All arguments here use C>=1, A>=2C and0<u<=1/2.

FIRST SELECTED MINIMUM, with at least one selection after it.
Let R_next=2A+epsilon b_next. Cancelling the positive next numerator
and the identical remaining factors in(23), deletion is valid exactly
when

    b(s+b+t+2)<=(s+2)(Ct+R_next).                      (28)

The difference on the right increases with s,t,R_next: its derivatives
in s,t are Ct+R_next-b and C(s+2)-b, positive on(26) since
R_next>=4C, b=d+1<=M/2+1. Thus it suffices to set s=t=M-1.
Under(I), lower R_next to4C. The difference is exactly

    M^2[C-u(2+u)]+M[4C-2-2u]+3C-1.                   (29)

Since u<=c-2 gives u(2+u)<=r_h<=C, all three grouped terms are
nonnegative. Under(II), R_next>=4C M_max>=4CM, so the difference is
at least

    (M+1)C(5M-1)-(M/2+1)(5M/2+1)
      >=(15/4)M^2+M-2>0,                             (30)

using C>=1 and M>=1. This proves the new mixed first-factor deletion.

NONFIRST, NONLAST SELECTED MINIMUM.
Let Q_b=2A+epsilon b and R_next=2A+epsilon b_next. The exact required
inequality is

    b[C(s+b+t)+R_next]<=(Cs+Q_b)(Ct+R_next).           (31)

Its difference increases in Q_b,R_next,s,t: the nontrivial derivative
terms are Cs+Q_b-b and C(Ct+R_next-b), which are positive because
C(M-1)+4C-b>0. Lower s,t to M-1 and Q_b,R_next to2A. Put q=2A-C>=3C.
The resulting minimum difference is

    (CM+q)^2-(d+1)[C(2M+d)+q]
     =M^2[C^2-Cu(2+u)]
          +M[q(2C-u)-C(2+u)]+q^2-q.                 (32)

In(I) the first coefficient is nonnegative. The second is at least
C(6C-4u-2)>=2C, and q^2-q>=0. In(II),
q=2A-C>=C(4M_max-1)>=3CM, so z=q/M>=3C. Dividing the first
line of(32) by M^2 bounds it below by

    (C+z)^2-(3/2)[(5/2)C+z]
       >=16C^2-(33/4)C>0.                            (33)

Here(d+1)/M<=3/2, (2M+d)/M<=5/2, and the polynomial increases for
z>=3C,C>=1. This proves(31), with the full numerator b=d+1 retained.

LAST, NONFIRST SELECTED MINIMUM.
The ratio of the selected local factor to the deleted one is

    b^2(1+s+b+t)/[(Cs+2A+epsilon b)^2(1+t)]
       <=b^2(1+s+b+t)/[(s+4)^2(1+t)].                 (34)

The last expression decreases in t. Its s derivative, apart from its
positive factors, is2-s-2b-2t<0. Its maximum on(26) is therefore

    (d+1)^2(2M+d)/[(M+3)^2 M]
       <=(5/2)[(M/2+1)/(M+3)]^2<=5/8.               (35)

This case needs no choice between(I) and(II).

Repeat minimum-level deletion while there are at least two selections.
Each step is covered by(28),(31), or(34) and makes Psi_S nondecreasing.
There are finitely many selections, so the process reaches ONE eligible
checkpoint. It never requires defining a fictitious empty version of
the exceptional first factor. This is an induction, not an executed
recurrence or an enumeration of subsets.

For the remaining checkpoint put X=1+s>=M and Y=1+t>=M. Then
S_m=s+b+t=X+Y+d-1, and its upper ratio is

    Psi_S/H_base(H_m)
     =(d+1)^2(2X+2Y+2d-1)/[(X+1)^2Y].                (36)

Source eligibility(2) gives d>=9. The expression decreases with X and Y:
the X derivative has sign4-2X-4Y-4d<0 and the Y derivative has sign
-(2X+2d-1)<0. It is at most

    [(d+1)/(M+1)]^2(4+2u-1/M).

Since M>=9/u and0<u<21/50,

    (d+1)/(M+1)=u+(1-u)/(M+1)
        <=10u/(9+u)<70/157<9/20.

The last strict comparison is1400<1413. Also2+u<121/50. Thus

    Psi_S/H_base(H_m)<2(121/50)(9/20)^2=9801/10000.     (37)

Equations(23),(28)--(37) prove this same strict bound for EVERY
nonempty source selection, at arbitrary count, under(27). No lower
guarantee was used as an upper estimate.

## 8. Every independent parameter pair satisfies the source dichotomy

If C>=r_h then(I) holds. In the other case C<r_h, equations(1),(25)
give epsilon^2<C^2-1<4/m+4/m^2<=6/m<=1/256. Therefore epsilon<1/16 and

    theta_sp=pi/2-arctan epsilon>23/16>1,
    kappa theta_sp<=log C<log r_h<2/m,
    kappa<2/m<=1/768<1/16.                            (38)

The elementary inequalities pi>3 and arctan epsilon<epsilon justify
the first line; pi>3 follows from the inscribed regular hexagon.
The exponential branch for T is legitimate here because kappa<1 has
now been proved. Put X_sp=(1-kappa^2)theta_sp/kappa. Then

    X_sp>((1-kappa^2)theta_sp^2/2)m
         >(134895/131072)m>m.

The exact identity
1+(1+kappa)T=(exp(X_sp)-kappa)/(1-kappa)>=exp(X_sp) yields

    A>=C exp(X_sp)/epsilon>16C exp(m)>2C M_max.         (39)

For the last step, M_max<=c^(m-1) and exp(1)>5/2>c. This proves(II).
It covers all parameter scalings with m, including oscillatory choices.
For kappa>=1, the argument automatically stays in(I). No ordering of
kappa and epsilon is used, and epsilon=1/2 is included.

## 9. Main realization: all counts on the band C>=r_h

We rederive the actual main construction to state this additional scope
without treating the older unverified draft as a premise. Apply Section2
to the FULL physical ordinary blocks. In this section P_i,Q_i are their
full-step outputs. Set

    M_i^tr=P_i+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon),
    sigma_i=1/M_i^tr, D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).

Use(12) with b_loc=b_i, sigma=sigma_i, obtaining phi_i,c_(i,1),
c_(i,2),U_i,Y_i. Conditions(11) hold by definition. Define

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), Phi_k(z)=delta max(z_k-ell_k,0),
    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i(z), f=env_1 J, x_0=e_0.    (40)

The local proximal points have second coordinate
sigma_i(1+Q_i-y_j+q_j) in[0,sigma_i(1+Q_i)]. Their first coordinate
minus U_i is sigma_i[epsilon(b_i-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0, by b_i>=B_sp and
the last estimates of Section2. Therefore at each proposed global
proximal point all preceding terms are in their post-checkpoint zero
wedges, the next has first local input at most0, and every further term
is in its pre-activation zero wedge. The current local subgradient is
an active subgradient of J, so it is the actual envelope gradient.

The physical state induction is the one with frozen coordinates
ell_j+D_j U_j, current coordinate ell_i+D_i, and later zeros.
After the checkpoint the new coordinate is
D_i sigma_i(Q_i+b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1). In the tail, if u is its already
executed mass, the proposed proximal coordinate remains above ell_k
because D_k-(u+1)delta>=0 for0<=u<=s_k. Thus grad f=delta e_k there and

    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).                            (41)

Section3 proves convexity and1-smoothness. For every pair of actual
samples, write z_a=x_a-grad f(x_a). The exact residual is
Q_ab=J(z_a)-J(z_b)+<grad f(x_a),z_b-z_a><=0 by the subgradient
inequality. This includes every minimizer and the unqueried terminal.

The full minimum set is

    C_env={x:x_i<=zeta_i,
               epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D_i c_(i,2), i<k;
               x_k<=ell_k}, zeta_i=ell_i+D_i c_(i,1).   (42)

Unlike the source case, z_bar=(c_(0,1),c_(0,2),0,...,0) is now feasible:
its second coordinate is ell_1, so the next local input is(0,0), and
all other terms vanish. The same normal representation(21), with
unprimed sigma and full-step outputs, proves it is the closest minimizer.
Consequently the EXACT main payoff is

    E(f,h)=(b_0-1)^2/
       {[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k)}
                     *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i^tr]^2. (43)

Define A_floor=epsilon+epsilon^(-1) and C_floor=min(C,A_floor).
The function p(theta)+q(theta)/epsilon has minimum at an endpoint.
Indeed its derivative divided by R is
(epsilon^(-1)-kappa)cos theta-(1+kappa/epsilon)sin theta, which either
is always negative or changes sign once from positive to negative.
Thus every actual arc slope obeys p+q/epsilon>=C_floor, giving

    M_i^tr>=P_i+epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon
                      >=C_floor s_i+A_floor.           (44)

By(10),(43),(44) the actual main payoff has the upper surrogate

    Psi_M=2(b_0-1)^2/[(s_0+1)^2(1+2s_k)]
              *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/(C_floor s_i+A_floor)]^2.
                                                              (45)

Assume C>=r_h. Because A_floor>=5/2>r_h, this implies C_floor>=r_h;
also A_floor>=C_floor>=1. The same minimum-level operation works.
For a FIRST minimum, the exact required comparison is

    d(s+b+t+1)<=(s+1)(C_floor t+A_floor).

Its difference increases in s,t on(26). At s=t=M-1 it is
M^2[C_floor-u(2+u)]+M(A_floor-C_floor)>=0.
For an INTERIOR nonfirst minimum the comparison is

    d[C_floor(s+b+t)+A_floor]
                    <=(C_floor s+A_floor)(C_floor t+A_floor).

Its difference increases in s,t. At s=t=M-1, divided by M^2, it is
C_floor^2-C_floor u(2+u)+z(2C_floor-u)+z^2, with
z=(A_floor-C_floor)/M>=0, hence is nonnegative.
For a LAST nonfirst minimum, since C_floor s+A_floor>=1+s, the ratio
is at most d^2[1+2(s+b+t)]/[(1+s)^2(1+2t)]. It decreases in s,t and
at their minima is at most u^2(3+2u)<=1.

Stop again with one eligible checkpoint. Put X=1+s,Y=1+t, so X,Y>=M.
Its ratio to H_base is

    2d^2(2X+2Y+2d-1)/[X^2(2Y-1)].

It decreases in both X and Y, and hence is at most
2u^2[2+u+(1+u)/(2M-1)]. Main eligibility gives d>=4, so by(25)
M>200/21>19/2. The bracket is strictly less than
121/50+(71/50)/18=2249/900<5/2. Therefore

    E(f,H_m)<=Psi_M<(441/500)H_base(H_m), C>=r_h, k>=1. (46)

The main k=0 member is Hub_(1/(1+2S_m)) from1, with closest radius1
and payoff H_base(H_m).

The remaining main regime is exactly C<r_h at arbitrary count. There
(39) controls the source's FIXED allowance A, whereas M_i^tr in(43)
uses the actual block-dependent Q_i. There is no lower bound M_i^tr>=A
in this proof. The unpaid comparison is an adequate lower bound on
P_i+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon), coupled across the actual
ordered full-step blocks, or a different quantitative use of(43).
We assert neither failure of the actual construction nor an escape
there. The scalar sum deletion of the old LOWER mass channel cannot
close this remaining actual-state interface.

## 10. Analytical boundary tests on the actual functions

The two prescribed tests are reproduced to check the normalization and
chronology of the new comparison, not used as accepted premises.

TWO SOURCE CHECKPOINTS, ZERO ORDINARY STEPS.
Set epsilon=kappa=1/2, b_0=b_1=10 and all ordinary blocks empty or zero.
Put sigma=1/(A+5/2); from(1), A>5/2 and0<sigma<1/5. The local function is

    phi(z)=sigma max{0,C(z_0-1+sigma/2),
                         (z_0-1+sigma/2)/2-z_1+sigma},
    f_loc=env_1 phi.

The full original source function on R^3 is

    F(x)=f_loc(x_0,x_1)/2
          +(25sigma^2/2)f_loc(x_1/(5sigma),x_2/(5sigma))
          +Hub_(25sigma^2)(x_2)/2.                     (47)

Its full minimum set is both scaled wedges(13) together with x_2<=0.
The point x_*=(1-sigma/2,sigma,0) is feasible. At its second local
input(1/5,0), the horizontal plane is negative and the endpoint plane
is-2/5+5sigma/4<-3/20. Its first endpoint normal is
e_0-x_*=sigma(1/2,-1,0), so it is the closest minimizer and

    rho_S^2=5sigma^2/4.

The complete actual queried states and gradients are

    x_0=e_0, g_0=(sigma/4,-sigma/2,0),
    x_1=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma,0),
    g_1=(0,5sigma^2/4,-5sigma^2/2),
    x_2=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma-25sigma^2/2,25sigma^2).

Both local terms vanish at x_2; F(x_2)=(25sigma^2)^2/4. The true payoff
is250sigma^2. The new upper formula(23) gives625sigma^2, in the correct
direction. It does not give the false50sigma^2 from extrapolating the
one-stage radius25sigma^2/4. The polyhedron keeps the terminal cap on
x_2, never inserts x_1<=0, and(21) is valid for it. This is outside(3),
so no hierarchy deletion or family escape is inferred from this test.

EQUAL MASS, DIFFERENT ORDERED OUTPUTS.
Keep epsilon=kappa=1/2. Set theta_*=arctan(3/4),
R_*=C exp(-theta_*/2), p_*=4R_*/5,q_*=3R_*/5,
A_*=1+2(1+q_*)/C. At backward endpoint(1/2,-1), a last local step1
fails(5), and(6) becomes2sin theta-(3/2)cos theta=0. Its actual root is
theta_*, with normal n(theta_*)=(1/2,-1). The local word(A_*,1) has
backward states

    r_2=(1/2,-1), r_1=(1/2+p_*,-1-q_*),
    r_0=(1/2+p_*+A_*C,-1-q_*),

and projections v_2=(1/2,-1),v_1=(p_*,-q_*),v_0=(C,0). The cap test
at A_* has right side1+q_*+1/4+p_*/2>1+q_*. Hence its actual outputs
are P=A_*C+p_*,Q=q_*>0.

For local word(1,A_*), the last A_* caps because A_*>1+3/(2C), and the
earlier step1 stays capped. Its states are

    r_2=(1/2,-1), r_1=(1/2+A_*C,-1),
    r_0=(1/2+(A_*+1)C,-1),

with v_1=v_0=(C,0), and its outputs are P=(A_*+1)C,Q=0. Both local
sums equal A_*+1. For the source functions these are the physical
ordinary words(2A_*,2) and(2,2A_*). Add any source-eligible checkpoint
and tail: equations(12),(14)--(19) explicitly give their full static
functions, physical trajectories, and entire minimum sets. Formula(23)
continues to hold for EACH distinct output, using only the proved
inequality P+Q>=A_*+1. Neither the initial bound nor its scalar pruning
claims equality of these outputs.

For a zero local step(6) retains the current point and contributes
zero to P,Q. For a single arbitrarily huge local step alpha satisfying
alpha>=1+(1/kappa-epsilon)/C, (5) caps immediately, giving
P=C alpha,Q=0. Both(10) and(44) retain their indicated directions.
Thus the projection bounds handle the zero and huge-step endpoints
without a continuous-time replacement. No numerical evaluation of any
of these trajectories was made.

## 11. The original benchmark, read from accepted evidence

Only fact_id04219554e41d9750 supplies the following benchmark inputs.
For x,y>=0 let Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2 and
G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2. Let C_0=B_0=1 and, for n>=1,

    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

These are the ORIGINAL ALL-split recurrences. The accepted fact gives
the positive limit Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1), and for every j>=0

    lambda^(-j)B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
          <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2.          (48)

Its Section8 certifies every row of the public fixed interval table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
The header, row1024 and matching hash were read in this round. No
generator, recurrence, checker or mathematical evaluation was executed.
With D80=1208925819614629174706176 that accepted saved row gives

    D80 B_1024<=19229705732238461978760749948
              <15910 D80=19234009790068750169575260160.

Since sqrt(2)>140/99 and lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2)>6725 (the numerator
slack is82), and sqrt(15910)<127, (48) at j=10 gives

    Z_1<(15910+381/50+9/10000)/6725
         <15918/6725<237/100.                          (49)

These are written scalar deductions from existing accepted evidence.

For the physical hierarchy(24), v_h>1-9/(4m)>255/256 and
lambda^(-m)<1/256. Indeed r_h-beta<9/(4m) follows by expanding(24),
using tau<=1/4, lambda^2<6 and m>=2. Consequently

    (1+2S_m)/lambda^m
       =2v_h(1+tau/m)^m+2(1-v_h)(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m). (50)

Binomial truncation gives(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4 when m>=2.
At tau=1/4, (50) is strictly greater than
2(255/256)(81/64)-1/256=20623/8192>5/2. Uniformly for
1/5<=tau<=1/4 it is greater than
2(255/256)(121/100)-1/256=6161/2560>12/5. Thus

    B_(n_m)H_base(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,
    B_(n_m)H_base(H_(m,tau))<79/80, 1/5<=tau<=1/4.      (51)

For each fixed0<tau<=1/4, v_h tends1, (1+tau/m)^m tends exp(tau),
and(2/lambda)^m tends0. Combining(48),(50) proves

    lim_m B_(n_m)H_base(H_(m,tau))=Z_1/(2exp(tau)).      (52)

No parameter supremum is interchanged with a limit in this argument.

## 12. Complete scoped theorem and the remaining quantifiers

THEOREM (joint unverified analytical candidate).
Let the physical family be exactly(3), the source functions exactly
(12),(14)--(16), and the main functions exactly(40). For every real
0<tau<=1/4, integer m>=1536, every independent kappa>0 and0<epsilon<=1/2,
and EVERY nonempty chronological selection satisfying its source
threshold(2), at any count1<=k<=n_m,

    2F(x_(n_m))/dist(e_0,argmin F)^2
                         <(9801/10000)/(1+2S_m).       (53)

For every such m,tau, every pair additionally satisfying C>=c(c-2), and
EVERY nonempty main-eligible selection, also at any count,

    2f(x_(n_m))/dist(e_0,argmin f)^2
                         <(441/500)/(1+2S_m).          (54)

All minima are zero. Every physical trajectory, complete minimum set,
smoothness normalization and original interpolation row is justified
in this SAME joint proof. The parameter endpoints and all horizon-
dependent choices are included.

Define L_source,all to be the supremum of the ACTUAL closest-distance
payoffs of all source functions, over all their independent parameters
and eligible selections, including their k=0 member. Then the exact
source-only optimum is

    L_source,all(H_(m,tau))=1/[2(1+S_m)].               (55a)

Indeed M_m>=2^m>=32, from(24). The k=0 payoff divided by H_base equals
1-1/(2M_m)>=63/64>9801/10000, since630000>627264. Thus(53) puts every
nonempty source selection strictly below that actual k=0 member.

Define L_source,all^H=max{H_base,L_source,all}. Then

    L_source,all^H(H_(m,tau))=H_base(H_(m,tau)).          (55)

The same equality(55) holds after also adjoining every main function with
C>=c(c-2) and its k=0 member. The Huber member attains the maximum;
every source k=0 value1/[2(1+S_m)] is smaller, and(53),(54) bound all
nonempty selections. Both the source-only optimum(55a) and these
Huber-augmented family optima obey the finite benchmark deficits(51)
and the exact fixed-tau limit(52). For(55a), its ratio to H_base tends
one because M_m>=2^m tends infinity.

PROOF. Sections2--5 establish the actual source upper comparison(23),
with the full source minimum polyhedron retained. Sections6--8 prove
its all-count bound(37) uniformly over every pair, giving(53). Section9
proves the actual main upper comparison and its all-count band bound,
giving(54). The k=0 values and(51),(52) give(55) and the benchmark
conclusions. Every supporting calculation is included jointly, without
adopting an unverified intermediate result as an accepted fact.

The full main realization on C<c(c-2), with arbitrary checkpoint count,
is NOT bounded by this theorem. In particular, the supremum of BOTH
full actual families is still open. There is no normalized escape,
no upper bound on W_n(H_m), no assertion for tau>1/4, and no resolution
of the original all-word exact-profile minimax problem.

in7; X_sp in8; M_i^tr,sigma_i,D_i,ell_i,Phi_i,delta,J,f,C_env,zeta_i,

Omega,G,C_n,B_n,U_n^ZJ,Z_1,D80 in11; L_source,all,L_source,all^H in12. Section-local
symbols are explicitly scoped; no universal notation is redefined.

# Global polynomial cuts: a prefix obstruction that clock redistribution cannot repair

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max, invocation92, guidance008ca6d8bbfd18aa.
This is a complete joint analytical draft, not a verifier-accepted fact.
No formal call or fact_submit is authorized or made. The original minimax
problem remains OPEN. All parameters below use the SAME enclosing integer m.

## 1. Result and scope

For the full original free-pi law specified below, the two literal arrays

    Gamma_t,ij=(t_i-t_j)^2,
    Gamma_w,ij=(w_i-w_j)^2,  w=Ltri^(-1)e,

have nonnegative entries and zero diagonal. Both full weighted sums of N
are invariant under every global skew C with Ce=Ct=0 and every symmetric
clock-null detail Kdel with Kdel e=Kdel t=0. No support assumption is used.
The audited scalar formulas in TASK are correct, with the exact actual
clock, original alpha bracket, finite moments, and all rows retained.

The two signs are decisively different:

    T_t < -(S+1)^2/9                         for every m>=1536;
    T_w > 17q/5                             for every m>=2^26.

Thus the clock screen passes with the displayed slack. The prefix screen
excludes the fixed common-column/terminal-distribution architecture for
every m>=2^26, for ALL compatible global skews and ALL PSD clock-null
details, not merely terminal-six details. In fact its value is independent
of the entire metric K whenever Ke=0 and t^T Kt=Xreq, with the compatible
skew moments imposed. The proof of this stronger invariance is included.
The sign of T_w for 1536<=m<2^26 is not resolved here; the stated absolute
eventual threshold is sufficient for this scoped exclusion.

The one permitted second mechanism is the explicit non-null clock
redistribution in Section8. It preserves PSD, Ke=0, t^T Kt=Xreq, K00=3q,
the source charge, and the passing clock screen. Its first-two-source
and old endpoint screens also pass. Nevertheless its prefix screen is
identically the same positive number. Consequently no clock-only
redistribution with the fixed alpha and z can meet both global screens.
No neutral two-anchor lift is attempted after this invariant obstruction.
There is no third law.

This excludes a certificate architecture, not a primal schedule or the
unrestricted minimax claim. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 gives an exact complete
certificate framework, not necessity of this particular common column.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 retains its conditional scope: the desired full-W
upper bound for H_m is still unproved, and neither it nor the original
profile problem is decided by this report. Throughout W=2R.

## 2. Original objects, moments, and the full formal certificate

Let m>=1536 be an integer and set

    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(r+1)=(H_r,1+a*c^r,H_r), h=H_m,
    n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S_r=sum H_r, S=S_m, D=1+2S,
    q=17/(16D), pi=sqrt(q), b=1+a, g=1+beta.

Here h has n actual physical steps. Its queried indices are0,...,l;
the unqueried terminal sample is n. Let e be the all-ones column in R^n
and e_i the original coordinate column. Define

    (Ltri)_ii=1, (Ltri)_ij=h_j for j<i, otherwise0,
    t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(u<i)h_u,
    T=t_l=S-a, Delta=T-1=S-b, U=S+1.

Thus T is the actual last queried clock, never1/pi-b. In this report U is
a scalar, not the previous terminal-six detail matrix.

Here are guards used below, with elementary justifications:

    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 41/100<a<5/12,
    7/5<b<17/12, 199/100<g<2,
    S>n>2^20, S<c^m, pi<1/1000,
    17/(48S)<q<17/(32S), D/S<201/100.

For a>41/100, use sqrt(2)<99/70 and
(70/169)(6143/6145)>41/100. For a<5/12, use sqrt(2)>7/5.
The bounds on c,beta,b,g follow from their definitions. Put vword=a/(c-2).
Because c(c-2)>1>beta, 0<vword<1. Substitution in the concatenation yields

    1+S_r=(1-vword)2^r+vword c^r.

All steps exceed1, so S>n; the displayed identity gives S<c^m. The q
bounds follow from2S<D<3S, and S>2^20 yields pi<1/1000. These are finite
symbolic identities; no hierarchy or trajectory was computationally run.

Let pop(r) be the number of binary1 digits of a nonnegative integer r.
The product of h_i-1 over a prefix OR suffix of length r is

    R_r=beta^r c^(-pop(r)), 0<=r<=n.

Indeed the level at physical index i is the exponent of2 dividing i+1.
Counting multiples of2^k gives the sum of these levels over r consecutive
prefix positions as r-pop(r); palindrome reversal proves the suffix
version. Set

    s=R_n, k_i=h_i product_(u>i)(1-h_u),
    E=pi+s, F=pi-s, eta=F/E, V=(1-pi)/pi.

The odd length n gives k_i=(-1)^i|k_i| and
|k_i|=R_(l-i)+R_(l-i+1)<=2. Telescoping consecutive products proves

    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S.

For completeness, n>16m^2 for m>=16 (check m=16 and induct using
2^(m+1)-1=2(2^m-1)+1). Also beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>2, so

    s S^2 < beta^n c^m < (5/32)^m < 1/64.

In particular 0<s<pi, eta>0, and eta E^2=pi^2-s^2.

The terminal distribution uses its ACTUAL bracket. Put

    tbar=(S-V)/(1-pi),
    j=min{i: t_(i+1)>=tbar},
    alpha_j=(1-pi)(t_(j+1)-tbar)/h_j,
    alpha_(j+1)=(1-pi)(tbar-t_j)/h_j,
    alpha_i=0 otherwise, v=k-alpha.

The mean is inside the clock range. In fact the exact identity

    (1-pi)(T-tbar)=15/(32pi)-b+(a+1/2)pi

gives 1/(3pi)<T-tbar<1/(2pi) and tbar>S/2. The lower inequality follows
by comparison with(1-pi)/(3pi); the upper follows by comparison with
(1-pi)/(2pi), using pi<1/1000 and b<3/2. Thus alpha0=alpha1=0.
The mean is before the final H_4 block, whose mass is less c^4<40, and
after the first H_4 block. Its bracket cannot reach the final six queries.
This also follows directly from the sharper suffix bounds in Section5.
At a clock tie the earlier bracket coefficient is zero. In all cases

    alpha>=0, e^T alpha=1-pi, t^T alpha=S-V,
    e^T v=E, t^T v=V.

Define the unchanged common column and required metric energy by

    z=(E/a)(b e_0-e_1), Z=t^T z=-E/a, e^T z=E,
    X=Xreq=D/17-(pi^2-s^2)/a^2=D-pi^(-2)-eta Z^2.

Here 0<X<D/17<S/5. Positivity follows from a>2/5,
(pi^2-s^2)/a^2<425/(64D), and D>11.

For ANY symmetric PSD K with Ke=0 and t^T Kt=X, prescribe

    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T,
    dvec=F(v-z), rvec=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-2alpha,
    A^T=-A, Ae=dvec, At=rvec,
    N=Ltri^T(P+A).

These skew moments are compatible. Namely e^T dvec=0,
e^T rvec=-dvec^T t, and t^T rvec=0, using
2pi V=2(1-pi) and X+V^2+eta Z^2=2(S-V).
An explicit compatible choice is

    tmean=e^T t/n, cvec=t-tmean e, Ve=cvec^T cvec,
    fvec=rvec-tmean dvec+(dvec^T t)e/n,
    Acan=(dvec e^T-e dvec^T)/n
           +(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.

Indeed e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0; multiplication gives Acan e=dvec and
Acan t=rvec. Every other choice is Acan+C, C^T=-C, Ce=Ct=0.

The full original signed multipliers are

    lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
    lambda_(u,n)=0 for every prefix u,
    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for queried i!=j,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,
    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.

From (P+A)e=2pi v and (P-A)t=2alpha,

    mu=2pi Ltri^T v, nu=0,
    mu_i=2pi[h_i(pi+sum_(u<i)alpha_u)+(h_i-1)alpha_i]
          >=2q h_i>0.                                     (2.1)

All original off-diagonal N entries must be nonpositive; no pairs are
removed. The terminal and optimum signs already pass. The identity

    N_ij=P_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(P_uj+A_uj)                  (2.2)

always uses every original row, including indices before any anchor.

To link this algebra to the actual problem, normalize L=1 and put
x_star=g_star=f_star=0. In the orientation of accepted1252b8823bb5a290,
let Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. With the original formal gradients,

    y=e x_0-Ltri Gpre, x_n=-s x_0+k^T y,
    Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>.

The coordinate change is invertible. Telescoping the finite product gives
the x_n formula. Prefix flows cancel the function values. Since
Pe=Ev+Fz, Pe-Ae=2s v+2Fz, and q=s^2+eta E^2, direct expansion of the
outgoing terminal square from accepted1252 gives, on the FULL original
formal space,

    q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
      =||g_n+s x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y
           +eta||z^T y-E x_0||^2.                          (2.3)

This retains the initial charge once and the free terminal gradient.
Its PSD property does not supply the missing nonnegative finite signs.

## 3. Physical clock metric and its genuine finite corrections

Put Qmass=sum h_i^2, Rmass=sum h_i^3 and

    tmass=(sum h_i t_i)/S,
    Vmass=sum h_i(t_i-tmass)^2,
    rho_clock=X/Vmass,
    Kclock=rho_clock[diag(h)-hh^T/S], yclock=Kclock t.

These quantities refer to the nonuniform actual word. If p_i=t_i-1,
palindrome reversal sends p_i to S-p_i-h_i. Consequently

    sum h_i p_i=(S^2-Qmass)/2,
    sum h_i^2 p_i=(S Qmass-Rmass)/2.

Telescoping (p_i+h_i)^3-p_i^3 and substituting the second identity gives
sum h_i p_i^2=S^3/3-S Qmass/2+Rmass/6. Therefore

    tmass=1+(S^2-Qmass)/(2S),
    Vmass=S^3/12+Rmass/6-Qmass^2/(4S),
    M2:=sum h_i t_i^2
       =S+S^2-Qmass+S^3/3-S Qmass/2+Rmass/6.           (3.1)

No uniform-mesh replacement has been made. The exact level counts are
sum h_i^d=sum_(r=0)^(m-1)2^(m-1-r)(1+a c^r)^d for d=1,2,3.
In particular Qmass=2S-n+a^2(c^(2m)-2^m)/(c^2-2).
Since vword>4/5 and vword c^m>100,
S>(99/100)a c^m/(c-2). Hence

    Qmass/S^2 < 2/S+(100/99)^2(c-2)^2/(c^2-2) < 1/15.

To check the last bound, (c-2)^2/(c^2-2) is increasing for2<c<5/2
and is less1/17, while (100/99)^2/17<1/16 and2/S<1/500.
Equation(3.1) then proves

    Vmass>(2/25)S^3, 0<rho_clock<125/S^2,
    yclock_i=rho_clock h_i(t_i-tmass),
    Kclock>=0, Kclock e=0, t^T Kclock t=X.            (3.2)

The PSD statement is weighted Cauchy--Schwarz. A useful exact telescoping
upper bound, retaining the sign of every finite correction, is

    (U^3-1)/3-M2=sum_i[h_i^2 t_i+h_i^3/3]>0.         (3.3)

This is the algebraic identity obtained from
(t_i+h_i)^3-t_i^3. Its use below is an inequality with explicit discarded
positive terms, not an integral approximation.

The default source-normalized details are, for0<=i<=n-3,

    d_i=h_(i+1)e_i-(h_i+h_(i+1))e_(i+1)+h_i e_(i+2),
    omega_0=(3q-(Kclock)00)/g^2,
    omega_i=8(q+v_i^2+v_(i+1)^2+v_(i+2)^2)/(h_i+h_(i+1))^2 (i>=1),
    Korig=Kclock+sum_i omega_i d_i d_i^T.

Each detail annihilates e and t by direct substitution of consecutive
clock increments. Also (Kclock)00<250/S^2<1/S<3q, so all coefficients
are positive. Thus Korig>=0, Korig e=0, Korig t=yclock, Korig00=3q.
The first mechanism allows the much larger class Kclock+Kdel with any
PSD Kdel annihilating e,t; it does not depend on this particular detail
sum or on a terminal-six parameter R.

## 4. The two FULL-array invariants and all factors

For a real original column u, let u^2 mean coordinatewise square and
Gamma_u,ij=(u_i-u_j)^2. From Ne=mu and N^T e=2alpha,

    <Gamma_u,N>=(u^2)^T mu+2alpha^T(u^2)
                   -2(Ltri u)^T(P+A)u.                (4.1)

This is a literal sum over every i,j, including both orientations.
For u=t, (P+A)t=2(Kt+Vv+eta Zz-alpha). Therefore

    T_t=(t^2)^T mu+2alpha^T(t^2)
          -4(Ltri t)^T(Kt+Vv+eta Zz-alpha).           (4.2)

For the prefix column, set w_0=1 and
w_i=product_(u<i)(1-h_u). The actual unit-curvature scalar quadratic
recursion x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i with x_0=1 gives precisely this column.
Equivalently w_(i+1)-w_i=-h_i w_i; finite telescoping gives
w_i+sum_(u<i)h_u w_u=1. Thus Ltri w=e, without a suffix substitution.
Since e^T(P+A)=(Pe-Ae)^T=2s v^T+2Fz^T,

    T_w=(w^2)^T mu+2alpha^T(w^2)-4s v^T w-4F z^T w. (4.3)

For clarity, the global neutral cancellation can also be checked before
using prescribed moments. A perturbation DeltaN=Ltri^T C with
C^T=-C, Ce=Ct=0 has DeltaN e=0 and DeltaN^T e=-Ct=0.
Formula(4.1) leaves -2(Ltri u)^T C u. It vanishes at u=t because Ct=0,
and at u=w because Ltri w=e and e^T C=0. Hence BOTH
<Gamma_u,Ltri^T C>=0 for every such global C.

Similarly, for symmetric Kdel with Kdel e=Kdel t=0, both the row and
column sums of Ltri^T Kdel vanish. Its remaining contribution is
-2(Ltri u)^T Kdel u, zero for u=t by Kdel t=0 and for u=w by
Ltri w=e and Kdel e=0. No locality enters either argument. PSD of
Kdel is not needed for cancellation, only for the certificate class.

Finally (4.3), together with(2.1), contains NO K. Consequently it remains
unchanged even if Kt changes, provided Ke=0, t^T Kt=X, and A is changed
to obey the prescribed compatible moments. This is stronger than
clock-null-detail invariance. Any globally nonnegative multiplier array
must satisfy T_t<=0 and T_w<=0, since both Gamma arrays are nonnegative
and zero on the diagonal. Symmetry of Ltri Gamma is not asserted or used.

## 5. Exact alpha moments and uniform NEGATIVITY of the clock cut

Let ell=Ltri t. It is strictly increasing because
ell_(i+1)-ell_i=h_i(1+t_i)>0. For K=Kclock+Kdel,

    ell^T Kt=ell^T Kclock t
      =rho_clock/(2S) sum_(i,j)h_i h_j(ell_i-ell_j)(t_i-t_j)>0. (5.1)

Also ell_0=1, ell_1=1+2b, so
z^T ell=-E(1+b)/a and eta Z z^T ell=eta E^2(1+b)/a^2>0.
These signs use the actual first two original coordinates.

Here is a finite expansion which retains alpha's second moments. Define

    drem_i=U-t_i=sum_(u>=i)h_u,
    Qtail_i=sum_(u>=i)h_u^2,
    Etail_i=sum_(u>=i)h_u drem_u^2,
    D_r=sum_i alpha_i drem_i^r (r=1,2,3),
    Qalpha=sum_i alpha_i Qtail_i, Ealpha=sum_i alpha_i Etail_i.

The symbol drem differs from the detail vectors d_i and the skew dvec.
The exact first moment is

    D_1=1/pi-pi U, pi^2 U=17/32+q/2.                (5.2)

On the alpha support, drem_i<2/pi. To prove this without assuming a
uniform mesh, the mean suffix mass is

    dbar=D_1/(1-pi)=(15/32-q/2)/(pi(1-pi))<1/(2pi).

The last inequality follows from1/32-pi/2+q/2>0. The bracket gives
drem_(j+1)<=dbar<=drem_j. If h_j=1+a c^r, the physical suffix after
j contains H_r. From S_r>=vword c^r-1 one obtains

    h_j< (1/2)drem_(j+1)+3/2,
    drem_j<(3/2)dbar+3/2<2/pi.                     (5.3)

The same estimates, with dbar>1/(3pi), place drem_(j+1) above200, so
the alpha support cannot reach the final six (whose total step mass
is less12). This supplies a direct support-separation check including ties.

All the following are exact finite identities. Put M1=sum h_i t_i.
The descending suffix masses satisfy
sum_(u>=i)h_u drem_u=(drem_i^2+Qtail_i)/2. Thus

    ell_i=M1+U-(U+1)drem_i+(drem_i^2+Qtail_i)/2,
    sum_(u>=i)h_u t_u^2
       =U^2 drem_i-U(drem_i^2+Qtail_i)+Etail_i.     (5.4)

Using Ltri^T k=h in(4.2), and then inserting(5.2)--(5.4), gives

    T_t=2q M2+C_U
        +2[(1-pi)+D_1]D_2+2D_1 Qalpha+2pi Ealpha
        -4ell^T Kclock t-4eta Z z^T ell,           (5.5)
    C_U=(8-2q)U^2+4U-2q U^3-4(U+1)/q.

For an explicit check of the cancellation, before collecting terms the
nonmetric part is

    2q M2+2(1-pi)alpha^T(t^2)
      +2pi sum_i alpha_i sum_(u>=i)h_u t_u^2
      -4V M1+(4/pi)alpha^T ell.

Insert alpha^T(t^2)=(1-pi)U^2-2U D_1+D_2 and(5.4).
The M1 terms cancel exactly, since V=(1-pi)/pi. Substitution of
D_1=1/pi-pi U leaves exactly C_U and the displayed D_2,Qalpha,Ealpha
terms. Hence no alpha variance or nonuniform physical correction has
been deleted from the identity.

Now evaluate the sign with finite inequalities. Put r=qU; then
1/2<r<8/15, and

    C_U/U^2=8-2r-4/r-2q+(4-4/r)/U < -17/30.

Indeed 8-2r-4/r is increasing for1/2<r<8/15 and its value at8/15
is -17/30. The remaining terms are negative because r<1.
By the exact positive correction identity(3.3),

    2q M2 < (2r/3)U^2 < (16/45)U^2.

Furthermore0<D_1<1/pi, Qalpha<=D_2<4/q, and
Ealpha<=D_3<8/pi^3, from(5.3). Therefore the entire positive remainder
involving D_2,Qalpha,Ealpha is less

    24/q+16/pi^3 < U^2/10.

For the last strict inequality, U>1/(2q), so its ratio to U^2 is less
96q+64pi<1/10. Drop only the two nonpositive terms justified in(5.1)
and after it. Equation(5.5) proves

    T_t < [-17/30+16/45+1/10]U^2=-U^2/9.           (5.6)

This holds for every m>=1536 and every allowed clock-null detail and
compatible global skew. It also proves that the NONMETRIC part of(4.2)
is less -U^2/9, a fact used jointly in the next mechanism.

## 6. Exact prefix identities and uniform POSITIVITY of the prefix cut

The product formula in Section2 gives the actual prefix column

    w_i=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-pop(i)), |w_i|<=1,
    w_0=1, w_1=-a, w_2=a beta.

This is not k, whose products run from i+1 to the end. The full finite
product also gives the useful check

    k_i w_i=s h_i/(h_i-1),
    v^T w=s sum_i h_i/(h_i-1)-alpha^T w.            (6.1)

In particular the true alpha bracket has not been silently replaced.
Its square contribution in(4.3) is nonnegative and retained until the
lower bound. The common-column contribution is fixed by the first TWO
source coordinates:

    z^T w=(E/a)(b+a)=E(1+2a)/a,
    4F z^T w=4(q-s^2)(1/a+2)<18q.                (6.2)

The negative sign of w_1 is essential to this expression.

Let J_r be the prefix energy sum h_i w_i^2 restricted to the initial
H_r block, always with the same enclosing m parameters, and with w_0=1.
Then J_0=0,J_1=b. The exact finite concatenation identity is

    J_(r+1)=[1+a^2 beta^(2(2^r-1))]J_r
       +(1+a c^r) beta^(2(2^r-1)) c^(-2r).        (6.3)

To derive it, the incoming squared prefix amplitude at the middle step
is beta^(2(2^r-1))c^(-2r). After this step it is multiplied by
a^2 c^(2r), so the right H_r block has incoming amplitude square
a^2 beta^(2(2^r-1)). Add the left block, middle contribution, and right
block. This is a symbolic identity, not an executed recurrence.

For every m>=2^26 and every1<=r<=24, Bernoulli's inequality gives

    beta^(2(2^r-1)) >= 1-(2^r-1)/(2m)>7/8.

Using a>2/5 in(6.3), its coefficient of J_r is greater57/50>9/8.
The middle contribution is positive. Consequently

    sum_(i<n)h_i w_i^2 >=J_25 > b(9/8)^24 >56/5.   (6.4)

The last inequality uses b>7/5 and
(9/8)^8>1+8/8=2, hence(9/8)^24>8. No large sum is left with an
unevaluated sign, and no H_r was numerically generated.

Finally |v^T w|<=2n+1<3S by |k_i|<=2, |w_i|<=1 and alpha's mass.
The finite suffix bound sS^2<1/64 gives

    4s|v^T w|<3/(16S)<q.                          (6.5)

Combining(2.1), (4.3), and(6.2)--(6.5), with alpha>=0, proves

    T_w >=2q sum h_i w_i^2-4s|v^T w|-4F z^T w
         > (112/5-1-18)q=17q/5>0.               (6.6)

This is a sum of all original entries against a literal nonnegative
zero-diagonal cost, invariant under every globally allowed skew and
every clock-null detail. Therefore at least one original off-diagonal
N_ij is positive for every such completion at every m>=2^26.
No claim of an original primal witness follows.

## 7. First-two-source coordinates and original endpoint capacities

These mandatory checks are for Korig and the explicit redistribution
Khat in Section8, not an assertion about arbitrary detail diagonals.
Both have K00=3q and unchanged v,z,alpha. Finite product identities give

    v0=bs/a, v1=-gs/(a beta), z0=Eb/a, z1=-E/a,
    mu0=2bq, mu1=2gq, alpha0=alpha1=0,
    P00=3q+b^2q/a^2,
    P01=K01-bq/a^2-bs^2/(a^2 beta).               (7.1)

The first two rows of Ltri^T are b e^T-a e0^T and
g e^T-g e0^T-beta e1^T. Since e^T(P+A)=2s v^T+2Fz^T, they give

    N00=(b^2/a-3a)q=(1/a+2-2a)q,
    N01=-(2b/a)(q+s^2/beta)-a(P01+A01),
    N10=2gbq/a-gP00-beta(P01-A01),
    beta N01+a N10
       =-gb(1-a)q/a-a g K00-2a beta K01.          (7.2)

These are exact original-coordinate identities for every compatible A.
In particular both source coordinates and the finite s^2 corrections
are present; the latter cancel in the last line rather than being ignored.

Let theta=1 for Korig and theta=1/2 for Khat. Their source clock block
is theta Kclock, and their only source-reaching detail has coefficient
(3q-theta(Kclock)00)/g^2. Thus

    K01=-theta rho_clock bg/S
            -(b+g)(3q-theta(Kclock)00)/g.

Substitution in(7.2) yields a q coefficient

    -gb(1-a)/a-3ag+6a beta(b+g)/g,

plus the clock correction

    2a beta theta rho_clock b
       [-(b+g)/g+{g+b(b+g)/g}/S] <0.

The bracket is negative already when S>b+g. For the q coefficient,
g(1/a-a)>39/10, 3ag>23/10, and
6a beta(b+g)/g<35/8, using b/g<3/4. Therefore

    beta N01+a N10 < -q.                          (7.3)

This weighted pair test passes; it is not a simultaneous sign assertion
for the two entries or the whole array.

Write y=Kt. The source row sum and clock capacity are

    r0=N00-mu0=(1/a-4a)q>11q/15,
    B0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)lambda_(0,j)=2a y0+2bq.    (7.4)

For the last identity, (Nt)0=-2a y0 follows from the same first row of
Ltri^T and the prescribed moments; then B0=mu0-(Nt)0. For Korig,
y0=yclock0<0, and(3.2) gives

    |yclock0|/(bq)<100(D/S)^2/289<7/5.

Here X<D/17, tmass-1<S/2, Vmass>2S^3/25 and q=17/(16D)
give the first strict bound. The function2-1/a+4a is increasing and
exceeds6/5 at a>41/100. Since2a(7/5)<7/6, (7.4) implies

    B0-b r0>bq/30,  B0<2bq<Delta r0.              (7.5)

For the upper comparison, Delta>10 and r0>11q/15 suffice.
For Khat, y0=yclock0/2, so B0 only increases toward2bq; both tests
in(7.5) still pass. The source column capacity N00 is positive.

For the actual last queried coordinate, alpha_l=z_l=0,v_l=b. Put
kappa=Kclock_ll and tau=omega_(l-2). The last detail is the only one
reaching l, and

    tau=8(q+b^2a^2 beta^2+g^2a^2+b^2)/(b+g)^2<4.

Indeed a^2<1/5,b^2<3,g^2<4,q<1/10 and b+g>3 give that strict bound.
For both metrics write kappa_new for the diagonal of their clock part.
Then0<kappa_new<1 and

    Dlast=Nll=kappa_new+b^2(1+tau),
    rlast=Dlast-2pi b,        1<rlast<16,
    Bl=(Nt)l-T mu_l=2y_l+2b(V-pi T),
    V-pi T=15/(32pi)-1+a pi.                       (7.6)

For Korig,0<2y_l<1 follows from rho_clock<125/S^2 and
T-tmass<S. For Khat the same bound is checked in Section8.
Thus

    g rlast<Bl<Delta rlast.                        (7.7)

Explicitly Bl>2b/(3pi)>32>g rlast. On the other side
Bl<1+3/pi<1/(4q)<S/2<Delta rlast; the penultimate comparison uses
S=17/(32q)-1/2 and pi<1/1000. Last column capacity Dlast>0 also passes.
These endpoint screens coexist with the global obstruction(6.6).
Neither their success nor(7.3) implies the individual interior signs.

## 8. Mechanism2: one explicit non-null clock redistribution, and its failure

For ANY allowed K, the exact clock constraint is

    ell^T Kt >= M_t/4,
    M_t=(t^2)^T mu+2alpha^T(t^2)
                 -4ell^T(Vv+eta Zz-alpha).        (8.1)

The nonmetric expansion in Section5 proves M_t<-U^2/9. Thus this
particular clock inequality is not what excludes the original metric.
The prefix constraint, independently of Kt, is exactly

    4F z^T w >= (w^2)^T mu+2alpha^T(w^2)-4s v^T w. (8.2)

For the frozen common column, (6.4)--(6.5) force the right side to exceed
(107/5)q, whereas the left side is less18q. Equivalently a necessary
projection bound would be

    1/a+2 >= [(w^2)^T mu+2alpha^T(w^2)-4s v^T w]/[4(q-s^2)]
             >107/20,

but1/a+2<9/2. Hence no choice of Kt can satisfy(8.2) with these fixed
alpha,z,pi. This states the exact missing constraint rather than trying
to change an invariant with neutral skew.

The sole tested redistribution is literal and has no unknown matrices.
Let p=2^(m-1)-1 be the original root query, rroot=e_l-e_p,
Delta_root=T-t_p. Then t_p=1+S_(m-1) and

    Delta_root=S_(m-1)+h_p-b>S/2,

because S=2S_(m-1)+h_p and h_p>2b. Define

    Khatclock=Kclock/2+X rroot rroot^T/(2 Delta_root^2),
    omegahat0=(3q-(Kclock)00/2)/g^2,
    Khat=Khatclock+omegahat0 d0 d0^T
                          +sum_(i=1)^(n-3)omega_i d_i d_i^T. (8.3)

Every coefficient is positive. Direct multiplication gives

    Khat>=0, Khat e=0, Khat00=3q, t^T Khat t=X,
    Khat t=yclock/2+X rroot/(2 Delta_root).          (8.4)

In particular this is a non-null clock-metric change: its coordinate0
is yclock0/2, differing from yclock0. The source charge and every term
in the original square identity(2.3) remain accounted for. A is given
literally by the canonical formula in Section2 with this new rvec; all
compatible additional global C are still permitted.

The clock pairing is positive:

    ell^T Khat t=(ell^T yclock)/2
                      +X(ell_l-ell_p)/(2 Delta_root)>0.

Therefore Section5's SAME nonmetric bound gives
T_t(Khat)<-U^2/9. Its source block is theta=1/2 as used in Section7.
Also Khatclock_ll=kappa/2+X/(2 Delta_root^2)<1, and
0<yhat_l<yclock_l/2+1/5<1/2, justifying the endpoint bounds there.
Thus PSD, charge, both original endpoint capacities, the weighted first
source pair, and the global clock screen have all been checked for this
single redistribution.

But T_w(Khat)=T_w(Korig)>17q/5 for every m>=2^26, by(4.3).
The exact original obstruction is the positive full weighted sum
sum_(i!=j)(w_i-w_j)^2 N_ij; it is not an unchecked entry of a local block.
There can be no all-sign completion of(8.3), even by arbitrary global
neutral skew. The requirement of preserving BOTH global cuts is therefore
mathematically impossible for any clock-only redistribution with the
frozen terminal and common columns. This failed second test ends the
assigned round; it does not authorize a new common column or a third law.

## 9. Joint audit, retrieval, limitations, and handoff

The source draft0a257ce254beff8c was recalled as an UNVERIFIED joint
candidate, not cited as a fact premise. The algebra needed here has been
derived again in full. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used only for the
original certificate orientation/terminal square; accepted931b9653ce0f7201
only identifies the conditional benchmark goal and its unresolved scope.

The query-memory and obtain-immediate-conclusions procedures informed
recall and the two invariant calculations. A targeted search-math-results
query in mature-subproblem/theorem-level-blocker mode was scoped to this
certificate architecture, not the minimax problem. Matlas returned generic
bilinear/scaling results with different hypotheses. Web search supplied
PEP/interpolation context. No external result was imported as a premise.
The branch reduced to direct finite array identities and elementary
product inequalities, so no extra paper theory was needed for its closure.

Author audit: all indices are original; T=S-a; the prefix w and suffix k
are separate; both source coordinates enter(6.2),(7.1)--(7.3); alpha's
actual bracket and second moment are retained in(5.5); finite unequal-step
corrections are explicit in(3.1),(3.3),(5.4); all skew and metric cancellation
identities are global; the strengthened K-invariance of T_w includes the
changed compatible At; the only new metric is(8.3); a passed source or
endpoint screen is never called a full sign certificate.

The absolute eventual threshold2^26 is deliberately coarse and proved
without any numerical or recurrence replay. No sign assertion is made
for the prefix cut below it. No primitive or dominant benchmark DP was
modified or executed. No original primal adversary or complete minimax
resolution has been constructed. The new mathematical claims have no fact
IDs and remain JOINT UNVERIFIED because TASK permits zero formal calls.

Resources: at most two substantive mechanisms; mathematical programs0;
numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replay0; solvers/optimizers0;
benchmark DP0; formal calls/fact_submit0; installations0; consults0;
subagents0. One Matlas query and one web call were textual background only.
No lifecycle action, TASK overwrite, outward publication, finalization,
revocation, shutdown, or Goal completion occurred. Return at cap1; no
previous credit or automatic renewal is claimed.

Next interface for the main agent: the fixed source common column has too
small a projection on the exact quadratic prefix. Clock redistribution
and global skew cannot change this. A future, separately authorized
attempt would need to change a nonmetric part of the architecture or use
a different certificate family. This report proposes no third candidate.

For tail capacity D and q=sqrt(1-theta)>0, choose an integer unit-prefix capacity A>=max{1,D(1-q)/(q(1+q))}. The positive saturated root T of T²-(A+D)T-AD=0 then satisfies T>=D/q, so the parent demands theta_0=1-(D/T)²>=theta. A supplied E_theta certificate also certifies E_theta_0 after adding (theta_0-theta)b_i to every actual incoming-terminal coefficient. The full saturated identity in the self-contained candidate2d75b4696641736c (notation correctiona83051c5f43a2a0c) supplies the parent certificate and the forced prefix restriction; these are candidate mechanisms, not accepted premises. The genuinely missing theorem is reversal of an arbitrary actual full intrinsic certificate with all signs and PSD retained. Two direct screens did not prove it: literal trajectory reversal uses the gradient at the wrong endpoint in the GD equation, and the published GSW induction requires basic composition. The newer inverse-certificate congruence has an unresolved sign and performance-setup matching problem. Do not declare the plan solved or infer reversal from equal capacity/quadratic product.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001355 -->
# Full Silver calibration and whole-array continuation tests

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft. No fact_submit or numerical optimization.
This does not establish the original exact profile converse or a separation.
Every mathematical assertion below is proved in this draft; no unverified
shared finding is used as a correctness premise. The accepted interpolation
and full-certificate framework is fact `1252b8823bb5a290`.

The source starting point is Altschuler--Parrilo, *Acceleration by Stepsize
Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization*,
arXiv:2309.16530 (2023), equations (3.1)--(3.9), Theorem 3.4 and its proof.
Its C>=0 convention is C_ij=-2Q_ji in the present notation. The exact local
PDF and extracted text were read. We reconstruct the needed identities,
including the complete objective and remainder, instead of importing a
distance-only statement. The generic skew-completion idea has an earlier
intrinsic-potential analogue in shared candidate666b5b9b14038a0f; the new
tests here concern actual full objective certificates and the assigned path.

## 1. Exact coefficients at every depth

Set rho=1+sqrt(2), n_j=2^j-1, and define the undeformed word by

 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+rho^(j-1),H_j).

Its mass is M_j=1+sum H_j=rho^j, since M_(j+1)=2M_j+rho^(j-1).
Normalize smoothness to1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All formal vectors
x_0,g_0,...,g_n remain independent. Put

 x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 C_ij=-2Q_ji=2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2.

Here is one finite-node array L^(j), without optimum entries. At depth0 it
is zero. In a join let p=n_j, q=2n_j+1, M=rho^j, d=M/rho, and b=1+d.
Embed L^(j) on the left and rho^2 L^(j) on the right. Let right weights
w_i=h_i for p<i<q and w_q=1. For every p<i<=q add rho w_i to L_(p,i)
and to L_(q,i), omitting the diagonal entry L_(q,q). Add M to L_(q,p).
All coefficients are explicitly nonnegative. Repeated contributions add.

Define

 J_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))
                       +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.

The full finite identity is

 J_j=sum_(i!=k)L^(j)_ik C_ik+M_j(M_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2.       (1)

Here is an induction with every nontrivial cancellation displayed. It is
zero=zero at j=0. In a join write g=g_p, e=g_q,
P=sum_(p<i<q)h_i g_i, W=P+e, and S=M-1. The quadratic parts of the two
weighted fans, before multiplication by rho, are respectively

 sum_i w_i C_(p,i): -M||g||^2-2d<g,W>-||W||^2
                                      +sum_right h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2,
 sum_i w_i C_(q,i):  ||W||^2-M||e||^2
                                      +sum_right h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2.

The quadratic part of C_(q,p) is
(1+2d)||g||^2-||e||^2+2<g,W>.
These formulas follow by expanding the definitions and using
2sum_i<h_i g_i,sum_(p<t<i)h_t g_t>=||P||^2-sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.
Thus the added fans and M C_(q,p) have zero g,W cross coefficient
because M=rho d. Their g and e diagonal coefficients are
M(1+2d-rho) and -(rho+1)M. Their interior diagonal coefficients are
2rho h_i(h_i-1), exactly the difference between the parent and
rho^2 times the right child. Their value coefficients are

 f_p: 2M(rho-1)=2(S+b),
 f_q: 2rho(M-2)+2M=2[(rho^2-2)S-b],
 f_i, p<i<q: -4rho h_i=2(1-rho^2)h_i.

They therefore equal all value coefficients of J_(j+1)-J_left-rho^2 J_right.
After using both child identities, the g diagonal left over is

 M(M-1)-d(1+d)-M(1+2d-rho)=0,

and the e diagonal is
rho^2 M(M-1)+(rho+1)M=(rho M)(rho M-1).
This proves (1) at every depth.

Write M=M_m, S=M-1, v=2M-1. A sharp full objective array in the current
Q convention is

 Lambda_ik=2L^(m)_ki/v for finite i!=k,
 Lambda_(i,star)=2h_i/v for i<n,
 Lambda_(n,star)=2M/v,
 Lambda_(star,i)=0 for all finite i.

Its COMPLETE certificate, not just a primitive consequence, is

 (1/v)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum Lambda_ik Q_ik
                         =||x_n-Mg_n||^2/v.                 (2)

To check this multiply by v and use (1). The query optimum entries give
2sum h_i f_i-2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>+sum h_i||g_i||^2. The terminal optimum
entry gives2M f_n-2M<g_n,x_n>+M||g_n||^2. The finite array gives
-J_m+M(M-1)||g_n||^2. All values cancel because v=M+S. The remaining
query terms telescope by
||x_0||^2-2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>+sum h_i^2||g_i||^2=||x_n||^2.
The terminal terms complete the displayed square. This verifies every
original formal-space coefficient. The scalar Huber function with slope
1/v, quadratic core radius1/v, and x_0=1 attains2f_n=1/v: its iterates
are x_i=1-(sum_(t<i)h_t)/v>=M/v>1/v. Thus (2) is tight.

The actual canonical AP objective certificate is different. Define

 t=(1+sqrt(4M^2-3))/2, so t^2-t+1=M^2.

In the C convention its finite coefficients are the SAME L^(m), and its
optimum entries are C_(star,i):h_i for i<n, C_(star,n):t,
C_(n,star):S, with the other C_(i,star) coefficients zero. In Q notation,

 Lambda_ik=2L^(m)_ki/t,
 Lambda_(i,star)=2h_i/t (i<n), Lambda_(n,star)=2,
 Lambda_(star,n)=2S/t, Lambda_(star,i)=0 (i<n).

All are nonnegative, and the exact full identity is

 (1/t)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum Lambda_ik Q_ik
                         =||x_n-tg_n||^2/t.                 (3)

Indeed (1) and the same norm telescope give the C sum
||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2+2t<x_n,g_n>-2t f_n
                         -(M^2+t-1)||g_n||^2.
Since M^2+t-1=t^2 this is the required identity. The source paper uses
f_n<=r_m||x_0||^2 with r_m=1/(2t); our normalized objective coefficient
is 2r_m=1/t. No factor2 is suppressed.

Both (2) and (3) have rank-one complete remainder. Their kernels are,
respectively, x_0=sum h_i g_i+M g_n and x_0=sum h_i g_i+t g_n. The
kernel dimension is n+1 in scalar formal coordinates. These are actual
all-depth baseline arrays, with different objective constants and optimum
incidence; they must not be identified with one another.

## 2. Full coordinates and exact singular conditions

Let N=n+1, e the vector of N ones, e_n the last coordinate, and
V=I+T with T_it=h_t for t<i. For an arbitrary real N by N array A put

 b=Ae, a=A^T e-2e_n,
 G=sym(AV)-e_n e_n^T, sym(D)=(D+D^T)/2.

The actual multipliers are Lambda_ij=-A_ij (i!=j), Lambda_(i,star)=b_i,
Lambda_(star,i)=a_i. All actual signs and flows hold precisely when

 A_ij<=0 (i!=j), b>=0, a>=0.                              (4)

Expanding Q, or first canceling its value coefficients by these flows,
gives the complete matrix

 Mcert(q,A,h)=[[q,-b^T/2],[-b/2,G]].                       (5)

At either baseline put w0=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),tau), where tau=M or t,
and q0=1/v or1/t, as appropriate. Make the invertible fixed-coordinate
substitution x_0=z+w0^T g. At any nearby parameters the matrix becomes

 [[q,r^T],[r,D]],
 r=q w0-b/2,
 D=G+q w0w0^T-(w0b^T+bw0^T)/2.

For q>0 its PSD condition is EXACTLY

                    D-r r^T/q >=0.                     (6)

At the baseline r=D=0. If q,A,h are analytic in s, write
r=s r1+s^2 r2+..., D=s D1+s^2 D2+.... Then D1>=0 is necessary, but
on ker D1 one also needs D2-r1 r1^T/q0>=0. At any subsequent singular
block [[B,C],[C^T,E]], the exact conditions are B>=0,
range(C) contained in range(B), and E-C^T B^dagger C>=0. Here B^dagger
inverts B on its range and is zero on its kernel. To see the range
condition, a vector in ker B must have zero mixed product with every
other vector under a PSD form; completing squares on range B then gives
the last condition. Thus there is no license to discard mixed blocks.

At an initially zero A_ij, a one-sided feasible derivative requires
dot A_ij<=0; at an initially zero component of a or b, the corresponding
derivative must be nonnegative. Positive coefficients allow signed
variations only while their FINAL values satisfy (4).

## 3. An explicit dense rank-one transport, with no unknown PSD

Here we attempt continuation from the sharp baseline (2). The chosen
physical path is exactly the assignment, with epsilon=s/m:

 c=rho(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
 H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), j<m.

Let h be that word, M=1+sum h, v=2M-1, q=1/v, and
p=V^(-1)e. Thus p_0=1 and p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i.
In a neighborhood of epsilon=0 define

 mu=1-sqrt(1-v p_n^2),
 wphys=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),M),
 delta=(mu-wphys^T p)/h_0,
 w=wphys+delta(e_0-e_1).                                (7)

This is analytic at zero, since there 1-v p_n^2=(M-1)^2/M^2>0,
and delta(0)=0. It satisfies e^T w=v and w^T p=mu. All w_i are
positive sufficiently near zero. For n=1 the second coordinate is the
terminal; the formula still applies.

Put S0=e_n e_n^T+q w w^T and define

 U=[e,p], H=U^T U,
 R=[e_n+w-2V^T e_n, q(2-mu)w-p_n e_n],
 P=U H^(-1)U^T,
 Kpart=R H^(-1)U^T-U H^(-1)R^T
                             -U H^(-1)(U^T R)H^(-1)U^T.

The two columns of U are independent because h_0>0. Directly,
U^T R is skew: its first diagonal uses sum w=v=1+2sum h, the second
uses q mu(2-mu)=p_n^2, and its mixed entries sum to zero using Vp=e.
It follows by multiplication that Kpart is skew and Kpart U=R.

At the undeformed baseline let A0 be the exact array from (2), V0 its
V, and K0=(A0 V0-V0^T A0^T)/2. Define the ACTUAL proposed full array

 K=Kpart+(I-P)K0(I-P),  A=(S0+K)V^(-1).                  (8)

There is no support deletion in (8); it generally creates dense interior
entries. It preserves A(0)=A0. Indeed every skew matrix with KU=R has
the form Kpart+(I-P)Omega(I-P), where Omega is arbitrary skew, because
the difference annihilates U on both sides. Taking Omega=K0 recovers
K0 at zero by this decomposition. Also

 Ae=2q w, A^T e=2e_n, sym(AV)=e_n e_n^T+q w w^T.

Consequently the FULL matrix (5) is identically
q[1;-w][1;-w]^T. All nullspace, mixed-block and range requirements hold
exactly. Optimum signs hold locally because w>0 and a=0. The remaining
indispensable condition is A_ij<=0 for EVERY distinct pair. It is not
implied by the identities just proved.

## 4. Exact sign failure, including all free skew repairs at three steps

For m=2 write r=sqrt(2). At epsilon=0, h=(r,2,r), M=3+2r,
v=5+4r. The baseline A0, with all entries divided by7, is

 [[6-2r, 10-8r, 0, 0],
  [-6+2r, -8+12r, 0, -6+2r],
  [0, 4-6r, 10+6r, 2-10r],
  [0, -6+2r, -10-6r, 18+8r]].

The physical derivative with respect to epsilon is
dot h=(-2/rho,-1,-2/rho). Formula (7) gives dot delta=1-4r.
Differentiating the EXPLICIT formula (8), using only product and inverse
rules, gives

 dot A_02=(-302+467r)/98,
 dot A_30=(-187+221r)/49.                               (9)

For reproducibility K0, with all entries divided by7, is

 [[0,-6+2r,10-8r,-4-r],
  [6-2r,0,-12+4r,-8-2r],
  [-10+8r,12-4r,0,-2-11r],
  [4+r,8+2r,2+11r,0]].

The values (9) can thus be checked by substituting into (7)--(8),
with d(X^(-1))=-X^(-1)(dX)X^(-1). The first value is positive, since
sqrt(2)>1 and467>302. Because A0_02=0, formula (8) makes the actual
multiplier Lambda_02=-A_02 negative for every sufficiently small positive
epsilon. This is an exact sign counterexample to this proposed transport.

More strongly, keeping the boundary weights (7), NO choice of the free
skew part can repair this derivative at depth2. At zero put

 u=(r-2,1-r,1,0)^T,
 z=(2r-3,2-2r,0,1)^T.

They span the orthogonal complement of e,p, where
p=(1,1-r,r-1,2r-3)^T. Hence all alternative skew first derivatives,
while retaining the same A0 and dot w, differ by gamma(u z^T-z u^T).
After multiplication by V0^(-1), their changes at entries02 and30 are
respectively gamma and -r gamma. Therefore every such derivative obeys

 r dot A_02+dot A_30=(40+10r)/7>0.                       (10)

Both baseline entries are zero, so both derivatives would need to be
nonpositive. Equation (10) rules this out. This test permits EVERY finite
interpolation pair and every free skew direction for the specified w.
It does NOT exclude changing w differently, creating a nonzero PSD
remainder, relaxing q, or choosing another baseline certificate.

The accompanying tiny exact Q(sqrt(2)) audit checks all value flows,
complete matrices and derivatives at one and three steps. It is an
arithmetic audit, not a solver and not a correctness authority; the
displayed exact matrices and formulas are the mathematical evidence.

## 5. All-depth stiffness of any rank-one continuation

There is a separate quantitative necessity that does not fix the two-weight
choice (7) or the free skew part. It describes rank-one continuation at the
sharp Huber rate, not arbitrary PSD continuations.

At depth m let N=2^m and M0=rho^m. For an integer i let pop(i) be the
number of ones in its binary expansion. On the undeformed Silver word,

 p_i=(-1)^i rho^(-pop(i)),
 dot p_i=-(i+pop(i))p_i,                                (11)

where dots denote derivatives in epsilon=s/m at zero. To verify this,
sum_(t=1)^i nu_2(t)=i-pop(i): summing floor(i/2^k) over k gives the
left side and summing the binary expansion gives the right side.
The product of h_t-1 therefore has exponent -pop(i). Under the physical
path the logarithmic derivative of h_t-1 is nu_2(t+1)-2, whose sum is
-i-pop(i). This proves (11), including p_n=-1/M0 and
dot p_n=(N+m-1)/M0.

Let D=rho^2+2=2rho+3. The physical mass has exact formula

 M(epsilon)=A(epsilon)c^m+(1-A(epsilon))2^m,
 A(epsilon)=a/(c-2).

Here A(0)=1, dot A(0)=-D, and hence

                    dot M=M0(m-D)+D N.                (12)

Suppose ANY differentiable full certificate, with every pair available,
has q=1/(2M(epsilon)-1), a rank-one remainder q||x_0-w^Tg||^2, and
starts at (2). No multiplier formula or support pattern is assumed.
On the actual full-curvature quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, all Q_ij are zero,
x_0=1 and g_i=p_i. Thus

 q(1-w^T p)^2=q-p_n^2.

The baseline selects the analytic minus branch, so w^T p=mu in (7).
Let dot wphys=(dot h_0,...,dot h_(n-1),dot M), and let
zeta=dot w-dot wphys. Differentiating the preceding scalar equality gives

 p^T zeta=(dot M-M0(N+m-1))/(M0-1)=:Delta_m.             (13)

For detail, dot mu=[dot M-(2M0-1)(N+m-1)]/[M0(M0-1)].
Also wphys^T p=1+(M-1)p_n, whose derivative is
-dot M/M0+(M0-1)(N+m-1)/M0. Subtracting gives (13).

Tightness at q=1/(2M-1) additionally forces every optimum-source
multiplier to vanish: on the attaining Huber trajectory every Q_(star,i)
is strictly negative, while all finite Q and all Q_(i,star) are zero.
The PSD certificate must have zero value there, so complementarity gives
this assertion. Then A^T e=2e_n and sum w=2M-1, whence sum zeta=0.
Since max_i p_i=1 and min_i p_i=-1/rho, this also yields

 sum_i |zeta_i| >=sqrt(2)|Delta_m|.                    (14)

Indeed subtract the midpoint of the range of p before applying the
triangle inequality, and use sum zeta=0. The two-weight derivative (7)
attains (14), so its failure in Section4 is not caused by excessive
total redistribution in this particular norm.

More significantly, put w0=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),M0). Since all h_i>1,
h_i|p_i|=|p_i|+|p_(i+1)|. Also
sum_(i=0)^n |p_i|=(1+1/rho)^m=(sqrt(2))^m. Therefore

 sum_i (w0)_i |p_i|=2(sqrt(2))^m-1/M0.

It follows that every such rank-one continuation satisfies

 max_i |zeta_i|/(w0)_i
       >= |Delta_m|/[2(sqrt(2))^m-1/M0].               (15)

By (12), Delta_m/N tends to -1 as m tends to infinity. Consequently,
in the original s parameter the right side of (15) is asymptotic to

                         (sqrt(2))^m/(2m).            (16)

Thus no horizon-uniform bound on the componentwise relative FIRST
derivative of this rank-one boundary vector is possible, even with the
complete coefficient array available. Natural physical weights have
bounded relative s derivatives at zero, so the same divergence cannot
be hidden by merely counting their physical variation.

This is NOT a bound on total finite-path change: an analytic path may
have a very thin boundary layer. It is NOT an obstruction to PSD
remainders of higher rank. Such a remainder can absorb the quadratic
probe, so (13) need no longer hold as an equality.

The same phenomenon occurs at the actual canonical source baseline (3).
Keep q(epsilon)=1/t(epsilon), t^2-t+1=M(epsilon)^2, and assume the
complete remainder remains rank one starting at (3). Set
wphys=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),t). The quadratic calibration again forces
w^T p=1-sqrt(1-t p_n^2). Differentiation gives

 p^T(dot w-dot wphys)
                   =(dot M-M0(N+m-1))/(t0-1),         (17)

where t0=(1+sqrt(4M0^2-3))/2. To check, use
dot t=2M0 dot M/(2t0-1),
sqrt(1-t0/M0^2)=(t0-1)/M0,
and (t0-1)^2+t0=M0^2. The weighted denominator is now
2(sqrt(2))^m-1+(t0-1)/M0, so the same asymptotic lower bound (16)
holds. This calculation allows both directions of optimum incidence;
it uses only the rank-one full remainder and the specified rate.

## 6. A complete higher-rank analytic repair, with unaffordable exact cost

There is no qualitative obstruction to analytic continuation when the
objective coefficient is unrestricted. The following construction gives
an explicit full-array path from EITHER baseline, at every m>=2, with
all actual pairs and both optimum directions strictly positive for s>0.
Its objective cost is far too large. This is a quantified failed repair,
not the uniform rate conclusion requested in TASK.

Use N=2^m, n=N-1, M0=rho^m, and set

 Hmax=1+3M0, Lmax=6M0, R=(1+4Hmax)^2,
 d_i=8R^(n-i), D=diag(d_i), Qsafe=4sum_i d_i,
 eta0=1/[2N(1+n Hmax)],
 Asafe=D+eta0(NI-ee^T).

All physical h_i(s) lie below Hmax, and |h_i(s)-h_i(0)|<=Lmax s.
For the latter, differentiate
rho^(j-1)(1-s/m)(1+s/m)^(j-1), j=0,...,m-1. The j=0 derivative is
at most2/(m rho) in magnitude. For j>=1 its magnitude is at most
(j/m)rho^(j-1)(1+1/m)^(j-1)<=3M0. Both asserted bounds follow.
The bound (1+1/m)^m<3 follows by expanding the binomial and using
binom(m,k)/m^k<=1/k! and sum_(k>=0)1/k!<3.

First omit the eta0 term. The gradient block is sym(DV)-e_n e_n^T.
After congruence by D^(-1/2), its off-diagonal entries have absolute
values at most (Hmax/2)R^(-|i-j|/2). The sum of absolute off-diagonal
entries in each row is at most Hmax/(sqrt(R)-1)=1/4. Its diagonal is1,
except the terminal value1-1/8=7/8. The inequality
2|z_i z_j|<=z_i^2+z_j^2 therefore proves

 sym(DV)-e_n e_n^T >= D/4.

For every scalar z and gradient vector g, weighted Cauchy--Schwarz gives

 Qsafe z^2-z sum_i d_i g_i+g^T[sym(DV)-e_n e_n^T]g
 >=(Qsafe/2)z^2+[1/4-(sum_i d_i)/(2Qsafe)]sum_i d_i g_i^2
 >=z^2+sum_i g_i^2.

Here Qsafe=4sum d_i and min d_i=8. Thus the complete matrix before
the eta0 term is at least the identity, including its initial row.
The eta0 term has zero row and column sums, so it changes only the
gradient block by eta0 sym((NI-ee^T)V). Its norm is at most
eta0 N(1+n Hmax)=1/2: NI-ee^T has norm N, and
||V||<=1+||T||_F<=1+n Hmax. Consequently

                    Mcert(Qsafe,Asafe,h(s)) >= I/2.     (18)

Its actual finite multipliers are all eta0>0. Its optimum multipliers
are b_i=d_i and a_i=d_i-2*1_(i=n), all strictly positive. This proves
the full signs and PSD directly, without an appeal to Slater theory.

Let A0,q0 be either FULL baseline in Section1, and let
C_A=sum_(i,j)|A0_ij|, K=2 C_A N Lmax. Define

 A(s)=[A0+K s Asafe]/(1+K s),
 q(s)=[q0+K s Qsafe]/(1+K s).                            (19)

The physical h(s) stays the assigned word. The frozen baseline array
changes its full matrix by a gradient block sym(A0(T(s)-T(0))). Its
norm is at most C_A N Lmax s=K s/2. Hence

 Mcert(q0,A0,h(s))>=-(K s/2)I.

Convexly combining this with (18) proves FULL PSD of (19) at every
s in[0,1]. All final multiplier signs hold by convex combination, and
every pair is positive for s>0. The matrix and array are real analytic;
the baseline singular kernel and all mixed directions are included in
the displayed bounds. Everything is predetermined by m and s.

The exact cost explains why this does not solve the research task.
C_A>=sum b_i>=2 for either baseline, so K tends to infinity. For every
fixed s>0, the fraction K s/(1+K s) eventually exceeds1/2. Therefore

 q(s)>=Qsafe/2>=16(1+4Hmax)^(2n).                       (20)

The resulting certificate level diverges rather than being O(1/M_m).
This specific dense repair is feasible but fails the scalar comparison
by an enormous margin. It rules out interpreting singularity of the
chosen baseline as an absolute obstruction to whole-array analytic
continuation; an obstruction relevant to TASK must be quantitative.
No optimality is claimed for (19), and its cost is not a lower bound on
other higher-rank repairs.

## 7. What remains open

The canonical full baseline has now been calibrated at every depth, and
the sharper full baseline is separately explicit. The dense proposal
(7)--(8) solves its full PSD and all mixed-block conditions identically,
but its ACTUAL coefficient signs fail. Even arbitrary free skew changes
cannot fix its prescribed boundary-weight derivative at three steps.
Any rank-one continuation at either displayed rate also has the growing
relative-derivative requirement (16). These are scoped obstructions,
not a claim that every optimal certificate has either shape.

The explicit higher-rank path (19) uses every such sign direction and
is feasible at every depth, but its actual objective coefficient (20)
is unaffordable. The assignment permits other higher-rank PSD remainders,
different boundary weights, nonzero optimum sources, and a relaxed q.
Those freedoms are not excluded here. To obtain a separation, one must control the FINAL
signs and the exact finite-path Schur remainder uniformly in m, with
limsup q_m M_m<e/rho at s=1 (or the corresponding strict fixed-s gap).
Neither a derivative, the rank-one scalar moment, nor the real-skew
parameterization supplies that remaining estimate. No such certificate
or unrestricted obstruction has been proved in this round.
