# Zero source load and a final-three-query coefficient cut

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, invocation88.
Guidance36171551390a5c9e; plan82e903a1d3f19b49.
Original exact-profile minimax target OPEN. No fact_submit; no new fact_id.

## 1. Outcome and scope

Setting the entire incoming optimum vector to zero genuinely repairs the
previous source-column deficit. For the first law below, the full original
quadratic is PSD at the assigned charge, every optimum multiplier has the
required sign, both incoming moments are exactly zero, the first and last
column capacities pass, and both endpoint row-moment screens pass.

The literal canonical skew nevertheless has

    lambda_(r,l)<-3/2,   N_rr-2alpha_r<-3/2,

where l=n-1 is the last QUERY and r=l-1. More strongly, no compatible skew
completion can make this law nonnegative. Let j=l-2 and define

    F3=2(P_jl+P_rl)+(3-a*beta)P_ll-mu_l-nu_l-2alpha_l.

For EVERY compatible skew, there is the exact original-coefficient identity

    F3=b*lambda_(r,l)
          +sum_(i<j)[lambda_(l,i)+lambda_(i,l)].                (1.1)

But for this law F3<-1/6. This is a positive weighted sum of specified
original finite coefficients. It excludes all completions, including any
explicit or unspecified skew correction annihilating both e and t. The
argument does not infer all-completion failure from the canonical entry.

The second, separately frozen law changes BOTH the paid allocation and
metric: nu=(1/64)e_r, with a polynomial tail weight m^(-2)rho instead of
p^2 rho. Its incoming clock is t_r/64, not1/64. Its exact paid charge still
passes, and the same original-coefficient cut gives F3<-1/6. Neither law
is a certificate or a legal primal counterexample.

The stronger scoped implication is that, with the fixed terminal pool and
fixed late common column below, a weighted-variance metric having unit
source and last atoms and an additional tail omega*rho with0<omega<=1
cannot be repaired merely by choosing ANY nonnegative incoming optimum
vector or ANY zero-sum paid vector at charge q<=17/(16D). This statement
allows arbitrary compatible skew matrices. It is a necessary-condition
argument, not a third coefficient law or an existence assertion.

The obstruction requires a nonvanishing local metric contribution at the
last query; the normalized metrics here have K_ll=O(p). Changing the
terminal pool, the common column, or the metric architecture remains open.
No unrestricted dual infeasibility or original minimax answer is asserted.

All new mathematical statements in this file are part of ONE jointly
argued UNVERIFIED draft. Earlier unverified reports supply awareness only.
Only accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 are used as facts, at the
scopes stated below. Accepted d8b59c7d92425121 is representation only and
is not a premise of feasibility or a quantitative rate here.

## 2. Original problem and literal finite data

For a finite predetermined nonnegative word h of length n and L>0, W_n(h)
is the supremum of2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite
Euclidean dimensions, convex differentiable L-smooth functions with an
attained minimum, chosen minimizers x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns
W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tending to1, where W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and U_n^ZJ
is the original exact all-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark. It is not a
restricted-word or restricted-function-class question.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/D<47/50 for the word below, with
U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. It gives the799/800 separation only CONDITIONALLY on the
unproved full bound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for every m>=1536. Accepted1252
supplies the full interpolation orientation and the unqueried-terminal
square, with every queried-prefix pair retained. No new rate follows
without all original nonnegative coefficients.

Fix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. EVERY child keeps these same parameters:

    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(u+1)=(H_u,1+a*c^u,H_u), 0<=u<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1, j=l-2,
    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).

All short notation in this report is LOCAL. In particular rho is a
probability vector, not the benchmark exponent, and j=l-2 unless explicitly
used as a dummy ordered-pair index. e is the all-ones column on indices
0,...,l, and e_i is a coordinate column. Define

    Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_iu=h_u for u<i, Ltri_iu=0 for u>i,
    t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(u<i)h_u,
    T=t_l=1/p-b, Delta=T-1, hgap=h_r=1+beta,
    t_r=T-hgap.

The actual last three steps, at j,r,l, are (b,hgap,b). The actual terminal
sample is n, distinct from l, and its gradient remains independent.

Here are uniform symbolic bounds used below:

    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1,
    2/5<a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12, 1-2a>a^2,
    7/5<b<17/12, 15/8<hgap<2,
    c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-40),
    3/(4p)<Delta<1/p, Delta-b>1/(2p),
    p<dclock:=1/Delta<4p/3,
    0<rclock:=hgap/Delta<4p.                                  (2.1)

For the lower a bound, sqrt(2)<10/7 and
7*6143/(17*6145)>2/5 suffice. The upper bound follows from
beta/(1+eps)<1; then1+a<sqrt(2) proves1-2a>a^2. For the mass put
vword=beta/[c(c-2)]. Since c>1+sqrt(2),0<vword<1, and substitution gives

    1+S_u=(1-vword)2^u+vword*c^u.

This proves the p bounds. The clock bounds follow by substituting
Delta=1/p-b-1. No recurrence or numerical trajectory is executed.

For suffix length Ls in[0,n], let sbit(Ls) be its binary digit sum and put

    rtail_Ls=product_(u=n-Ls)^(n-1)(h_u-1)
             =beta^Ls*c^(-sbit(Ls)), s=rtail_n,
    k_i=h_i product_(i<u<n)(1-h_u), u_i=|k_i|,
    U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.

Indeed h_(n-v)-1=a*c^v2(v), where v2(v) is the exponent of2 dividing the
positive integer v, and sum_(v=1)^Ls v2(v)=Ls-sbit(Ls). With Ls=l-i,

    u_i=rtail_Ls+rtail_(Ls+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,
    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a*hgap, u_j=b*a*beta.

The last three terms give U>7/3. Also n>16m^2 for m>=16 by induction and
beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2. Therefore beta^n<2^(-4m), and

    u_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64.

In particular

    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, s<p, rho_i>0.                           (2.2)

Finite product telescoping gives e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h and t^T k=S.
These identities can also be checked by successively substituting the
literal finite products; they involve no executed recurrence.

## 3. Fixed terminal pool and late common column

Set Vtail=t^T[k-(1-p)rho] and Vlast=b(1-p). To justify the guard Vtail>32,
let

    Ztail=sum_(Ls=0)^n rtail_Ls,
    Mtail=sum_(Ls=0)^n Ls*rtail_Ls.

Expanding the binary digits gives

    Ztail=product_(u=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^u)/c),
    U=2Ztail-1-s,
    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(u=0)^(m-1)2^u beta^(2^u)/(c+beta^(2^u)).

Choose u with m/2<2^u<=m. Bernoulli's inequality gives beta^(2^u)>=3/4,
so that summand is greater than3m/28. An index shift yields

    sum_i rho_i(l-i)=(2Mtail-Ztail+1-ns)/U
                       >(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2.

For the last inequality ns<1 follows from n<2^m and s<2^(-4m); the
numerator is positive, U<2Ztail, and Mtail/Ztail>3m/28>1.
Since1/p-t_i>=b+l-i,
Vtail=(1-p)sum_i rho_i(1/p-t_i)>32 for every m>=1536.

The terminal law, the same in both mechanisms, is

    xiT=(2-Vlast)/(Vtail-Vlast),
    alpha=(1-p)[(1-xiT)e_l+xiT*rho], alpha_star=p,
    v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s, eta=F/E.

The exact identities and bounds are

    0<xiT<1/45, e^T alpha=1-p, V:=t^T v=2,
    alpha_l>19/20, a+p<=v_l<1/2,
    0<alpha_0<u_0, 0<v_0<u_0,
    e^T v=E, alpha^T t=1/p-3, 1-p/32<eta<1.              (3.1)

Here4/3<Vlast<17/12 and Vtail>32 give the mixture bounds. In particular
alpha_l>44(1-p)/45>19/20, and v_l=b-alpha_l<1/2. At0,
alpha_0/u_0=(1-p)xiT/U<1. The eta estimate uses2s/E<p/32.
Define the positive vector R=Ltri^T v. Its exact coordinates are

    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(u<i)alpha_u)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0,
    R_0=bE-a v_0<2p.                                      (3.2)

Subtract Ltri^T alpha from Ltri^T k=h to obtain this formula.

Keep the common vector required by the assignment:

    z=b e_l+(E-b)e_r,
    Z=t^T z=E*t_r+b*hgap,
    Zstar=1+b*hgap.

Thus e^T z=E, z_0=z_j=0, z_l=b, z_r=E-b. Direct substitution shows

    |Z-Zstar|<4p, 3<Z<4,
    |eta Z^2-Zstar^2|<33p.                              (3.3)

Indeed Z-Zstar=-(b+hgap)p+s*t_r and0<t_r<1/p. Square the clock and use
1-eta<p/32 for the second estimate. In particular E<1/100 and
b-E>4/3, beta*b>4/3, eta>9/10.

## 4. All original coefficients and the entire paid quadratic

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain the original independent
formal vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n. By accepted1252 use

    Q_iu=f_i-f_u+<g_i,x_u-x_i>+||g_i-g_u||^2/2.

For every queried index let y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. The GD
substitutions give

    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g, g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),
    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                                  (4.1)

Ltri is unit triangular and invertible. Thus these coordinates retain
ALL queried-gradient directions, and g_n is still a separate free vector.

Given positive weights w, write Ww=sum_i w_i and
K=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww. Given e^T zeta=0, define

    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T, d=zeta+F(v-z),
    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                         (4.2)

Every actual original coefficient is specified by

    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i (i<n),
    lambda_(u,n)=0 for u in {star,0,...,l},
    lambda_(i,u)=-N_iu for distinct queried i,u,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,
    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i,
    lambda_(u,u)=0.

In particular

    N_iu=P_iu+A_iu+h_i sum_(v>i)(P_vu+A_vu)            (4.3)

is a literal finite formula for EVERY queried pair. The prefix outgoing
mass at i is N_ii and its prefix incoming mass is N_ii-2alpha_i. Adding
the terminal mass2alpha_i cancels every queried value divergence. The
terminal and optimum divergences are2 and-2, respectively.

The prefix interpolation quadratic is-g^T N y. Substituting(4.1), using
skewness, and adding the full terminal expression from accepted1252 gives

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_iu Q_iu
        =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y
           +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2
           +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.            (4.4)

For a direct check, Pe=Ev+Fz, Pe-d=2s v+2Fz-zeta, and
eta E^2=p^2-s^2. The terminal subtraction is
-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2, so its cross term cancels the2s v part exactly.
All sampled values cancel by the stated divergences.

Writing ybar_w=sum w_i y_i/Ww and J=(1/4)sum zeta_i^2/w_i gives

    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2
       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+zeta_i*x_0/(2w_i)||^2
       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2.        (4.5)

Necessity of the charge is also literal: take scalar x_0=1,
y_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i), where
c0=1+sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i E), and set g_n=-s+v^T y. All three square
families vanish, and(4.1) recovers the original queried gradients.
Consequently full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J.
The common-gradient direction g_i=G is retained: its prefix remainder is

    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>+(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2,
    theta=t^T zeta, X=t^T Kt.

No restriction to a difference space or removal of the initial charge
has been made. PSD of this signed polynomial is not enough for a bound:
the interpolation coefficients must all be nonnegative as well.

## 5. Both incoming moments and a literal skew completion

Direct multiplication of(4.2) gives, for V=2,

    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta,
    nu=Kt+2v+eta Zz-At-2alpha,
    e^T nu=theta-(2-6p),
    t^T nu=X+4+eta Z^2-2(1/p-3).                      (5.1)

For the first moment use t=Ltri e and t^T A e=t^T d. For the second
use t^T A t=0. These derivations retain the actual clock of each incoming
coefficient. Set theta0=2-6p.

Given a prescribed incoming vector nu with mass Mnu=e^T nu and clock
Cnu=t^T nu, the compatible choices are

    theta=theta0+Mnu,
    Xreq=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2+Cnu.                       (5.2)

For any positive metric base what, let

    tbase=sum what_i t_i/sum what_i,
    Vbase=sum what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,
    w=(Xreq/Vbase)what,
    K=diag(w)-ww^T/sum w,
    zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.                              (5.3)

Then e^T zeta=0, t^T Kt=Xreq, t^T zeta=theta and
J=theta^2/(4Xreq). Define the LITERAL canonical skew by

    rvec=Kt+2v+eta Zz-2alpha-nu,
    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,
    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,
    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,
    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.                  (5.4)

The two moments(5.2) give e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0. Consequently
e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0. Multiplication shows Ae=d and At=rvec, so the
incoming optimum vector produced by the ORIGINAL coefficients is exactly
the prescribed nu. Every term in A is skew. All other completions are
A+C, where C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0; no such correction is assumed feasible.

For every law(5.3) below theta>0. The zeta_i have the signs of t_i-tbase,
change sign once, and sum to zero. Every proper suffix sum is positive.
If zeta_i<0, that suffix is at least-zeta_i, so
(Ltri^T zeta)_i>= (h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0. If zeta_i>=0, its diagonal plus
positive suffix expression is positive as well. Thus(3.2) proves mu_i>0
for EVERY queried index. All terminal multipliers and the assigned nu
are nonnegative. This argument concerns optimum signs only.

## 6. Law I: exactly zero incoming load

Freeze the first law as follows:

    nu=0, theta=theta0=2-6p,
    Xreq=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2,
    xiM=a(1-a)-a^3/8, sigma=xiM/(1-xiM),
    what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+p^2*rho.

Use(5.3),(5.4),(4.2),(4.3) for every other coefficient, with C=0.
Since0<xiM<1/4, one has0<sigma<1/3 and every what_i>0. The unit
source and last atoms imply Vbase>=Delta^2/2: for any scalar c0,
(1-c0)^2+(T-c0)^2>=Delta^2/2, and the other terms are nonnegative.
The new zero-load estimates are

    2/p-26<Xreq<2/p,
    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p>0,
    1<Xreq/Delta<2, |Xreq/Delta-2|<32p,
    1<theta0<2, theta0/Xreq>p.                         (6.1)

For example Xreq=2/p-10-eta Z^2 and eta Z^2<16. Also
Xreq-2Delta=2b-8-eta Z^2<0. Finally

    theta0-pXreq=p(4+eta Z^2)>0.

There is no residual1/64 in either incoming moment: equations(5.1)
give e^T nu=t^T nu=0 exactly, and(5.4) produces nu_i=0 entrywise.
The paid charge obeys

    J=theta0^2/(4Xreq)<(65/128)p,
    qmin=p^2+J<(66/128)p<(17/32)p<qtar.                (6.2)

The first inequality follows from Xreq>(1023/1024)theta0/p and
(1024/1023)/2<65/128; p<1/128 proves the next one. Thus the entire
original-space polynomial(4.5) is PSD at qtar.

### 6.1 Every column total, including the failed penultimate column

To give every diagonal and column without a matrix-existence placeholder,
use the suffix sums

    Wtail_i=sum_(u>i)w_u, Dtail_i=sum_(u>i)d_u,
    Ftail_i=sum_(u>i)fvec_u, Ctail_i=sum_(u>i)cvec_u.

For ALL i=0,...,l the canonical diagonal and finite incoming mass are

    Ddiag_i=N_ii
       =w_i[1-(w_i+h_i Wtail_i)/Ww]+v_i R_i
        +eta*z_i*(Ltri^T z)_i
        +h_i[(Dtail_i-(l-i)d_i)/n
             +(cvec_i Ftail_i-fvec_i Ctail_i)/Ve],
    c_i=Ddiag_i-nu_i-2alpha_i
          =sum_(u!=i, u<n)lambda_(u,i).                (6.3)

This includes all queried columns and their actual terminal loads.
For a correction C the diagonal changes by h_i sum_(u>i)C_ui.
The first and last diagonals are invariant under such corrections:

    Ddiag_0=b(Pe-d)_0-aP_00
           =b(2s v_0-zeta_0)-a(K_00+v_0^2),
    Ddiag_l=P_ll.                                    (6.4)

Here the first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T and z_0=0.

For explicit source bounds normalize xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta and put

    B=sum what_i, M=sum what_i*xclock_i,
    M2=sum what_i*xclock_i^2, B0=what_0,
    Hvar=M2-M^2/B,
    fbase=B0*M/B, kbase=B0(1-B0/B),
    Y=-(Kt)_0=(Xreq/Delta)*fbase/Hvar.

One has Hvar>=1/2, B<5/2, M>=1, fbase>2/5,
Hvar<=B/4<5/8 and fbase/Hvar<3. The upper variance bound uses
xclock in[0,1] and xclock^2<=xclock. Hence

    Y>1/2,
    aK_00/Y=a*dclock*(B-B0)/M<(5/6)p,
    -zeta_0=(theta0/Delta)*fbase/Hvar<12p.              (6.5)

For the middle bound B-B0<3/2, M>=1 and(2.1) suffice.
Using theta0/Xreq>p in(6.4) gives

    p/4<Ddiag_0<32p,   c_0=Ddiag_0-2alpha_0>p/8.       (6.6)

For the lower bound the positive metric difference is greater than
(b-5/6)pY>17p/60, and the subtracted a v_0^2 is less than p/100.
The source terminal mass2alpha_0<p^2/32 cannot destroy the margin.
Thus the old constant-load shortage really has disappeared.

For reference the whole paid source-capacity identity also holds.
Define Rsource=K_00+(b/a)zeta_0+(b^2/a^2)(q-p^2). Setting
x_0=x, y=u e_0+[x-(z_0/E)u]e and g_n=-s*x+v^T y in(4.4) shows
K_00*u^2+zeta_0*x*u+(q-p^2)*x^2>=0 when the full form is PSD, so
Rsource>=0. Expansion gives

    b^2*q/a-N_00
       =a Rsource+a(v_0-bs/a)^2+a eta(z_0-bE/a)^2>=0.  (6.7)

The constants use s^2+eta E^2=p^2. Thus N_00<=b^2 q/a, which is
compatible with(6.6), since nu_0=0 rather than a fixed positive load.

The metric scale is less than8p, so K_ll<=w_l<12p. Since
v_l>a>2/5 and eta>1-p/32,

    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta b^2>21/10,
    c_l=P_ll-2alpha_l>1/10.                           (6.8)

The last adjacent pair also passes its necessary symmetric-factor test:

    N_lr+N_rl=2P_lr+hgap P_ll
       =2K_lr+hgap K_ll+v_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)
                    +eta*b*(2E-eps*b)<-1/5.          (6.9)

Here v_r=-a*hgap-alpha_r, v_l<1/2, so the v term is less than
-(3/25)hgap<-9/40. The positive parts of the metric and common terms
are less than24p and4p. This does not assert the separate two signs.

In fact the canonical completion fails at the penultimate query.
For i=r,l, w_i<12p, |(Kt)_i|<12, |zeta_i|<24p and |d_i|<28p.
Also |rvec_i|<24, tmean<1/p, |d^T t|<3, and hence |fvec_i|<55.
As |cvec_i|<=Delta and Ve>=Delta^2/2, formula(5.4) yields

    |A_lr|<400p.                                     (6.10)

All bounds use only the two endpoint clocks, not an assumption that
an exponentially long array was evaluated. Since R_r>0, v_r<0 and
z_r=E-b, equations(4.3) give

    N_rr=K_rr+hgap K_lr+v_r R_r
                 +eta(E-b)(E+beta*b)+hgap A_lr<-3/2,
    N_rl=K_rl+hgap K_ll+v_l R_r
                 +eta*b*(E+beta*b)-A_lr>3/2.         (6.11)

Indeed K_rr+hgap K_lr<=w_r<12p and K_rl>=-w_l>-12p;
eta(b-E)(E+beta*b)>8/5 and eta*b*(E+beta*b)>8/5 by(3.3).
The remaining error is bounded by812p in the first line and412p
in the second. Consequently c_r=N_rr-2alpha_r<-3/2 and the literal
original entry lambda_(r,l)=-N_rl<-3/2.

This is an explicit failed OTHER column as well as an explicit failed
entry. Formula(6.3) specifies every remaining column. They are not all
certified nonnegative; a complete all-column passing claim is false.
The next section proves failure for every skew completion, so no
exhaustive classification of the remaining canonical columns is needed.

### 6.2 Both endpoint row-moment screens, rederived for nu=0

Let

    S0=sum_(u>0)N_0u,
    P0=sum_(u>0)(t_u-1)N_0u, G0=P0-Delta*S0.

Nonpositive first-row off-diagonals require
S0<=0 and Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0. Directly from(5.1),(6.4), with nu_0=0,

    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0,
    P0=b(4s+2FZ-theta0)-2a(Pt)_0+2a alpha_0-mu_0.

The metric part of S0 is less than-p/12 by(6.5); the rest is less
than7p^2. Hence S0<-p/16. The detailed finite clock calculation in
Appendix A proves

    p<G0<30p,
    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<30p-1/32<0.                 (6.12)

Thus this ENTIRE first-row moment screen passes, notwithstanding(6.11).

For the last row define

    Slast=mu_l-P_ll,
    Jlast=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,
    Hlast=(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.

Nonpositive off-diagonals require Slast<=0, Jlast<=0, Hlast>=0.
Let

    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/Hvar,
    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/Hvar.

Both are positive and less than3. Formula(5.1) gives exactly

    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast
            +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b
            +eta*b*(2Z-hgap*b),
    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta0]/Delta.                (6.13)

For the three-atom base without p^2*rho,
Alast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/(1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2)>=1-xiM.
Restoring the positive tail changes this ratio by at most32p^2;
Appendix A supplies the quotient estimate. Also
2-64p<Cclock<3. The metric negative term is bounded by24p.
The v_l polynomial is increasing on[a,1/2], with derivative greater
than2. The terms preceding the common-column term in(6.13) therefore
exceed

    2a-2a^2-2xiM-125p=a^3/4-125p>0.

There is no incoming-load loss in this zero-load formula. By(3.3),
the common-column term is greater than3. Furthermore mu_l<19p,
P_ll>21/10 and (Nt)_l<26. It follows that

    Slast<-2, Jlast<26-(21/10)Delta<0, Hlast>3.        (6.14)

Both endpoint screens really pass. They do not imply sign feasibility.

## 7. An exact final-three-query cut for every skew completion

This part is a direct finite-sign argument in the original coefficient
array. It does not require the canonical formula or a flow-existence
oracle. Keep j=l-2,r=l-1 and the physical steps(b,hgap,b).
The last rows of Ltri^T imply, for EVERY skew A,

    N_lr+N_rl=2P_rl+hgap P_ll,
    N_jl+N_lj=2P_jl+b N_rl-b*beta*P_ll.                (7.1)

For the second identity, N_lj=P_lj+A_lj and
N_jl=P_jl+A_jl+b(P_rl+A_rl+P_ll); substitute
P_rl+A_rl=N_rl-hgap P_ll and hgap-1=beta.
Thus no untested symmetry or omission of a queried index is involved.

The exact row and column sums at l give

    mu_l+nu_l+2alpha_l-2P_ll
       =N_lj+N_jl+N_lr+N_rl
                       +sum_(i<j)(N_li+N_il).

Insert(7.1) and use2+hgap-b*beta=3-a*beta. With F3 as in Section1,
this proves the exact identity

    F3=-b N_rl-sum_(i<j)(N_li+N_il)
       =b*lambda_(r,l)+sum_(i<j)[lambda_(l,i)+lambda_(i,l)].

If every finite coefficient is nonnegative, F3>=0. This necessity
holds regardless of the signs or values of the other canonical columns.

Now keep the fixed terminal and common columns of Section3, and allow
any weighted-variance K. Since k_j=b*a*beta and k_r=-a*hgap,

    v_j+v_r=a(a*beta-1)-alpha_j-alpha_r
                                      <=a(a*beta-1).

The common part in F3 is
eta[(1-a*beta)b^2+2bE]; the bracket is positive, so it is at most the
same bracket without eta. Off-diagonal entries of K are negative.
Assuming only the necessary optimum signs mu_l,nu_l>=0, we obtain

    F3<=G(v_l)+3K_ll+2bE,
    G(v)=2a(a*beta-1)v+(3-a*beta)v^2
                               +2v-2b+(1-a*beta)b^2.

For a<=v<=1/2 the derivative of G is positive: it is
2a(a*beta-1)+2(3-a*beta)v+2>2-2a>0. Since v_l<1/2,

    G(v_l)<G(1/2)
       =g(a,beta):=3/4-a+a^2-a*beta*(5/4+a+a^2).

Both partial derivatives of g are strictly negative for
2/5<=a<1/2 and beta>0. In fact
partial_a g=-1+2a-beta*(5/4+2a+3a^2)<0 and
partial_beta g=-a*(5/4+a+a^2)<0. Therefore

    G(v_l)<g(2/5,99/100)=-5169/25000<-1/5.

Also2bE<4p. This proves the useful exact-scope estimate

    F3<-1/5+3K_ll+4p.                                (7.2)

For Law I, K_ll<12p, so F3<-1/5+40p<-1/6. This uniformly excludes
EVERY compatible completion of Law I for every SAME enclosing m>=1536.
In particular repairing the isolated coefficient(6.11) cannot repair
all the other coefficients appearing in(1.1).

### 7.1 Literal clock-neutral four-index circulations and their effects

No circulation is needed to prove the obstruction. To make the surviving
skew freedom concrete, take four distinct queried indices i0<i1<i2<i3,
write t_a=t_(ia) just in this paragraph, and define original-index columns

    u=(t_1-t_2)e_(i0)+(t_2-t_0)e_(i1)+(t_0-t_1)e_(i2),
    vcir=(t_1-t_3)e_(i0)+(t_3-t_0)e_(i1)+(t_0-t_1)e_(i3),
    C=kappa*(u*vcir^T-vcir*u^T), kappa any real scalar.

Summing each column gives zero. Taking its clock inner product gives
(t_1-t_2)t_0+(t_2-t_0)t_1+(t_0-t_1)t_2=0, and the identical formula
with2 replaced by3. Consequently C^T=-C, Ce=Ct=0, and C is supported
on these four original queried indices. Its EXACT array effect is

    DeltaN_iu=kappa[(Ltri^T u)_i*vcir_u
                                 -(Ltri^T vcir)_i*u_u].

Thus DeltaN e=0, (DeltaN)^T e=0 and DeltaN t=0. It preserves mu,nu,
all row clocks, P, the charge, and F3. Its source and last diagonal
effects are zero, using the first and last rows of Ltri^T. It can move
individual signs but cannot alter the negative total(1.1).

These formulas can generate the entire neutral skew space using fixed
anchors i0=0,i1=l. More explicitly, for each interior index i set
q_i=e_i-[(T-t_i)/Delta]e_0-[(t_i-1)/Delta]e_l. Each q_i annihilates e
and t and has its unique interior coordinate1. For any neutral skew C,

    C=sum_(0<i<u<l) C_iu*(q_i q_u^T-q_u q_i^T).

The right side has the same interior entries and annihilates e,t.
Their difference has all interior/interior entries zero; its interior
rows then have only columns0,l, which must vanish by the two distinct
clock constraints. Its remaining two-by-two skew block also vanishes.
This proves the identity without an existence oracle. Every summand
is a four-index original-coordinate formula. For three indices the
space annihilating e,t is one-dimensional, and a skew map on that
space is zero; therefore no nonzero three-index skew has both kernels.
None of these formulas is presented as a successful sign repair.

## 8. Law II: late incoming mass and one polynomial tail weight

Only AFTER the decisive all-completion result in Section7, freeze this
second and final law:

    ellload=1/64, nu=ellload*e_r,
    omega=m^(-2), what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega*rho,
    theta=theta0+ellload,
    Xreq=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2+ellload*t_r.

Keep the same alpha,z and sigma. Use(5.3),(5.4),(4.2),(4.3) literally,
including-nu in rvec. This is a genuinely different paid allocation
and an explicit polynomial, rather than exponentially tiny, positive
tail metric. There is no third law.

The incoming moments are NOW

    e^T nu=ellload, t^T nu=ellload*t_r,

exactly. In particular, inserting a bare ellload instead of
ellload*t_r into Xreq would be wrong. As t_r=1/p-b-hgap,

    Xreq=theta/p-Gamma,
    Gamma=4+eta Z^2+ellload*(b+hgap), 4<Gamma<21.

Thus1<theta<129/64, Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta/p>0 and
Xreq<theta/p<9/(4p). The paid vector has both moments in(5.3), and

    J=theta^2/(4Xreq)<(516/1023)p<(65/128)p,
    p^2+J<(66/128)p<qtar.                             (8.1)

The factor516/1023 is exactly (129/64)*(1024/1023)/4.
Every optimum coefficient still passes by Section5. The full original
polynomial(4.5), with the new metric and paid vector, is PSD at qtar.
No order-p term was substituted for a constant or unweighted mass.

The base retains the two unit endpoint atoms, so Vbase>=Delta^2/2.
Since0<omega<1,

    Xreq/Vbase<8p, w_l<16p, K_ll<16p.

Section7 therefore gives F3<-1/5+52p<-1/6 for every compatible skew.
This disposes of the full second law at a finite original-coefficient
cut. Endpoint success is not asserted for this modified metric: the
p^2-tail perturbation estimates in Section6 are not silently reused
with omega=m^(-2). Equations(5.1),(6.3) remain the literal row, column
and clock formulas with the new nu,w; regardless of their other signs,
(1.1) prevents simultaneous finite-sign feasibility.

### 8.1 Scope beyond the two literal laws

This is a necessary-condition consequence, not a third design. Retain
the terminal pool and common column of Section3. Let
what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega*rho with0<omega<=1 (even any sigma>=0),
and let w be a positive scalar multiple of this base. Allow ANY
nonnegative incoming vector nu, ANY zero-sum paid vector zeta, and
ANY compatible skew. Suppose the complete original polynomial is PSD
at some q<=qtar and all optimum signs are nonnegative.

Set Mnu=e^T nu, Cnu=t^T nu, theta=t^T zeta, X=t^T Kt>0.
The same exact moment identities imply

    theta=theta0+Mnu>0,
    X=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2+Cnu,
    Cnu<=T*Mnu=(1/p-b)Mnu,
    X<theta/p.                                      (8.2)

From(4.5) and weighted Cauchy--Schwarz,

    theta^2=[sum_i zeta_i(t_i-tbase)]^2
        <=[sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i]*[sum_i w_i(t_i-tbase)^2]
        =4JX,
    q>=p^2+J>=theta^2/(4X)>theta*p/4.

Since qtar=17p/[16(2-p)]<9p/16, theta<9/4 and X<9/(4p).
The metric scale is X/Vbase, so again K_ll<=w_l<16p.
Equation(7.2) gives F3<-1/6, contradicting(1.1) if every original
finite coefficient is nonnegative.

Thus arbitrary reallocation of incoming mass, including moving it
to very late clocks, cannot fix THIS fixed terminal/common architecture
with a bounded additive tail in an endpoint-dominated variance metric.
This argument even permits a non-minimizing paid vector; its extra
charge cannot help the displayed necessary inequalities.

More precisely, finite-sign feasibility would require from(7.2)

    K_ll>1/15-(4/3)p.

For a general PSD detail matrix the cut(1.1) remains exact, but the
weighted-variance simplification2K_jl+2K_rl<=0 may fail. The precise
remaining local requirement is the unsimplified nonnegative F3, with
2K_jl+2K_rl+(3-a*beta)K_ll retained. A different common column or
terminal pool also changes the scalar obstruction. None of those
surviving freedoms is ruled out, solved, or instantiated as a third law.

## Appendix A. Finite first-endpoint clock calculation for Law I

This appendix applies ONLY to the zero-load,p^2-tail first law.
Set K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and Y=Xreq/Delta+eY. Since z_0=0,
(Pt)_0=-Y+2v_0 and mu_0=2pR_0-a zeta_0. Expanding the formulas
in Section6.2 gives exactly

    G0=(a/Delta)(b*theta0-4-eta Z^2)
         +2bFZ-2pT R_0+4bs-4a v_0+2a alpha_0
         +2bsDelta v_0-aDelta v_0^2+Epert,
    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta0(Delta-a)eY/Xreq.       (A.1)

In particular Xreq-Ttheta0=b*theta0-4-eta Z^2. No source incoming
load, weighted or unweighted, occurs in this identity.

Temporarily omit only p^2*rho from what. The remaining atoms are at
normalized clocks0,1,1-rclock with weights1,1,sigma. Let
Dclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2. Substituting B=2+sigma,
M=1+sigma(1-rclock), M2=1+sigma(1-rclock)^2 yields

    fbase/Hvar=(1-xiM*rclock)/Dclock,
    kbase/Hvar=1/Dclock,
    Epert=Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)
         [theta0(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)
                            -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock]. (A.2)

These are exact scalar identities. The first two follow from
B*Hvar=(1+sigma)Dclock. One has1-2p<Dclock<=1,
|1/Dclock-1|<rclock, a*dclock<p, rclock<4p,
Xreq*dclock<2 and theta0<2. Subtracting theta0 from the bracket in
(A.2) bounds its absolute error by35p. Consequently

    |Eint-2xiM*hgap*dclock|<106p^2.                  (A.3)

Restoring p^2*rho changes each of B,M,M2,B0 by at most p^2.
Both variances are at least1/2. Direct quotient subtraction bounds
the changes of fbase,kbase,Hvar by2p^2,4p^2,5p^2, and hence the
changes of fbase/Hvar and kbase/Hvar by32p^2. For clarity, in
Hvar=M2-M^2/B the three contributions are bounded by p^2,
(2M p^2+p^4)/B, and M^2 p^2/(B_old B_new); B>=2 and M<=B bound
their sum by5p^2. The other two numerator bounds follow by the same
displayed product/quotient subtraction, with B0_old=1.
The exact metric part of G0 is

    a(Xreq/Delta)(2fbase/Hvar-kbase/Hvar)
                   +theta0(1-a/Delta)fbase/Hvar-b*theta0.

Its change is less than400p^2. Each base uses its own variance
normalization, so this includes the change in w. The same estimate
bounds the change in Alast in Section6.2 by32p^2.

In(A.1), replacing theta0 by2 and eta Z^2 by Zstar^2 costs less
than42p^2. Replacing2bFZ by2b*dclock*Zstar costs less than63p^2:
use |p-dclock|<4p^2 and |Z-Zstar|<4p. Replacing-2pT R_0
by-2b*dclock costs less than20p^2, using R_0=bE-a v_0 and
pT=1-bp. The remaining five terms involving s,v_0,alpha_0 have total
absolute value less than5p^2 by(2.2),(3.1). Together with(A.3),

    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<1000p^2,
    Cfirst=a(2b-4-Zstar^2)+2b(Zstar-1)+2xiM*hgap
          =Cold+b*hgap*(2-a*b*hgap),
    Cold=a(2a-3)+2xiM*hgap
         =a(1-2a)-a^3/2-2eps*xiM.                    (A.4)

Using1-2a>a^2, a>2/5, xiM<1/4, eps<=1/6144 gives
Cold>a^3/2-eps/2>1/32. Also a*b*hgap<85/72<6/5 and
b*hgap>21/8, so the extra term exceeds21/10. Hence Cfirst>2;
a direct triangle bound in the first expression gives |Cfirst|<20.
Since p<dclock<4p/3 and p<2^(-40), this proves p<G0<30p as claimed.

## 9. Failure synthesis, literature and resource record

Law I does what the latest guidance intended at the source: it removes
the impossible constant incoming load and recomputes both moments and
the full initial price. The fixed late common column still pays the
last diagonal and both endpoint moments. It nevertheless places an
unpayable joint burden on original edges involving the final three
queried indices. The canonical negative penultimate column is one
manifestation; the exact positive weighted sum(1.1) is the reason no
balanced completion can repair it.

Law II changes the actual incoming clock and uses a polynomial tail
weight. Its global charge and both incoming moments are correct, but
its last metric diagonal stays O(p), and the same finite cut fails.
Section8.1 proves why varying the incoming allocation within this
bounded-tail architecture cannot change that conclusion. The local
common/terminal geometry or metric scaling must change as well.

This refutes coefficient-level sufficiency of PSD, optimum signs,
source/last capacities, the last adjacent-pair test and both endpoint
row moments for the prescribed first law. It does not construct a
smooth-convex primal witness. The original target remains OPEN.

Skills used: query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results,
construct-counterexamples and identify-key-failures. The selective
verification prohibition in TASK.md overrides the usual intermediate
fact gate for this invocation; every new result remains unverified.

Search stage: mature_subproblem. Active program: the paid original
coefficient architecture with zero source load and balanced skew.
Missing mechanism: all finite signs beyond endpoint moments. One
Matlas query about two-kernel skew matrices returned unrelated
noncommutative electrodynamics, Metzler control and weighing-matrix
statements. One web search located exterior-algebra background,
including Oxford lecture notes at
https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/hitchin/files/LectureNotes/Projective_geometry/Chapter_3_Exterior.pdf .
No external result is imported or used as a proof premise. No new
paper-dependent branch is opened: the four-index formulas and the
final cut are direct finite identities in the already audited original
coordinates, with all steps displayed above.

Resources: ONE invocation88; exactly TWO frozen coefficient laws.
Mathematical programs, numerical evaluations, arithmetic/trajectory/
recurrence replays, solvers, optimizers, benchmarkDP, formal calls,
fact_submit, installations, consults and subagents: ZERO. One Matlas
query and one web call; no external theorem imported. Administrative
text reads/writes and integrity checks only. No task overwrite,
automatic renewal, worker/service control, outward publication,
finalization, revocation, shutdown or Goal update. Return at cap1;
controller completion after return is not observed.

# Same-round correction: two further candidates fail before the threshold

UNVERIFIED research note, not a fact. Guidance63aeee04d056a192 was actually
read after first-path report8ce8b65121586185 had already been persisted.
It permitted at most two different candidates in the SAME round42 and
required early viability screens. This note records those two screens;
no long multiplier-continuation proof was attempted after either failed.
All new arguments are reproduced jointly below, using the original
identity in fact04219554e41d9750 and thresholds in4c92d3fc4eb6153e.

## Actual starting certificates and the two proposed physical deformations

Both candidates start at the ordinary all-ones word of their own length r.
That word has a concrete original full certificate: for0<=i<r set

  Lambda_(i,i+1)=2(i+1)^2,
  Lambda_(i+1,i)=2(i+1)(i+2),

and set every other ordered coefficient to zero. Row sum minus column
sum is(-2,...,-2,2r). In the original matrix formula the off-diagonal
entry(i,i+1) is-(i+1)^2; the interior diagonal is i^2+(i+1)^2, the
first diagonal1 and the last diagonal r^2. Thus the full remainder is

  M=sum_(i=0)^(r-1)(i+1)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2 >=0.       (1)

This proves the start in all original coordinates. It imposes no support
restriction on a proposed continuation.

Candidate B uses a terminal unit step to keep the full-curvature product
zero while transferring mass into separated earlier long steps. Its
intended coefficient change would release the terminal row and its full
Schur complement, rather than retain the old primitive-join array:

  hB(t)=(1+t/2,1+29t/20,1+13t/20,1+13t/10,1),
  0<=t<=1, A_B(t)=6+39t/10.                            (2)

The proposed endpoint is(3/2,49/20,33/20,23/10,1), capacity99/10.

Candidate C instead uses six strictly nonunit steps for t>0, a small
interior excess, unequal neighboring middle steps, and a free terminal
step. It would require simultaneous selection of the original full array,
without the terminal-unit Schur specialization:

  hC(t)=(1+11t/20,1+33t/20,1+t/100,
                              1+19t/20,1+31t/20,1+3t/10),
  0<=t<=1, A_C(t)=7+501t/100.                          (3)

Its proposed endpoint is(31/20,53/20,101/100,39/20,51/20,13/10),
capacity1201/100. Neither endpoint is one of the eight old frozen words.
These are proposed physical continuations from(1), NOT assertions that
all their intermediate words have feasible certificates.

Both endpoints pass the original unit-quadratic screen
A product|1-h_i|<=1. For B the product is zero. For C the excesses
are(11/20,33/20,1/100,19/20,31/20,3/10); their product is below1/25
and A<13, giving A product<13/25<1.

They also pass every original scalar cut. At index i write
K_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j and D_i=1+sum_(j>i)h_j. The positive slacks
K_i D_i-A(h_i-1) at the respective endpoints are

  B: (69/20,13/25,297/20,33/100,89/10),
  C: (7709/2000,99/1000,352399/10000,
                               18709/1000,61/400,7107/1000).     (4)

For completeness the cut follows from the projection interval with
constant gradient1 before the cut and gradient-(h_i-1)/D_i after it,
initial point K_i and terminal point equal to that last gradient.
The energy identity below gives V=K_i^2-A^2(h_i-1)^2/D_i^2. Thus a
negative slack would be an actual scalar obstruction. Passage of(4)
is only a necessary test. A stronger actual triangle defeats both words.

## One exact static-function construction for both early screens

Consider a word(H,b,m_1,...,m_k,c,ell), k=1 or2. Put

  B=b-1, J=1+sum m_i, C=sum m_i+c, D=1+ell, K=1+H.

The local C here is a sum of physical steps, not the benchmark C_n.
Let p=(1,0), choose a noncollinear q with

  p.q=-u, ||q||^2=N,
  w=-(B p+C q)/D.                                     (5)

All B,C,D in the applications are positive. Hence0 lies strictly inside
the triangle T=conv{p,q,w}. Define one static function for this whole word

  F(x)=max_(v in T)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).                  (6)

Completing the square gives its unique maximizing v=Proj_T(x). Projection
normal inequalities imply
||Proj_T(x)-Proj_T(y)||^2<=<Proj_T(x)-Proj_T(y),x-y>, so projection is
1-Lipschitz. The two maximizing inequalities for F(x+z) and F(x) bound
F(x+z)-F(x)-<Proj_T(x),z> between0 and||z||^2. Therefore F is convex,
differentiable and1-smooth, with gradient Proj_T. It has unique minimizer0:
Proj_T(x)=0 implies <x,v><=0 for all v in T, and0 is interior.

Start x_0=Kp. The intended gradients are p,p, then q on every middle
query through the second long step, then w,w. The actual positions are

  x_1=p, x_2=-B p,
  x_j=-B p-(sum of preceding middle steps)q,
  x_(r-1)=D w, x_r=w.

At the p queries the projection test reduces to p.(q-p)<=0 and
p.(w-p)<=0. At q the normal is -B p-z q, where1<=z<=J. Its two required
dot products, tested at z=1,J because they are affine in z, are

  P_z=-B(1+u)+z(u+N),
  N_z=B^2-B(C+D+z)u+z(C+D)N.                          (7)

The second actual dot product is N_z/D. At the two w queries it suffices
that w.p<=||w||^2 and w.q<=||w||^2; their normals are ell w and0.
These tests check every triangle vertex at every actual query. They
therefore imply ALL ordered interpolation inequalities, including remote
and optimum rows: with f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

  Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.                          (8)

Direct GD telescoping gives the original primitive energy

  V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_r+s g_r||^2=K^2-A^2||w||^2.      (9)

Indeed substituting f_i into the definition of V_h leaves
sum_i(2h_i<x_i,g_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2)-2s<x_r,g_r>-s^2||g_r||^2,
and the sum telescopes to ||x_0||^2-||x_r||^2.
A negative value of(9) excludes every original full nonnegative array
and arbitrary full PSD remainder, by fact04219554e41d9750's identity.

## Candidate B: a whole terminal-unit segment is excluded below Gamma5

Use99/100<=t<=1 and q=(-2/5,sqrt(2)/10), so u=2/5,N=9/50. Then

  B=29t/20, J=2+13t/20, C=2+39t/20, D=2, K=2+t/2,
  w=((80-67t)/200,-sqrt(2)(40+39t)/400).               (10)

The four quantities in(7), in order P_1,P_J,N_1,N_J, are

  29/50-203t/100,
  29/25-1653t/1000,
  18/25-2549t/1000+1943t^2/2000,
  36/25-231t/100+16453t^2/20000.                       (11)

All are negative on the stated interval. For the last two, use t>=99/100
in the negative term and t^2<=1 in the positive term to bound them by
-83201/100000 and-97/4000 respectively. The first two are already
negative at99/100 and decrease.

The remaining normals are also strict: w.p<7/100, while
||w||^2>1521/20000>7/100, using40+39t>78. Furthermore
w.q=(-360+229t)/2000<0. Finally p.q<1 and p.w<1.
Thus(6) is an actual static function for the entire physical trajectory.

Since K<=5/2, A_B(t)>49/5 and ||w||^2>1521/20000, equation(9) gives

  V_h<25/4-(49/5)^2(1521/20000)
      =-526921/500000<-1.                              (12)

This excludes every t in[99/100,1], not only the endpoint. The lower
end has capacity9861/1000, strictly below Gamma5, since

  Gamma5_c107-9861/1000=29641/806000>0.

Because A_B(1)=99/10>Gamma5, every above-Gamma5 point of the proposed
interval(2) is already in this excluded final segment. No coefficient
continuation could reach its proposed sufficient-capacity endpoint.

## Candidate C: a six-step segment is excluded below Gamma6

Use999/1000<=t<=1 and q=(-3/10,1/10), so u=3/10,N=1/10. Then

  B=33t/20, J=3+24t/25, C=3+251t/100,
  D=2+3t/10, K=2+11t/20,
  w=((900-897t)/1000,-(300+251t)/1000)/D.              (13)

The four quantities in(7) are now

  2/5-429t/200,
  6/5-1761t/1000,
  1/2-2689t/1000+26631t^2/20000,
  3/2-2637t/1000+112611t^2/100000.                     (14)

The last two are at most-854761/1000000 and-8253/1000000 using
t>=999/1000 and t^2<=1. The first two are negative and decreasing.
All intermediate q queries are covered by the affine-in-z check in(7).

For the w normals,0<w.p<1/500. The magnitude of its second coordinate
exceeds11/46, since its numerator exceeds11/20 and D<=23/10. Thus
||w||^2>121/2116>1/18>1/500. Also w.q<0, because w has positive first
coordinate and negative second coordinate. The p normals are strict.
This completes all original interpolation checks via(6)--(8).

Here K^2<7, A_C(t)>12 and ||w||^2>1/18, so

  V_h<7-12^2/18=-1.                                   (15)

The lower segment capacity is1200499/100000. It is below the accepted
threshold because

  Gamma6_c107-2401/200=112799/32240000>0,
  2401/200-1200499/100000=1/100000>0.

Since A_C(1)=1201/100>Gamma6, every above-Gamma6 point of(3) lies in
the excluded segment[999/1000,1]. At t=1 the witness is especially
simple: w=(3,-551)/2300, x_0=(51/20,0).

## Verdict under the actually consumed correction

Both new candidates have explicit actual feasible starts and proposed
sufficient-capacity endpoints that survive the cheaper original screens.
Both fail a complete actual-function screen on a parameter interval
beginning BELOW their respective thresholds. Full multiplier integration
was therefore not pursued for either candidate. No interior feasibility
claim, new fact, or original-target progress is asserted.

The concrete difficulty exposed here is the same retained two-transition
geometry: redistributing mass around an exact terminal unit or a small
interior excess leaves the p,p,q,...,q,w,w projection trajectory legal,
with its terminal norm too large. A viable different word must break at
least one of these explicit normal/payoff conditions before reaching the
threshold. No surviving such deformation was constructed in this round.

Both extra candidates allowed by63aeee04d056a192 have now been screened.
No further round or resource grant is inferred. Numerical/evaluator,
solver, machine-algebra, DP/replay, formal/fact_submit, consult,
installation and native-subagent calls all remain zero.

# Max48: motion on faces at the original queries

Status: complete joint analytical DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact submission is
authorized in this launch. This is round48, the first of two rounds after
completed47; it does not renew the allocation. The original all-horizon,
all-nonnegative-word exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

## 1. Scope, accepted inputs, and the changed law

The former T1/T2 laws held the gradient constant inside each original H2
half. The law here replaces that assumption by affine projection motion
on an exposed segment: the tangent component changes at every physical
query. A remote support condition obstructs an orthogonal-face version.
An actual triangle then realizes the correct face transition, with every
subsequent physical query given explicitly. The latter construction is
legal for every enclosing integer m>=100, but its entire specified
family is quantitatively weaker than the complete same-word references.

The accepted mathematical inputs are:

* fact1252b8823bb5a290: necessity of every ordered smooth-convex
  interpolation inequality, including the optimum; its proof also gives
  the normalization used here.
* factb98bb807d514e80d: the full K_n reference and the exact all-split
  benchmark estimate B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3).
* fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a: the complete alternating-ray maximum F_ray_n,
  with K_n<=F_ray_n.
* factdfde2fa326c908bf: the FULL schedule-dependent complex cycle
  domain D_h and its payoff Gap_h. We use a legal point in this full
  domain to bound its complete supremum from below.

Accepted e9a55f57461f253c was read in its stated finite scope: actual H6,
epsilon1/6, dimension66, unique minimizer0. Its dimension is not its
radius, and it supplies no changing-enclosing-m child or asymptotic
transfer. The accepted finite triangle62a7d9329121fd75 concerns the
different word(2,2,1); it is not used as an iteration premise here.

Shared frozen diagnostic9a299b9a2f951782 was read without rerunning it.
It reports substantial internal H2 changes and asymmetric directed
slacks, with H6 labeled optimal_inaccurate. Those observations motivate
the changed mechanism only. No floating-point equality, exact rank or
new-m law is inferred. Earlier unverified T1/T2 drafts are not proof
premises; all new calculations used below occur in this joint draft.

The supporting literature search returned Theorem4 of Jai Moondra,
Hassan Mortagy and Swati Gupta, "Reusing Combinatorial Structure: Faster
Iterative Projections over Submodular Base Polytopes," arXiv:2106.11943,
v3(2023). Its general-polytope portion distinguishes tangent feasibility
from the normal-cone condition. The PDF was downloaded, and Section3,
Lemma1, Theorem4 and its proof were read through browser-extracted text
after the local extractor failed. Here both conditions are proved
directly from the three vertices; no external theorem is a proof premise.

## 2. Original words and local notation

Fix an integer m>=100 and use the SAME enclosing parameters in every
child:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m, beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1.

Thus the actual first seven steps are

    (ell,1+beta,ell,1+b,ell,1+beta,ell),
    ell=1+a, b=beta*c.

The following are local scalars, denoted A48,eta48,b48,k48,r48,chi48,
d48 in the shared glossary when necessary:

    A=1+sum H2=4+2a+beta,
    eta=a^2*beta,
    k=A/(b*eta),
    r=sqrt(b(b+1)/(A(k-1))),
    chi=1+(A-1)(b+1)/(b*k+1),
    d=chi-ell.                                             (1)

In particular k is not a horizon, a checkpoint count, or K_n; b is a
bridge EXCESS, not the original benchmark B_n. Define the partial sums
and tangent coefficients of the actual H2 by

    (p0,p1,p2,p3)=(0,ell,ell+1+beta,A-1),
    (t0,t1,t2,t3)=(1,-a,a*beta,-eta).                       (2)

For any integer i>=0, s2(i) is its number of binary1 digits, with
s2(0)=0. For a positive integer j, nu2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing
j. These are combinatorial definitions, not numerical routines run here.

All vectors and inner products below are Euclidean. An exposed-face
normal q means that the relevant face lies in the hyperplane
<q,v>=||q||^2 and the whole convex set lies in its lower halfspace.
The tangent vector w is perpendicular to q. A core reset means x=g
at the stated physical query, with optimum x_star=g_star=f_star=0.

## 3. Uniform bounds on the actual enclosing parameters

All inequalities in this section hold for every m>=100. Squaring positive
rationals gives

    12/5<lambda<29/12,
    99/100<=beta<1,
    12/5<c<(29/12)(101/100)=2929/1200<5/2.

Consequently

    2/5<a<5/12,
    19/8<b=lambda(1-epsilon^2)<5/2,
    23/4<A<6,
    99/625<eta<25/144,
    b*eta=beta^3*a<5/12.                                  (3)

For the lower a bound, (99/100)/(2929/1200)=1188/2929>2/5.
For the lower b bound, (12/5)(9999/10000)>19/8.
The other bounds follow by substitution; for example
A>4+4/5+99/100=579/100>23/4. In particular k>A and k>1.

Using b*k=A/eta rewrites chi as

    chi=1+eta(b+1)(A-1)/(A+eta).                           (4)

The middle ratio satisfies

    4/5<(A-1)/(A+eta)<5/6.

The first inequality is equivalent to A>5+4eta, which follows from
A>23/4 and 5+4eta<5+25/36<23/4. For the second,
(A-1)/(A+eta)<(A-1)/A<5/6 because A<6. Thus

    1+2673/6250<chi<1+875/1728,
    0<d<1/8, chi>17/12>sqrt(2).                           (5)

For positivity, 2673/6250>5/12 and ell<17/12. For the upper bound,
ell>7/5 and 875/1728<21/40, so chi-ell<1/8.
These estimates use rational inequalities on the actual parameters,
not continuity from a limiting word.

## 4. Exact moving-face H2 block and its remote obstruction

Suppose q is a face normal, w perpendicular to q, and the four proposed
projections q+t_i w all lie in that face. Put

    x_i=(A-p_i)q+t_i w,
    g_i=q+t_i w,
    y_i=x_i-g_i=(A-1-p_i)q,       0<=i<=3.                 (6)

Since t_(i+1)=(1-h_i)t_i for the three actual H2 steps, subtracting
h_i*g_i in(6) gives x_(i+1). The gradient changes at every one of these
steps whenever w!=0. Every normal reserve A-1-p_i is nonnegative, and
y3=0. Thus, if the full support conditions hold, these are actual
projections throughout the block, with the final core reset

    x3=g3=q-eta*w.                                        (7)

This replaces constant gradients by tangent reflection and contraction;
it does not replace H2 by one fictitious large step.

Now consider TWO such consecutive original H2 blocks, with respective
normal/tangent pairs (q0,w0) and(q1,w1), each ending in a core reset.
The intervening original step is1+b, so exact GD requires

    -b(q0-eta*w0)=A*q1+w1,  <qj,wj>=0.                   (8)

The putative gradients at the original indices0 and4 are q0+w0 and
q1+w1. The residual at4 is (A-1)q1. Dotting(8) with q1 gives

    <q1,w0>=A||q1||^2/(b*eta)+<q1,q0>/eta.

Hence the ORIGINAL directed interpolation residual for the natural
projection values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 is

 Q_(0,4)=(A-1)[(1+1/eta)<q0,q1>
                         +(A/(b*eta)-1)||q1||^2].        (9)

To check the convention, direct expansion of these values gives
Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, where Q_ij is exactly the signed residual in
accepted1252b8823bb5a290.

If q0 and q1 are orthogonal and q1!=0, then

    Q_(0,4)=(A-1)(k-1)||q1||^2>0.                        (10)

Thus this full-dimensional orthogonal-normal family fails a retained
original row. Tangent directions and additional dimensions are arbitrary.
No bound on their norms repairs(10). The zero q1 exception is a quadratic
core block, rather than a nonzero exposed-face normal.

More generally a necessary condition is

    <q0,q1><=-(A-b*eta)/(b(1+eta))*||q1||^2
             <-(7/4)||q1||^2,       q1!=0.               (11)

Indeed A-b*eta>23/4-5/12=16/3 and
b(1+eta)<(5/2)(1+25/144)=845/288. Their ratio exceeds
1536/845>7/4. This is a necessary condition on two particular
core-reset blocks separated by the low bridge; it is not a general
angle obstruction for arbitrary projection trajectories.

Arbitrary function values do not repair this particular geometry when
both core resets and the stated optimum are retained. At a core sample
x=g, the two optimum inequalities in1252b8823bb5a290 force
f=||g||^2/2. For any two samples within one block, (6) gives

    <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j>=||g_i-g_j||^2.

Thus Q_ij+Q_ji=0 independently of the values. If both inequalities are
nonpositive, both are equalities. Their differences, together with the
value forced at the block's core sample, uniquely determine
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 at every sample of that block. Apply this to
the blocks ending at3 and7. Formula(9) is therefore forced for EVERY
value assignment compatible with all interpolation inequalities.

The obstruction is not an adjacent-row failure. With these forced values,
every ordered pair within either individual block has Q=0. Across the
bridge, Q_(4,3)=0 and

    Q_(3,4)=-(A-1)(A/b+1)||q1||^2<0.

All optimum rows have Q_(i,star)=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-(A-1-p_i)||qj||^2<=0 in block j. Thus adjacent and optimum
rows all pass even though(10) is positive.

## 5. One actual triangle, with its actual first face transition

The illegal orthogonal second face suggests the following single static
convex set, for which we follow the TRUE projection instead. In the plane
let

    U=(1,k*r), V=(1,-a*k*r), L=(-b,r),
    C48=conv{U,V,L},
    F48(x)=max_(v in C48)[<x,v>-||v||^2/2],
    X0=(A,k*r).                                          (12)

All three vertices and the starting point are finite explicit vectors
depending only on m. Here L is a vertex label; the smoothness constant
is1 until the final scaling paragraph. The defining maximum is one
fixed function, not a Gram feasibility oracle.

The triangle is the intersection of the three halfspaces

    x <=1,
    -(k-1)r*x+(b+1)y <=(b*k+1)r,
    -(a*k+1)r*x-(b+1)y <=(a*b*k-1)r.                    (13)

The first line is tight at U,V, the second at U,L and the third at V,L.
The third right side is positive: a*b*k=A/(a*beta)>69/5>1 by(3).
All three inequalities are strict at0, so0 is an interior point of C48.

For completeness, a maximizer defining F48 exists by compactness and
is unique by strict concavity. It is the Euclidean projection P_C48(x),
since the objective is ||x||^2/2-||x-v||^2/2. The projection condition is

    <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in C48.                     (14)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along each segment
from g to v; sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2. Applying(14)
at x and x' and adding gives

    <x-x',P_C48(x)-P_C48(x')>>=||P_C48(x)-P_C48(x')||^2.

Cauchy--Schwarz proves the projection is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two
maximizing objectives gives

    0<=F48(x+z)-F48(x)-<P_C48(x),z><=||z||^2.

This proves differentiability with gradient P_C48, as well as convexity
and global1-smoothness. Since0 belongs to the triangle, F48>=0 and
F48(0)=0. If x!=0, an adequately small positive multiple of x belongs
to the triangle's interior and makes <x,v>-||v||^2/2 positive. Therefore

    argmin F48={0}, min F48=0,
    dist(X0,argmin F48)^2=A^2+k^2*r^2.                   (15)

For i=0,1,2,3 define

    Xi=(A-p_i,t_i*k*r), Gi=(1,t_i*k*r).                 (16)

Each Gi lies in the vertical segment UV: the four t_i lie between
-a and1, since eta=a^2*beta<a and 0<a*beta<1. Also

    Xi-Gi=(A-1-p_i)(1,0).

The first halfspace in(13) proves(14) for every vertex and hence every
point of C48. Thus all four Gi are the ACTUAL projections. The three
individual GD updates follow from(2), and the true values are

    F48(Xi)=||Gi||^2/2+(A-1-p_i), 0<=i<=3.              (17)

In particular X3=G3=(1,-A*r/b). The original fourth step1+b gives

    X4=-b*X3=(-b,A*r).                                  (18)

The orthogonal-face guess would prescribe G4=L=(-b,r), with residual
(0,(A-1)r). The old gradient U invalidates its normal condition by

    <U-L,X4-L>=(A-1)(k-1)r^2>0.                        (19)

This is the concrete instance of(10), at the actual physical indices0,4.
We do not use L as the true gradient at4.

Let n_plus=(-(k-1)r,b+1), and D_plus=(b*k+1)r. The aspect ratio in(1)
makes X4 a positive multiple of n_plus. Moreover

    <n_plus,X4>/D_plus
      =[b(k-1)+A(b+1)]/(b*k+1)=chi.

The perpendicular foot on the upper supporting line is therefore

    q=X4/chi.                                           (20)

It lies strictly inside segment LU. Indeed the orthogonal projection
parameter of X4 on its line, measured from L toward U, is

    theta=(A-1)(k-1)r^2/[(b+1)^2+(k-1)^2*r^2],
    0<theta<(A-1)/(k-1)<1.                              (21)

Its residual is a positive multiple of n_plus, so the second halfspace
in(13) proves(14) against the WHOLE triangle, including the remote V.
Thus G4=q is the actual gradient, and

    Y4=X4-G4=(chi-1)q,
    F48(X4)=(chi-1/2)||q||^2.                            (22)

This is a real transition from the vertical exposed segment to the
sloping exposed segment. The two first gradients U,V are noncollinear;
the law has not become a constant-H2 transcript in different notation.

## 6. The exact full trajectory after the transition

The next physical step is h4=ell, and(5),(20) give

    X5=X4-ell*G4=d*q, 0<d<1/8.                         (23)

We prove a sufficiently large segment of the line through q lies inside
the triangle. The point q itself belongs to C48. The point -q/2 also
belongs to C48, by all three original halfspaces(13). For the right
halfspace its horizontal coordinate is

    b/(2chi)<(5/2)/(14/5)=25/28<1.

For the upper halfspace its inner product with n_plus is -D_plus/2.
For the lower halfspace its left side is

    [A(b+1)-b(a*k+1)]r/(2chi)
        <=A(b+1)r/(2chi)<15r/2,

while the right side is (a*b*k-1)r>64r/5. This proves

    [-q/2,q] subset C48.                                 (24)

We retain every physical step in the remaining word. The defining ruler
recursion gives, for 0<=t<n,

    h_t=1+beta*c^(nu2(t+1)-1).

One way to verify the identity is induction on H_j: the center position
t+1=2^j has valuation j, and adding2^j to each right-child position
does not change its smaller valuation. For i>=0,

    sum_(j=1)^i nu2(j)=sum_(ell>=1)floor(i/2^ell)=i-s2(i).

The second equality follows by writing i=sum_l b_l*2^l, with b_l in
{0,1}, and summing b_l*(2^l-1). Therefore the exact physical prefix
product is

    P_i:=product_(t<i)(1-h_t)=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)).    (25)

In particular P5=-beta^5*c^(-2), and all these products are nonzero.
For every 5<=i<=n set

    Ti=(-1)^(i-5)*d*beta^(i-5)*c^(2-s2(i)),
    Xi=Gi=Ti*q, F48(Xi)=Ti^2||q||^2/2.                   (26)

For i>=5, s2(i)>=1 and beta^(i-5)<=1, so

    |Ti|<=d*c<5/16<1/2.

Equation(24) proves Xi lies in C48. Its projection is consequently
itself, giving exactly the gradient and value in(26). Equation(25)
then proves Xi+1=(1-h_i)Xi for every i>=5. This establishes(26) by
induction starting from(23); it is not a hypothetical quadratic tail.

All physical queries inside every later H2 block have changing nonzero
gradients: h_i>1 makes the scalar in(26) change sign at each step, and
no factor vanishes. In the first H2, changes arise from tangent motion
on the face; in the later blocks, they arise inside the triangle's
quadratic core. Both coordinate gradients continue changing. Dimension
is exactly2 and is not used as a substitute for the actual radius(15).

## 7. All remote and optimum interpolation rows

Set X_star=G_star=0 and f_star=0, and at every finite index use the
single actual value f_i=<Xi,Gi>-||Gi||^2/2 established in(17),(22),(26).
Direct expansion yields

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<Gi,Xj-Xi>+||Gi-Gj||^2/2
        =<Gi-Gj,Yj>,       Yj=Xj-Gj.                    (27)

Every finite Gi is in the fixed triangle: (16) handles0 through3,
(20)--(21) handle4, and(24)--(26) handle the full remaining word.
For j<=3, Yj is a nonnegative horizontal normal, so the first
halfspace(13) makes(27) nonpositive for EVERY i. For j=4, Y4 is a
positive multiple of n_plus, so the second halfspace does the same.
For j>=5, Yj=0 and every residual with this target index is zero. The optimum gradient0
also lies in the triangle; Y_star=0. Thus both optimum directions and
every remote pair, including both terminal directions, are covered
simultaneously by the same data. No directed row has been dropped.

## 8. Exact terminal gap, true cost, and complete comparisons

Since n=2^m-1, s2(n)=m and n-5 is even. Equations(15),(26) give the
exact true-distance payoff of this actual function:

 V48(m):=2(F48(Xn)-min F48)/dist(X0,argmin F48)^2
       =d^2 beta^(2(n-5)) c^(4-2m)
          *(b^2+A^2*r^2)/[chi^2(A^2+k^2*r^2)].          (28)

Let Qunit(m)=P_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m), the exact payoff of the scalar
unit quadratic on the SAME original word with initial distance1.
Then

    V48(m)/Qunit(m)=d^2*c^4*beta^(-10)*||q||^2/||X0||^2.

Here

    ||q||^2/||X0||^2
      =(b^2+A^2*r^2)/[chi^2(A^2+k^2*r^2)]<1/8.          (29)

To prove(29), its numerator-to-denominator ratio before chi^2 is a
weighted average of (b/A)^2 and(A/k)^2. Both are less than1/4:
b/A<10/23<1/2, and A/k=b*eta<5/12<1/2. Also chi^2>2 by(5).
Bernoulli's inequality, proved by induction from(1-u)^(j+1)
>=(1-ju)(1-u)>=1-(j+1)u whenever its induction lower bound is used,
gives beta^10>=(1-10/m)>=9/10. Consequently

    V48(m)/Qunit(m)
       <(1/64)(625/16)(10/9)(1/8)
       =6250/73728<1/10.                                (30)

All quantities in(28) are positive. This closes the specified balanced
triangle family as a source of improvement, with an absolute margin.

For comparison with the complete K and ray references, put
S=sum H_m and M=1+S. Summing the actual multiplicities gives

    M=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)
     =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta*c^m,
    zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).                          (31)

The last inclusion follows from c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1.
Thus M<c^m. It follows that

    (2S+1)*Qunit(m)<2c^m*c^(-2m)=2c^(-m)<1.

The full reference of factb98bb807d514e80d is

 K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum h),
            max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2
                                      *(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]}.

It includes the canonical Huber term. Fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a takes the
maximum over ALL feasible alternating-ray checkpoint sets, including
zero and one cut, so its complete F_ray_n is at least this full K_n.
Therefore, on exactly this original h and physical n,

    0<V48(m)<Qunit(m)/10
             <1/[10(2S+1)]<=K_n(h)/10<=F_ray_n(h)/10.   (32)

This comparison does not replace either full reference by a chosen cut;
using a lower bound on each complete reference is enough to prove the
displayed domination.

For the full complex channel of factdfde2fa326c908bf, take z=1. Its
cycle quantities satisfy alpha-|z|^2=0 and Im(z)=0, so EVERY cycle
inequality defining the actual D_h holds. Both accepted payoff bounds
coincide, and hence

    Gap_h(1)=|P_n|^2=Qunit(m),
    0<V48(m)<(1/10)*sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z).              (33)

This is the FULL unaugmented complex reference, not just the universal
lens or a short-cycle restriction. The Huber-augmented lens is also
larger than V48(m), by(32), without needing any lens optimization.

Finally use the original all-split benchmark, without substituting a
balanced approximation. Accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives, for p=log_2 3,

    B_n<=1+4n^p<5*3^m.

Combining with(30), beta<1 and c>12/5 proves

    0<B_n*V48(m)<(1/2)(3/c^2)^m
                       <(1/2)(25/48)^m ->0.            (34)

The equality of actual and chosen minimizer distances is already proved
in(15). For an arbitrary positive smoothness constant L_s, replace F48
by L_s*F48 and use physical steps h_i/L_s. The positions, true radius
and normalized payoff stay exactly(16),(18),(26),(28). This preserves
the project's W=2R convention. Equation(34) is a bound on this explicit
family's payoff only, not an unrestricted upper bound on W_n(h).

## 9. What the balanced choice closes, and what it leaves open

There are two distinct scoped conclusions in this joint draft.

First, ANY two fixed-face H2 blocks with core resets, the intervening
low original bridge and orthogonal nonzero normals fail the original
row(0,4). All adjacent and optimum inequalities passing does not fix
that defect. Equation(11) gives the stronger necessary correlation.
No failure is asserted for arbitrary oblique normals, intermediate
face changes, noncore endpoints, or other conjugate height laws.

Second, the explicit balanced-aspect triangle(12) is globally legal
but its true normalized payoff is less than one tenth of each complete
same-word reference in(32)--(33), and its original-B ratio tends to0.
This conclusion covers exactly the aspect ratio r in(1), arbitrary
positive spatial scaling and orthogonal embeddings of that instance,
and the objective scaling just described. It is not a claim about ALL
triangles, polytopes, or functions with changing gradients.

One useful exact interface survives if the aspect r>0 in(12) is released.
Keep A,b,eta,k fixed, and define the same triangle and X0. All first-block
formulas(16)--(19) remain valid. At4 the true projection is

    g4=L+theta(U-L),
    theta=(A-1)(k-1)r^2/[(b+1)^2+(k-1)^2*r^2].           (35)

This holds for every r>0: 0<theta<1; the residual is perpendicular
to UL, and its dot product with n_plus has the positive sign because
<n_plus,X4>-D_plus=(A-1)(b+1)r>0. Thus the residual is outward and
the remote V also passes. If q is the perpendicular foot of0 on the
upper supporting line and w=g4-q, then

    q perpendicular w, X4=chi*q+w,
    X5=d*q-a*w.                                         (36)

In(36), chi and d are EXACTLY those of(1), independent of the aspect.
The projection at5 cannot lie in the RELATIVE INTERIOR of the old upper
edge: its affine projection would have normal residual(d-1)q, pointing
inward, since d<1/8. A projection in the relative interior of that edge
requires a nonnegative multiple of its outward normal. Equivalently the
first original low step already uses more normal reserve than is
available on this face. This is an explicit normal-cone failure, not
just an inequality on the desired terminal gap. Endpoint projections,
which have larger normal cones, are not excluded by this argument.

For the balanced aspect w=0, (24)--(26) prove persistent core entry.
For a free aspect w need not vanish; the next point may instead hit
another face. No full-word estimate for those true further transitions
is asserted. Such a law must follow the actual projections at queries
inside H2, not hold the current edge for a whole block. This is a
materially different remaining interface from the closed T1/T2 carriers.

The frozen internal-H2 observation has a further qualitative warning:
motion on one fixed face makes BOTH directed Q residuals within that
face zero, as proved in Section4. The saved H6 diagnostics show a
nearly tight forward row and a substantially slack reverse row. Raw
diagnostics are not exact identities, but there is no evidential basis
for imposing a single fixed face throughout each original H2.

An eventual stronger construction on H_m would still leave the original
all-n/all-nonnegative-word lower comparison BLOCKED. That comparison is
a theorem-level requirement, not a routine induction. No such uniform
bridge or unrestricted upper separation is supplied by this draft.

Resource record: zero new host mathematical evaluations, solvers,
trajectories, DP calculations, machine algebra, proof replays, formal
verifier calls, fact_submit calls, consults, installations or subagents.
Only symbolic reasoning, reading/searching, PDF download/browser text
extraction, and persistence were used. Operational errors were
`rg: command not found` and `pdftotext: command not found`; neither was
silently treated as success and neither led to an installation.
