# Max37: exact triangular predecessors of a fixed affine suffix

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact is asserted. This is
the second and last analytical round of the wave with completed ceiling 37
and attempted ceiling 38, including the historical interruption. Numerical,
solver, machine-algebra, replay, formal-verifier, installation, consult and
subagent use in this round: zero. All calculations below are textual.

The original problem remains the all-schedule, all-sufficiently-large-horizon
comparison W_n(h) >= (1-o(1))/B_n, with W=2R. Nothing below establishes that
comparison or supplies an improved algorithm's upper certificate.

Accepted references used only in their stated scopes are:

* 1252b8823bb5a290: full smooth-convex interpolation and the definition of W.
* b98bb807d514e80d: the exact scalar reference K_n and its original B_n.
* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: the complete feasible alternating-ray reference F_ray_n.
* 0be8b54e0a33381c: the universal Huber bound and B_n>=2n+1. Its separate
  finite-terminal-library ConPD theorem is not a converse for this construction.

Family-specific symbols carry the suffix37 to avoid collisions with the
project glossary; letters u,v,A,D and the spelled-out Greek parameters in
local calculations are defined where they are introduced. All new
ingredients are proved jointly here. They are not unverified bricks
imported from earlier drafts. The earlier symmetric capped-quadratic rule
29d5fe71cbb2af40 and its closeout 2d68d0429ffcde3b are strategic context only.

## 1. Concrete family and result

Write X_i,G_i,F_i for the actual normalized GD point, its gradient, and its
function value. Work first with smoothness one and minimum value zero. Let e be a unit vector
in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space. For a compact convex set C_tri37 containing
zero and e, assume the explicit radial-cap condition

    <e,v> <= 1   for every v in C_tri37.                              (1)

Define the ONE STATIC function

    F_tri37(x) = max_{v in C_tri37} { <x,v> - ||v||^2/2 }.                (2)

Fix two preceding physical steps a37,b37>=0 and a finite following word
tail37=(h_tail37_0,...,h_tail37_(m-1)) with h_tail37_j>=0. Put S37=sum h_tail37_j, s_0=0 and
s_j=sum_(k<j)h_tail37_k. The empty following word is allowed. Fix r37>=1+S37.
The suffix jets to be retained are

    X_(2+j)=(r37-s_j)e, G_(2+j)=e,
    F_(2+j)=r37-s_j-1/2,             0<=j<=m.                    (3)

We solve the following DECLARED FAMILY exactly: the initial point p37 belongs
to C_tri37, so G_0=p37 and F_0=||p37||^2/2; the first two actual updates reach r37 e;
and C_tri37 satisfies (1). This is a genuine restriction, not a claimed necessary
form of every smooth convex extension of the suffix. At r37=1,S37=0, condition
(1) remains an explicit family assumption; it cannot be inferred just from
the endpoint jet at e.

Write A_min37(a37,b37,r37) for the smallest possible squared TRUE initial distance
within this family, when it is nonempty. The following table gives its exact
domain and value. The terminal normalized payoff for its optimal member is

    V(a37,b37,r37,S37) = (2r37-2S37-1)/A_min37(a37,b37,r37).                       (4)

The complete domain is as follows.

* If a37=1, the family is empty, for every b37>=0 and every allowed r37.
* If 0<=a37<1 and b37>1, then

      A_min37 = r37^2 / ((1-a37)^2 (b37-1)^2).

* If 0<=a37<1 and 0<=b37<=1, the only nonempty case is a37=b37=0,r37=1
  (therefore S37=0). Then A_min37=1. This includes any all-zero following word.
* If a37>1 and 0<=b37<=1, put alpha=a37-1. Then

      A_min37 = (r37+b37)^2/alpha^2.

* If a37>1 and b37>1, put alpha=a37-1, beta=b37-1, and

      D=b37 beta + r37(beta-1).

  If D<=0, then A_min37=(r37+b37)^2/alpha^2.
  If D>0 and r37<=alpha beta, then A_min37=r37^2/(alpha^2 beta^2).
  If D>0 and r37>alpha beta, then

      A_min37 = 1 + (r37-alpha beta)(r37+b37+alpha)/(alpha^2 beta).    (5)

At D=0 the scalar and appropriate limiting formulas agree. The formulas
r37^2/(alpha^2 beta^2) and (5) both equal one at r37=alpha beta;
this is a boundary of the minimizing branches only when D>=0. No inverse
division by a37-1 or b37-1 is being used to hide their zero cases.

In particular the following explicit triangular rule is legal, whether or
not it is radius-optimal:

    a37>1, b37>1, r37>=max(1+S37,alpha beta),
    T37^2=(r37-alpha beta)(r37+b37+alpha)/(alpha^2 beta),
    p37=e+T37 w,  q37=-(r37 e+alpha p37)/b37,
    C_tri37=conv{0,p37,q37,e},                                          (6)

where w is a unit vector perpendicular to e and T37 is the nonnegative square
root. Thus there is a concrete nonempty domain for every a37,b37>1 and every
finite nonnegative following word. Its true initial squared distance is
1+T37^2. When T37>0, zero is in the interior of the triangle conv{p37,q37,e}, so the
minimum is unique in the displayed two-dimensional span, which is the
ambient space used in this construction. No dimension, offset or minimum
change is unpriced.

## 2. Direct static realization, minimum and all directed rows

For a compact convex C_tri37 the maximizer in (2) is its unique Euclidean
projection P_tri37(x), since the maximand equals
||x||^2/2-||x-v||^2/2. The projection is characterized by

    <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in C_tri37,       g=P_tri37(x).              (7)

For completeness, necessity follows by differentiating squared distance
along g+u(v-g),0<=u<=1, at u=0. Sufficiency follows by expanding
||x-v||^2-||x-g||^2=||v-g||^2-2<x-g,v-g>.
Uniqueness follows from strict convexity of squared distance on C_tri37.

Applying (7) at x and z and adding gives
||P_tri37(x)-P_tri37(z)||^2<=<x-z,P_tri37(x)-P_tri37(z)>, hence the projection is
one-Lipschitz. The two maximizing inequalities in (2) give

    0 <= F_tri37(z)-F_tri37(x)-<P_tri37(x),z-x>
      <= <P_tri37(z)-P_tri37(x),z-x> <= ||z-x||^2.

The last bound proves differentiability, with gradient P_tri37(x). Thus F_tri37 is
finite, convex and one-smooth. Convexity also follows directly from (2).

Its exact minimizer set is the polar cone

    C_tri37^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for all v in C_tri37}.                       (8)

Indeed a polar z gives F_tri37(z)=0 by choosing v=0 and bounding all other
terms. If <z,v>>0 for some v in C_tri37, then sufficiently small positive u has
uv in C_tri37 and u<z,v>-u^2||v||^2/2>0, so F_tri37(z)>0. This proves (8).
For p37 in C_tri37 and z in C_tri37^polar,

    ||p37-z||^2=||p37||^2+||z||^2-2<p37,z> >= ||p37||^2.

Since zero is a minimizer, the TRUE distance from p37 to the minimum set is
exactly ||p37||. This also covers degenerate triangles and nonunique minima.

At any sampled point with gradient g_i=P_tri37(x_i), put y_i=x_i-g_i and
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. In the orientation of accepted 1252b8823bb5a290,

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                     (9)

Therefore (7) proves EVERY ordered interpolation row, including both
directions of each new-old pair. Include x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Equation
(9) covers all its rows as well. No terminal row is omitted here.

Condition (1) implies P_tri37(u e)=e and F_tri37(u e)=u-1/2 for every u>=1:
the vector (u-1)e satisfies (7) at e. Since r37-s_j>=r37-S37>=1,
the complete suffix (3) is an actual GD suffix on (2), including repeated
points at zero steps. Only its total S37 is used; arbitrary large individual
following steps are allowed subject to the displayed r37>=1+S37.

## 3. Proof of the complete predecessor table

Because p37 is in C_tri37, the first update is X_1=(1-a37)p37. Let q37=P_tri37(X_1).
The second actual update is equivalent to

    r37 e=(1-a37)p37-b37 q37.                                          (10)

If a37=1, then X_1=0 and q37=0, so (10) cannot hold with r37>=1.

Suppose 0<=a37<1. Convexity and 0,p37 in C_tri37 imply
q37=(1-a37)p37. Hence r37 e=(1-b37)(1-a37)p37. If b37=1 it is impossible.
If b37<1, p37 is a positive multiple of e; its scalar multiple is
r37/((1-a37)(1-b37)). By (1) this is at most one. Since r37>=1 and both factors
are at most one, it forces r37=1 and a37=b37=0. If b37>1, the unique possible p37 is

    p37=-r37 e/((1-a37)(b37-1)).

The segment C_tri37=conv{p37,e} contains zero, obeys (1), and contains q37=(1-a37)p37.
It realizes (10). This proves all a37<1 cases, with their exact distances.

Now suppose a37>1 and set alpha=a37-1. If b37=0, then X_1=r37 e, so
p37=-r37 e/alpha and q37=e. This realizes and proves the stated value.

For b37>0, equation (10) gives q37=-(r37 e+alpha p37)/b37. Write

    u=<e,p37>, A=||p37||^2, v=<e,q37>=-(r37+alpha u)/b37.

Condition (1) implies u<=1 and v<=1. The projection condition at q37,
tested against e, is

    0 >= <r37 e+(b37-1)q37,e-q37>
      = r37(1-v)+(b37-1)(v-||q37||^2).                            (11)

If 0<b37<1, use ||q37||^2>=v^2. The right side in (11) is at least
(1-v)[r37+(b37-1)v]. Here 1-v>=0 and
r37+(b37-1)v>=r37+b37-1>=b37>0. Thus v=1. Substituting in (11) gives
(1-b37)(||q37||^2-1)<=0, so q37=e. Equation (10) then forces
p37=-(r37+b37)e/alpha. The segment C_tri37=conv{p37,e} realizes these data.
If b37=1, (11) gives v=1, hence u=-(r37+1)/alpha. Thus
A>=u^2=(r37+1)^2/alpha^2, and the same segment with q37=e attains it.
For b37=1, q37 need not equal e for every other feasible member; only the
displayed minimizing member is asserted to have that gradient.

It remains to handle a37>1,b37>1. Set beta=b37-1>0 and define

    u_- = -(r37+b37)/alpha,       u_+ = r37/(alpha beta).

The cap at q37 and p37 gives u in [u_-,1]. Expanding (11) and using
||q37||^2=(r37^2+2r37 alpha u+alpha^2 A)/b37^2 yields the necessary bound

    A >= ell_bound37(u)
      := r37(r37+b37)/(alpha^2 beta)-[b37 beta+r37(beta-1)]u/(alpha beta)
       = (u_-+u_+)u-u_-u_+.                                 (12)

Also A>=u^2, and

    ell_bound37(u)-u^2=-(u-u_-)(u-u_+).                      (13)

This is now a solved one-variable lower bound, not an implicit feasible-set
oracle. If D=b37 beta+r37(beta-1)<=0, ell_bound37 has nonnegative slope.
On [u_-,min(u_+,1)], (13) gives
max(u^2,ell_bound37(u))=ell_bound37(u), whose minimum is at u_-.
If u_+<=1, on [u_+,1] the maximum is u^2, whose minimum is u_+^2;
D<=0 implies u_+>=-u_-, so this cannot improve u_-^2.
Thus A>=u_-^2. The segment with p37=u_-e,q37=e attains the bound.

If D>0, ell_bound37 has negative slope. The same interval argument gives the minimum
at u=min(u_+,1). If r37<=alpha beta, this is u_+. Take
p37=u_+e and q37=-alpha p37; the segment containing q37 and e also contains p37 and
zero. Since X_1=q37 is in C_tri37, its projection is q37, and (10) holds.
The cap holds because u_+<=1. Its distance is exactly u_+^2.

If D>0 and r37>alpha beta, the minimum is ell_bound37(1). Direct factoring gives

    ell_bound37(1)=1+(r37-alpha beta)(r37+b37+alpha)/(alpha^2 beta).

The triangular construction (6), checked next, attains it. At D=0 the two
root magnitudes coincide, so the endpoint formulas agree. This establishes
all necessity and sufficiency assertions in the table, even for larger C_tri37
and in arbitrary finite dimension satisfying the declared assumptions.

## 4. Complete triangular jet check

For (6) put A=1+T37^2 and retain its p37,q37. The actual points are

    X_0=p37, G_0=p37, y_0=0, F_0=A/2;
    X_1=-alpha p37, G_1=q37,
    y_1=(r37 e-alpha beta p37)/b37;
    X_(2+j)=(r37-s_j)e, G_(2+j)=e,
    y_(2+j)=(r37-s_j-1)e.

The first two update equations follow by substitution. Also <e,p37>=1 and
<e,q37>=-(r37+alpha)/b37<1, so the whole C_tri37 satisfies (1). The squared T37 in
(6) is nonnegative on the entire stated domain.

Here is the complete nontrivial normal table at q37:

    <y_1,p37-q37> = -alpha beta(A-1)/b37 <=0,
    <y_1,e-q37> = 0,
    <y_1,0-q37> = -(r37-alpha beta)/b37 <=0.                       (14)

To see the equality in the middle without assuming it, substituting the
formula for A into (12) gives A=ell_bound37(1), which is equality in (11).
Then <y_1,q37>=<y_1,e>=(r37-alpha beta)/b37. The other two identities in (14)
follow from <y_1,p37>=(r37-alpha beta A)/b37. Thus

    F_1=||q37||^2/2+(r37-alpha beta)/b37,
    ||q37||^2=(r37^2+2r37 alpha+alpha^2 A)/b37^2.                    (15)

At a suffix point let z=r37-s_j-1>=0. Its normal products are

    <z e,p37-e>=0,
    <z e,q37-e>=-z(r37+alpha+b37)/b37<=0,
    <z e,0-e>=-z<=0.                                       (16)

At p37, y_0=0, and at the minimum all normal products vanish. Equations
(14)--(16) test every gradient against every sampled normal, so (9) proves
all directed rows. Suffix-to-suffix products are zero. This is stronger
than checking only neighboring gradients or retaining only the old planes.

If T37>0, p37 and e are linearly independent and
alpha p37+b37 q37+r37 e=0 with all three coefficients positive. Consequently zero
lies in the interior of their triangle in span{e,w}, our chosen ambient
plane, and (8) is {0}. If T37=0, the initial
point lies on its one-dimensional span and the exact-distance argument
in Section2 still applies. The true squared initial distance is A, not r37^2.
The terminal objective is r37-S37-1/2>0, proving (4)--(6).

## 5. Preserving arbitrary absolute suffix jets and moving the minimum

The preceding construction does not require resetting the suffix's location
or values. Fix L>0, a scale tau37>0, a physical suffix starting point Z, a unit
direction e, and an absolute starting value V_2. Specify once and for all

    x_(2+j)=Z-tau37 s_j e,
    grad f(x_(2+j))=L tau37 e,
    f(x_(2+j))=V_2-L tau37^2 s_j.                              (17)

For every allowed r37 and its concrete C_tri37, put

    z_star=Z-tau37 r37 e,
    v_star=V_2-L tau37^2(r37-1/2),
    f(x)=v_star+L tau37^2 F_tri37((x-z_star)/tau37).                  (18)

It is one static convex L-smooth function, and all jets (17), including the
terminal, are identical as r37 changes. Its minimum value is exactly v_star.
Its two preceding points are z_star+tau37 p37 and z_star+tau37(1-a37)p37.
The update with physical step h/L is exactly the scaled update by h used
above. Its TRUE initial distance is tau37 sqrt(A), and its terminal gap is
L tau37^2(r37-S37-1/2). Thus its normalized payoff is precisely (4).
Both the movement of the minimum and its level have been recomputed.
For T37>0 in the chosen ambient plane, the minimizer is uniquely z_star.
If the example is embedded in a larger space, or the triangle is degenerate,
z_star is still the closest minimizer to the initial point, so the same
true-distance calculation applies.

## 6. Complete scalar-branch comparisons and a new finite ray improvement

First compare all scalar branches of the radius table on the SAME whole
word (a37,b37,tail37). Write C_S37=2S37+1.

For the branch A=(r37+b37)^2/alpha^2,

    alpha^2(2r37-C_S37)/(r37+b37)^2 <= alpha^2/(C_S37+2b37).              (19)

The inequality follows because
(r37+b37)^2-(C_S37+2b37)(2r37-C_S37)=(r37-C_S37-b37)^2>=0.
The right side is exactly the accepted K term selecting the first step a37.
Its equality choice r37=C_S37+b37 obeys r37>=S37+1. The possible further branch
restriction on r37 can only decrease this scalar family's maximum.

For a37<1,b37>1 put beta=b37-1. The payoff is at most
(1-a37)^2 beta^2/C_S37, since r37^2-C_S37(2r37-C_S37)=(r37-C_S37)^2.
Because (1-a37)^2<=1/(1+a37)^2, this is at most the accepted second-step cut
beta^2/((1+a37)^2 C_S37). This also treats a37=0.

For the branch A=r37^2/(alpha^2 beta^2), with a37>1,b37>1,D>0 and
r37<=alpha beta, normalize the initial distance to one.
The gradients become 1,-alpha,alpha beta/r37 and the exact two-checkpoint
alternating-ray parameters are

    s_0=s_1=0, b_0=a37, b_1=b37, z_term=alpha beta,
    m_old=1, s_2=S37, q_terminal=alpha beta/r37.

The terminal slope is at least one, and at most alpha beta/(1+S37).
All feasibility conditions of accepted 1fca3e5dd555cb4a hold. Its displayed
payoff 2q_terminal z_term-C_S37 q_terminal^2 equals this scalar branch's
payoff, so the full F_ray reference dominates it. The all-zero exception
has payoff one and is also the accepted all-zero value.

The genuinely triangular branch need not be dominated. Take

    a37=b37=2, S37=1, r37=3,
    e=(1,0), p37=(1,2 sqrt(3)), q37=(-2,-sqrt(3)),
    C_tri37=conv{p37,q37,e}.                                          (20)

Zero is interior. On the actual supplied word h=(2,2,1), the full jets are

    i    x_i                  g_i                 f_i
    0    (1,2 sqrt(3))        (1,2 sqrt(3))        13/2
    1    (-1,-2 sqrt(3))      (-2,-sqrt(3))         9/2
    2    (3,0)                (1,0)                 5/2
    3    (2,0)                (1,0)                 3/2.

The unique minimizer is zero, and the TRUE squared initial distance is13.
Its normalized objective is therefore

    V=3/13.                                                 (21)

Here K_3=max{1/11,1/7,1/27,0}=1/7. The FULL F_ray value is also1/7:
the no-cut and one-cut choices give those terms. Selecting both initial
steps2 gives m_0=m_1=1, zero offsets, z_term=1 and terminal old slope1.
The terminal slope interval is [1,1/(1+S37)]=[1,1/2], empty. A selected
step1 cannot be a nonterminal checkpoint, because it makes the next
positive gradient nonpositive. As a last checkpoint after a previous cut,
it has z_term=-O<=0 and returns to a previously visited ray with strictly
positive old slope, again impossible. Selecting only the last step1 gives
zero. These exhaust all checkpoint subsets. Thus

    3/13 > 1/7 = F_ray_3(2,2,1).                            (22)

For every scalar quadratic of curvature kappa in[0,1], on the same word
its payoff is kappa(1-2kappa)^4(1-kappa)^2. Since |1-2kappa|<=1 and
the derivative of kappa(1-kappa)^2 is (1-kappa)(1-3kappa),

    kappa(1-2kappa)^4(1-kappa)^2 <= 4/27 < 3/13.              (23)

An orthogonal sum of such quadratics has a distance-weighted average of
their payoffs, so it is also bounded by4/27. This is a complete comparison
with these applicable references, not just a weak sum-only Huber term.
No comparison to every possible scalar nonlinear hard function is claimed.

The family (2,2,tail37) has an explicit optimized triangular score for every S37.
For a37=b37=2, A=r37^2+2r37-2, and r37>=1+S37. Put

    sigma=(sqrt(13)-3)/2.

Then the exact best score within the triangular family is

    V_tri37(S37)=1/(S37^2+4S37+1),                         0<=S37<=sigma;
    V_tri37(S37)=2/(2S37+3+sqrt(4S37^2+12S37-3)),             S37>=sigma. (24)

To verify it, differentiate (2r37-2S37-1)/(r37^2+2r37-2). The derivative has
the sign of -[r37^2-(2S37+1)r37+1-2S37]. On r37>=S37+1 this bracket is strictly
increasing, since its derivative is at least one. Its value at S37+1 is
1-3S37-S37^2. Thus the maximum is the left endpoint when S37<=sigma;
otherwise it is the unique larger root
r37=(2S37+1+sqrt(4S37^2+12S37-3))/2. At that root the value is1/(r37+1).
This proves (24), including equality at the joining point.
In particular V_tri37(1)=(5-sqrt(13))/6, which is strictly above (21).

If every following step lies in[0,1] and S37>0, the same exhaustive
checkpoint argument gives

    F_ray_(m+2)(2,2,tail37)=K_(m+2)(2,2,tail37)=1/(2S37+5).              (25)

For clarity, a checkpoint smaller than1 cannot be a nonterminal cut;
as a last cut after another checkpoint it violates the strictly positive
old-slope constraint. As the sole checkpoint, a step below1 is infeasible
and a step equal to1 contributes zero. Zero steps cause no
exception. The two initial cuts are infeasible because their terminal
interval is [1,1/(1+S37)]. The two single initial cuts are1/(2S37+5) and
1/[9(2S37+1)]; the first is larger, as is seen from
9(2S37+1)-(2S37+5)=16S37+4>0. It also exceeds the no-cut term1/(2S37+9).

Taking merely r37=2S37+1 in the triangle gives

    (2S37+1)/(4S37^2+8S37+1) > 1/(2S37+5),

since the cross-multiplied difference is4S37+4>0. Thus (24) improves the
entire accepted ray reference on this whole explicitly stated family.
For S37=0 the two-cut ray is feasible and both optimal values are one.
When S37 tends to infinity, 2S37 V_tri37(S37) tends to one, so the relative
improvement over (25) tends to one. This is a finite improvement, not a
new profile constant. Taking tail37 to be m unit steps gives arbitrarily long
examples, but these schedules are not claimed to be near minimax.

Accepted 0be8b54e0a33381c gives B_3>=7, so the example is also above the
original U_3. There is no contradiction: W of a supplied bad word can
exceed the optimum schedule's error. A lower witness on that word is NOT
a minimax disproof, and the finite-library ConPD obstruction is not being
applied outside its hypotheses.

## 7. Exact paid continuation with every old triangle jet retained

Fix the triangle with a37=b37=2 and r37>1, and any following word with
0<=S37<=r37-1. Retain all the old jets, including p37, q37 and the suffix (3).
Let A=r37^2+2r37-2. The old nonzero normals are

    y_1=(r37 e-p37)/2,
    y_(2+j)=(r37-s_j-1)e.

Because y_2=(r37-1)e is nonzero, an added gradient vector z preserves all
old gradient projections and values if and only if it obeys both

    <e,z><=1,
    <y_1,z><=(r37-1)/2.                                      (26)

Here sufficiency is for any enlarged compact convex C_tri37' containing C_tri37 and
only added vertices satisfying (26). Necessity is the old projection
condition (7); it applies whether a proposed added vertex is later queried
or merely included. The initial normal y_0=0 adds no condition. Every old
value remains <x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Since the old triangle contains zero
in its interior, every enlargement still has the SAME unique minimizer0.

Intersect (26) with the line z=t p37. Since

    <e,p37>=1, <y_1,p37>=-(r37-1)(r37+2)/2,

the exact permitted interval is

    -1/(r37+2) <= t <= 1.                                    (27)

Now supply ANY finite preceding word c=(c_0,...,c_(ell-1)) of nonnegative
steps. Restrict the new preceding queries to have gradient equal to their
position, as in the initial member of Section1. This restriction is
explicit: it means each such point lies in C_tri37', and its proximal residual
is zero. The existing first point p37 is the endpoint of this prefix.

There is an exact complete rule, with no optimization oracle:

* No c_j may equal1.
* Define t_ell=1 and, backwards,

      t_j=1 / product_(k=j)^(ell-1)(1-c_k).

* Every t_j,0<=j<ell, must lie in the interval (27).

These conditions are necessary because an actual core query satisfies
X_(j+1)=(1-c_j)X_j, so all new points must be t_j p37; a unit step would
hit zero, which is absorbing. They must also satisfy every retained old
normal, giving (27). They are sufficient by taking

    C_tri37'=conv(C_tri37,{t_j p37:0<=j<ell}).                             (28)

Every new point is in C_tri37', hence has exactly its own position as gradient,
and the recurrence executes the actual preceding word forwards.
Condition (26) preserves EVERY old jet. Formula (9) proves every new-old
row in both directions: new proximal residuals are zero, whereas old
normals tested on new gradients give (26). New-new rows are zero.
The same unique minimum remains0. The TRUE squared initial distance is
t_0^2 A, and the payoff is exactly the old payoff multiplied by

    1/t_0^2=product_(j<ell)(1-c_j)^2.                        (29)

This is a paid continuation invariant for the specified class. It is
scalable in its own domain and is not an arbitrary-word extension theorem.

For one preceding step c>=0, the domain reduces exactly to

    c=0  or  c>=r37+3.                                       (30)

Indeed c<1 has t=1/(1-c)>=1, with equality only at c=0; c=1 is impossible;
and c>1 has t=-1/(c-1), which lies in (27) exactly when c-1>=r37+2.
For c>=r37+3, the distance is sqrt(A)/(c-1), the terminal numerator is
unchanged, and the payoff is multiplied by(c-1)^2. These are actual added
static-function instances with the same old minimum, not free radius resets.

The tempting prepend c=2 fails for every r37>1. Its forced new core point
would be -p37, and its old-normal violation is

    <y_1,-p37-q37>=(r37^2-1)/2>0.                                (31)

For the explicit r37=3 example the violation is4. Thus even though the old
two-step construction itself uses (2,2), that same core rule cannot simply
be prepended once more while retaining all its jets. A nonempty prefix
consisting only of steps2 also fails, since the last t before p37 is -1.

## 8. Exact scope, remaining interface and search provenance

The new finite witness escapes the old symmetric Huber family and improves
the FULL accepted alternating-ray reference, as (20)--(25) prove. The exact
radius table is for the explicitly declared support-projection family with
initial point equal to its gradient and a radial affine suffix. It optimizes
over all convex C_tri37 and all dimensions in that family; it does not optimize
over all functions compatible with (17).

The new continuation invariant pays every earlier distance and retains all
old jets. Its restrictive interval (27) is essential. Allowing a free initial
proximal residual, changing earlier function geometry outside an enlargement
of C_tri37, or choosing a wholly different suffix using the whole word lies outside
this scoped obstruction. Those routes are not declared impossible.

In particular any function (2) started from p37 in C_tri37 is killed by a first step1:
p37-grad F_tri37(p37)=0, an exact minimizer. Thus this entire initial-core family,
not just the selected triangular minimizer, cannot supply a positive lower
witness for every nonnegative word. A step1 later in the optional all-core
prefix is equally fatal. This is a complete, simple all-word obstruction to
THIS invariant; it is not a bound on unrestricted W or on the union of all
other available adversary families. Additional prefix freedom is indispensable
before claiming the original target. No unspecified Bellman oracle or
convexity of a composed suffix objective is used.

Novelty has been checked against shared memory. The older unverified C27
record 1261bd74709219dc already used nonregular projection triangles for a
different primitive-capacity test; projection triangles as a mechanism are
not claimed new. The present output is the solved backward radius table,
its fixed-jet anchor interface, complete same-word ray improvement, and exact
retained-normal continuation rule. The older C37 record ad323c2b7c7c75aa
concerns squared distances and unsaturated cone projection. Here the radial
cap is ACTUALLY active: at X_2=r37 e with r37>1, P_tri37(X_2)=e!=X_2, and the
triangle has full cone equal to the whole plane. No such older result is
being used as a proof premise.

Search procedure: active-program mutation, looking for an exact compact-set
projection/conjugacy construction and paid backward rows. The first Matlas
call returned "TimeoutError: The read operation timed out". A narrower retry
returned arXiv2309.02098 TheoremV.3 and arXiv2111.07720 Lemma5.1; neither
addresses this construction, and neither is imported. A web search located
the primary author page for Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur's interpolation paper.
The exact local downloads/1502.05666.txt, Section2.4, Theorem4 and its proof,
and Corollary1 were read. The projection, minimum and interpolation facts
needed here are reproved in Section2, so no external theorem is used as an
unexpanded black box and no external_refs are claimed.

Original arbitrary-word constant/profile goal: OPEN. New accepted fact IDs:
none. This draft must remain unverified unless main selects a later gate.

# Full H4 endpoint: an explicit cross-sibling pairing and its PSD obstruction

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation82, ended baseline81.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; guidance d3dbd9fb4f8d9159.
One invocation, completed ceiling82/attempted83, controller cap1.
Formal calls and fact_submit are forbidden by the current TASK. All new
ingredients below are derived jointly, not promoted to accepted facts.
The original minimax target and the universal H4 endpoint inequality remain OPEN.

## 1. Verdict and the precise scope of the failed pairing

The two requested cross-sibling projection relations have explicit expansions
in the original physical gradients. Unequal auxiliary denominators matter:
the symmetric auxiliary row sum retains an initial-position coefficient;
its mass-balanced version cancels that coefficient but has an indefinite
auxiliary quadratic. Both ordered rows, and their exact orientations, are
kept in Sections5-6.

We test ONE concrete allocation. Starting from the COMPLETE matched H4
identity, first combine ALL its terms exactly, including the parent bridge,
the full right H3, and every actual remainder. The resulting full polynomial
is written using the four H2 certificates, the three actual primitive-join
row combinations, and the canonical initial-maximizer mixture. This is an
equality, with the two off-root defects still signed; neither H3 primitive
energy is assumed nonnegative.

Extract the physical symmetric sibling normal with weight d_i, and the
mass-balanced auxiliary sibling normal with weight theta=d_i/k. Pay its
auxiliary quadratic by the right child's local bridge-normal pair with the
literal weight tau=theta*h^2/(E*J). All coefficients are positive. This tau
is exactly the LEAST right-only local allocation that makes the auxiliary
principal block positive semidefinite. It yields one explicit square but
leaves a nonzero mixed term (6.8). The WHOLE remainder is negative on the
formal direction (6.10); this is not merely inspection of one coefficient.

More generally, NO tau>=0 repairs this particular additive pairing while
the primitive-row coefficients and canonical initial mixture remain fixed:
below the displayed threshold its auxiliary block is indefinite; at or
above it the common-physical-gradient direction has negative value. The
physical sibling weight may be any nonnegative number in this exclusion.

This is a failure of a fully specified sufficient PSD identity, NOT a
feasible projection trajectory violating H4, NOT a claim that all possible
cross-sibling certificates fail, and NOT a claim about all-d propagation.
Changing initial-maximizer attachments or redistributing the original
primitive rows is outside this failed template. The obstruction identifies
why those attachments cannot remain untouched in this additive approach.

The mandatory original core and inherited cap/cap histories satisfy the
FULL endpoint comparison in the joint derivation below. Their actual right
H3 is retained explicitly. The earlier isolated Xi failures are not reused
as endpoint counterexamples. Since H4 remains unpaid, no second all-depth
mechanism is opened. There is no unpriced recursion or asserted C0/m loss.

## 2. Word, projection model, and elementary identities

Fix one integer m>=1536. All children use this SAME enclosing m:

 epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
 c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
 H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j.

Local masses and steps are

 A=1+a, B=1+beta, t=S2=2A+B, C=1+t,
 k=beta*c, K=beta*c^2, ell=C+k,
 s=S3=2C+k-1=t+ell, C3=1+s=2C+k,
 b=1+K, h=s+b=C3+K, S=S4=2s+b.

The literal H4 word, of length15, is

 (A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A,1+K,A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A).

Let D0 be a fixed nonempty compact convex subset of a finite-dimensional
Euclidean space, containing0. Write P for Euclidean projection onto D0,

 F_D(X)=max_(g in D0)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2),
 U_d(X)=max_(g in D0)(2<X,g>-d||g||^2), d>0.

Projection optimality is <X-P(X),g-P(X)><=0 for every g in D0.
The squared-distance expansion proves sufficiency, and differentiating
along segments proves necessity. Strict convexity proves uniqueness.
Adding the two conditions at X and Y gives

 <X-Y,P(X)-P(Y)>-||P(X)-P(Y)||^2>=0.              (2.1)

In particular P is1-Lipschitz. The maximizer defining F_D is P(X).
The two maximizing inequalities bound its first-order error at displacement
v by ||v||*||P(X+v)-P(X)||. Therefore F_D is convex, differentiable, with
gradient P and Lipschitz constant at most1. Its entire minimum set is the
polar cone D0^polar={X:<X,g><=0 for all g in D0}: this condition is
equivalent to P(X)=0, by projection optimality at0. F_D then equals0.
We retain this whole cone, not a fictitious unique minimizer.

Physical queries obey x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i, g_i=P(x_i), f_i=F_D(x_i).
The terminal g15 is retained and supplies no extra step. Use landmarks

 x=x0, u=x3, z=x4, v=x7, w=x8, uR=x11, zR=x12, y=x15.

Then z=u-(1+k)g3, w=v-b*g7, zR=uR-(1+k)g11.
At the ENDPOINT d=1 put

 J=E_R=1+2t, D=1+2s, M=1+2S=D+2h,
 eta=M-2s=D+2b, E=eta+2t.

Consequently M=E+2ell, D=J+2ell, and

                         E-J=2h>0.             (2.2)

All auxiliary samples use the same D0:

 p0=P(x/M), qL=P(v/eta), rL=P(z/E),
 rO=P(w/D), rR=P(zR/J), q=P(y)=g15.

At any physical or auxiliary sample the ordered interpolation row is

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
     =<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                     (2.3)

The equality follows by substituting f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.
This agrees with accepted1252b8823bb5a290, necessity paragraph1. No
external theorem is needed for (2.1)-(2.3).

For a physical word of mass T, input X and endpoint Y, define

 V=2sum h_i(f_i-f_N)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -T(T+1)||P(Y)||^2.

Telescoping ||x_(i+1)||^2 and substituting the displayed f_i identity gives

                   V=||X||^2-||Y+T P(Y)||^2.    (2.4)

No sign of V is assumed merely from this identity.
For p=P(X/N), N>0, and a convex mixture Z in D0, set

 R(X,N,Z)=N||p-Z||^2+2<X-Np,p-Z>>=0,
 N R(X,N,Z)=||X-NZ||^2-||X-Np||^2.               (2.5)

The two terms in R are individually nonnegative by projection optimality.
For a word of mass T, trailing d>=1, N=d+2T and qY=P(Y/d), its physical
displacement and qY give the convex mixture

 Z=(X-Y+(d+T)qY)/N.

Expansion of (2.4)-(2.5) proves

 U_N(X)-U_d(Y)=[V+L(T,Y,d)]/N+R(X,N,Z),
 L(T,Y,d)=2T<Y,P(Y)-qY>
                       +T^2(||P(Y)||^2-||qY||^2).            (2.6)

For d2>d1>=1, the two projection inequalities for q_j=P(Y/d_j) give

 d1(||q1||^2-||q2||^2)<=2<Y,q1-q2>
                       <=d2(||q1||^2-||q2||^2).

Their combination implies both differences are nonnegative, so L>=0.
At d=1, qY=P(Y), hence L(T,Y,1)=0 exactly.

The original W=2R normalization is unchanged. Projection objectives are
not identified here with residual-gradient affine-height dynamics.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 still requires a full-function-class upper at all
the specified large horizons; no such upper is obtained here.

## 3. Joint H2 and bridge ingredients

The following explicit certificates prevent an unverified H3 report from
being used as a building block. For one step of length l from i to j,
substitution of (2.3) and the physical update gives

 V_l=-2Q_ij-2(1+l)Q_ji+(2-l^2)||g_i-g_j||^2,
 ||g_i||^2-||g_j||^2
   =(2/l-1)||g_i-g_j||^2-(2/l)(Q_ij+Q_ji).       (3.1)

For a general join with left/right capacities Pcap,Ccap and bridge1+e,
0<=e<Ccap, write p for its bridge sample, n for its terminal, and l_j for
the right child's nonterminal steps. Define

 kap=(2Pcap+Ccap+e)/(Ccap-e),
 Rcoef=(Pcap+e)[Pcap*Ccap-e(Pcap+Ccap+e)]/(Ccap-e),
 Ejoin=sum l_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(n,p)+e Q_(p,n)+sum l_j Q_(j,n).

Direct substitution yields the off-root equality

 V_parent=V_left+kap V_right-(kap-1)Ejoin
                         +Rcoef(||g_p||^2-||g_n||^2).       (3.2)

Here is a coefficient check rather than an exact-root invocation. The
value part of Ejoin is
2sum l_j f_j+(e-Ccap)f_p+(2-Ccap-e)f_n; its gradient part is

 sum l_j(1-l_j)||g_j||^2+[(Ccap-e)/2-e^2]||g_p||^2
                                      +(Ccap+e)||g_n||^2/2.

The mixed terms cancel after inserting the actual right displacement.
The three scalar checks are

 (kap-1)(Ccap-e)=2(Pcap+e),
 Pcap^2-kap*e^2=Rcoef,
 kap*Ccap^2-(Pcap+Ccap+e)^2=-Rcoef.

They prove (3.2) by multiplication by Ccap-e. Accepted04219554e41d9750,
Sections10-11, has been read; its exact-root composition is not silently
applied to this perturbed bridge.

For H2=(A,B,A), set C1=1+A=2+a and

 kap1=(3C1+beta)/(C1-beta),
 Delta1=C1^2-beta(2C1+beta),
 R1=(C1+beta)Delta1/(C1-beta), deltaA=2-A^2.

Put u0=sqrt(2)-1. Then a=u0(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon),
2u0+u0^2=1, and

 u0*C1-beta=epsilon(1+epsilon-2u0^2)/(1+epsilon)>0,
 Delta1=(u0*C1-beta)(lambda*C1+beta)>0.

Thus R1>0, deltaA>0, 1<A<sqrt(2), 1<B<2, kap1>1. Combining (3.1)
with (3.2) on the two A leaves and expanding the norm drop along B,A gives

 V_H2=-sum Lambda_ij Q_ij
         +e0||g0-g1||^2+e1||g1-g2||^2+e2||g2-g3||^2,       (3.3)

 e0=deltaA,
 e1=R1(2/B-1),
 e2=kap1*deltaA+R1(2/A-1),

 Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2(1+A),
 Lambda_12=2R1/B,
 Lambda_21=(kap1-1)A+2R1/B,
 Lambda_13=(kap1-1)beta, Lambda_31=kap1-1,
 Lambda_23=2kap1+(kap1-1)A+2R1/A,
 Lambda_32=2kap1(1+A)+2R1/A.

Other multipliers are zero. All the listed coefficients are positive.
The value coefficients collect to(2A,2B,2A,-2t); for the bridge value
use -2A+(kap1-1)(C1-beta)=2B. Equation(3.1) supplies the two extra
norm-drop row pairs, so no independent adjacent-gradient square is omitted.
Denote the row and square portions of (3.3) by C_H2 and P_H2 respectively.
Their translates on indices0..3,4..7,8..11,12..15 are C_ab,P_ab,V_ab,
with ab=00,01,10,11. Each V_ab=C_ab+P_ab>=0 on an actual history.

For completeness the auxiliary bridge formula is derived independently.
Let a left word of mass T end at V0 with A0=P(V0), let C0=1+T,
bridge1+k0 lead to W0=V0-(1+k0)A0, and H=C0+k0. For trailing J0>0,
put N=J0+2H, r=P(W0/J0),

 Np=<V0-A0,A0-r>, Nr=<W0-J0*r,r-A0>,
 Theta=H[C0*J0-k0(C0+J0+k0)]/(J0+k0),
 Bbridge=Theta||A0-r||^2
       +2H(1+C0/(J0+k0))Np+2H*C0/(J0+k0)Nr.      (3.4)

Both normals are nonnegative. With e=A0-r and X0=V0+T*A0,

 Np+Nr=(J0+k0)<A0,e>-J0||e||^2,
 <X0,e>=C0*J0||e||^2/(J0+k0)
                +(1+C0/(J0+k0))Np+C0*Nr/(J0+k0).

Since W0+H*r=X0-H*e, these equations give

 Bbridge=||V0+T*A0||^2-||W0+H*r||^2,
 U_N(X)-U_J0(W0)=(V_left+Bbridge)/N
                    +R(X,N,(X-W0+(J0+H)r)/N).   (3.5)

The mixture has the actual physical prefix weights and extra r weight
J0+H, summing to N. Thus (3.5) retains its actual initial maximizer.

Useful uniform bounds are

 11/2<C<6, 2<k<5/2, K<25/4, C3>13, K<C3/2.      (3.6)

Indeed epsilon<1/100, beta>9/10, lambda>12/5,
lambda<49/20, hence c<5/2, a>1/3 and a<u0<1/2.
These imply the displayed bounds. For a capacity C0>2, k0<C0/2,
and J0>=2C0-1,

 C0*J0-k0(C0+J0+k0)
 > (C0/2)(2C0-1)-3C0^2/4=C0(C0-2)/4>0.         (3.7)

Thus all the Bbridge quantities used below are nonnegative.

## 4. The complete matched identity, and its full recombination

Define B_i=Bbridge(C,k,E;u,g3,rL),
B_o=Bbridge(C3,K,D;v,g7,rO),
B_r=Bbridge(C,k,J;uR,g11,rR), using (3.4).
Introduce the actual mixtures and remainders

 ZO=(x-w+(D+h)rO)/M, RO=R(x,M,ZO),
 ZI=(x-z+(E+ell)rL)/M, RI=R(x,M,ZI),
 ZF=(x-v+(eta+s)qL)/M, RF=R(x,M,ZF),
 ZL=(z-v+(eta+t)qL)/E, RL=R(z,E,ZL),

 RPr=R(w,D,(w-zR+(J+ell)rR)/D),
 RRr=R(zR,J,(zR-y+(1+t)q)/J).

The left H3 identity obtained by adding (3.5) and (2.6) is

 U_M(x)-U_eta(v)
  =V00/M+(V01+L(t,v,eta))/E+B_i/M+RI+RL.

Its full-word version (2.6) also holds, with RF. Substituting that exact
V_left into the outer prefix identity, with both subtracted terms retained,
gives

 Xi=M(RO+RI+RL-RF)-L(s,v,eta)+(M/E)L(t,v,eta),

 U_M(x)-U1(y)=V00/M+V01/E+(B_i+B_o+Xi)/M+G_R,    (4.1)

 G_R=V10/D+V11/J+B_r/D+RPr+RRr.                 (4.2)

Here L(t,y,1)=0, since q=g15. Equations(3.3)-(3.5) explicitly specify
every row, square, gradient and coefficient in (4.1)-(4.2). In particular
the entire actual right H3 remains, with no scalar replacement hypothesis.

Combining the common-p0 remainders using (2.5), and then using (2.6),
eliminates qL exactly and gives

 Xi=||w-(D+h)rO||^2+||z-(E+ell)rL||^2
    -(M/E)||z-E*rL||^2-||x-M*p0||^2
    -||v+s*g7||^2+(M/E)||v+t*g7||^2.             (4.3)

This equation is not assigned a sign. Its negative normal and radial
contributions have been included, not discarded.

Now combine the WHOLE expression (4.1)-(4.2). By (3.5) the first
part is the exact outer prefix gap, and (4.2) is the exact right gap.
Their sum is the left side of (4.1). Applying (2.6) to the full physical
word at d=1 gives the alternative complete equality

 M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]=V4+M R(x,M,Z_*),
 Z_*=[sum_(i=0)^14 h_i*g_i+(1+S)q]/M.           (4.4)

This is a recombination of all reserves, NOT the assumption V_left>=0.
In particular no fraction of B_o or G_R has been left outside the payment.
The internal auxiliary terms in (4.1)-(4.2) cancel only after their full
squares and normals are combined. They will be reintroduced as the actual
cross-sibling constraints in Section5.

To turn (4.4) into a coefficient-level test, retain both off-root defects.
Set

 kap=(3C+k)/(C-k), kapO=(3C3+K)/(C3-K),
 d_i=(C+k)[k(2C+k)-C^2]/(C-k),
 d_o=(C3+K)[K(2C3+K)-C3^2]/(C3-K).              (4.5)

Both are positive. To check the first sign without an approximation, use

 k-u0*C=epsilon[u0+4u0^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda*epsilon]>0.

The bracket is positive from u0>2/5, lambda<5/2, epsilon<1/8.
Since u0 solves u0(2+u0)=1, k/C>u0 implies k(2C+k)>C^2.
For the outer sign, the increasing map r -> c*r/(2+r), c>lambda,
sends r>u0 to a number greater than lambda*u0/(2+u0)=u0.
But K/C3=c*(k/C)/(2+k/C), proving the claim. The denominators in
(4.5) are positive by (3.6).

Let E_i,E_r,E_o denote the Ejoin in (3.2) for the actual joins at3,11,7:

 E_i=sum_(j=4)^6 h_j Q_(j,3)+Q_(7,3)+k Q_(3,7)
                                      +sum_(j=4)^6 h_j Q_(j,7),
 E_r=sum_(j=12)^14 h_j Q_(j,11)+Q_(15,11)+k Q_(11,15)
                                      +sum_(j=12)^14 h_j Q_(j,15),
 E_o=sum_(j=8)^14 h_j Q_(j,7)+Q_(15,7)+K Q_(7,15)
                                      +sum_(j=8)^14 h_j Q_(j,15).

Applying the jointly derived (3.2) at these three actual nodes gives

 C_rows=C00+kap*C01+kapO*C10+kapO*kap*C11
             -(kap-1)E_i-kapO*(kap-1)E_r-(kapO-1)E_o>=0,
 P_sq=P00+kap*P01+kapO*P10+kapO*kap*P11>=0,

 V4=C_rows+P_sq
       -d_i||g3||^2+(d_i-d_o)||g7||^2
       -kapO*d_i||g11||^2+(kapO*d_i+d_o)||q||^2. (4.6)

All inequalities on C_rows,P_sq refer to actual histories; the equality
is algebraic. Four physical H2 blocks and all three joins are present.
Write

 W_*=M*p0-sum_(i=0)^14 h_i*g_i-(1+S)q,
 N_*=2<x-M*p0,W_*>
    =2sum h_i<x-M*p0,p0-g_i>
                     +2(1+S)<x-M*p0,p0-q>>=0,

 Qbase=||W_*||^2+P_sq
       -d_i||g3||^2+(d_i-d_o)||g7||^2
       -kapO*d_i||g11||^2+(kapO*d_i+d_o)||q||^2. (4.7)

The exact full endpoint identity is therefore

          M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]=C_rows+N_*+Qbase.       (4.8)

N_* is the complete canonical initial-maximizer normal, and W_* is its
actual mixture square. Their nonnegative physical weights sum to M.
No sign is asserted for Qbase. Equations(4.1)-(4.8) jointly retain and
account for every term of the source identity before the new pairing.

## 5. Both original cross-sibling relations, fully expanded

For readability set A3=g3, A7=g7, A11=g11, and use physical displacements

 L01=A*g4+B*g5+A*g6=z-v,
 L10=A*g8+B*g9+A*g10=w-uR.

Thus

 Udiff=u-uR=(1+k)A3+L01+b*A7+L10,
 Zdiff=z-zR=L01+b*A7+L10+(1+k)A11.              (5.1)

The physical symmetric normal in the TASK is exactly

 Ng=-Q_(11,3)-Q_(3,11)
   =<u-uR,A3-A11>-||A3-A11||^2
   =k||A3||^2+(1-k)<A3,A11>-||A11||^2
                          +<L01+b*A7+L10,A3-A11>>=0.        (5.2)

Each ordered row is separately present; no extra physical step was added.

For the auxiliary relation, let

 NL=<z-E*rL,rL-rR>=-E Q_(rR,rL)>=0,
 NR=<zR-J*rR,rR-rL>=-J Q_(rL,rR)>=0.

Their symmetric, unscaled sum in the TASK is

 Nsym=NL/E+NR/J
     =<z/E-zR/J,rL-rR>-||rL-rR||^2>=0.          (5.3)

This has the following expansion in the original updates. Put
L00=A*g0+B*g1+A*g2=x-u. Then

 z/E-zR/J=(1/E-1/J)x+(1/J-1/E)L00
          +(1+k)(1/J-1/E)A3
          +[L01+b*A7+L10+(1+k)A11]/J.           (5.4)

Thus using Nsym with a scalar coefficient leaves a mixed initial-x term
because E!=J. We use the mass-balanced nonnegative combination instead:

 Na=NL+NR
   =<Zdiff,rL-rR>-E||rL||^2-J||rR||^2
                                           +(E+J)<rL,rR>>=0. (5.5)

It has literal original row weights E and J, and cancels x. It is NOT
silently equated to E*Nsym or to J*Nsym. Substituting (5.1) completely
expands its displacement in the actual original gradients.

The local right-child bridge pair needed to allocate payment is

 Nc=-Q_(rR,11)-J Q_(11,rR)
   =<uR-A11,A11-rR>+<zR-J*rR,rR-A11>
   =k||A11||^2+(J-k)<A11,rR>-J||rR||^2>=0.     (5.6)

Its last equality uses the original update zR=uR-(1+k)A11, not an
auxiliary step. These are the very two normal orientations occurring in
B_r, reallocated after the complete combination (4.4)-(4.8).

## 6. One literal allocation and the exact full-remainder obstruction

Freeze C_rows and the canonical mixture (W_*,N_*) of Section4. Extract
the three nonnegative combinations in (5.2),(5.5),(5.6) with coefficients

 theta=d_i/k, omega=d_i,
 tau=theta*(E-J)^2/(4EJ)=theta*h^2/(EJ).         (6.1)

They are strictly positive by (2.2),(4.5). This is a literal mass-dependent
choice, not an assertion that an unspecified feasible multiplier array exists.
On original ordered rows the added nonnegative coefficients are

 omega on each of -Q_(11,3), -Q_(3,11),
 theta*E on -Q_(rR,rL), theta*J on -Q_(rL,rR),
 tau on -Q_(rR,11), tau*J on -Q_(11,rR).

The exact candidate certificate is

 M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]
  =C_rows+N_*+omega*Ng+theta*Na+tau*Nc+Qpair,
 Qpair=Qbase-omega*Ng-theta*Na-tau*Nc.           (6.2)

No parent or right-child remainder has been dropped: Qbase was derived
from their COMPLETE sum, and all physical squared reserves are in P_sq.
For this attempted proof it would suffice that Qpair be PSD on the full
formal gradient space (p0,g0,...,g15,rL,rR).

Here is the reason for choosing tau in (6.1). With physical gradients and
p0 set to0, the auxiliary quadratic block of Qpair for an arbitrary tau is

 theta*E||rL||^2+J(theta+tau)||rR||^2
                                      -theta(E+J)<rL,rR>.    (6.3)

At alpha=(E+J)/(2E), setting rL=alpha*rR makes its value

       [J*tau-theta*(E-J)^2/(4E)]||rR||^2.      (6.4)

Thus PSD requires tau>=theta*h^2/(EJ). Equality, our choice, makes the
auxiliary block exactly

                 theta*E||rL-alpha*rR||^2.       (6.5)

This is the least possible right-only local payment for this block, not
an arbitrary oversized reserve extraction.

Nevertheless the full remainder fails. Write vaux=rL-alpha*rR and

                   Jvec=Zdiff-h*(J-k)*A11/J.

Expanding ALL the terms in (6.2) with (5.5)-(5.6) gives

 Qpair=Qbase-omega*Ng
       +theta*E||vaux||^2-theta<Zdiff,vaux>
       +(theta*h/E)<Jvec,rR>-tau*k||A11||^2.     (6.6)

Equivalently, with a complete square,

 Qpair=Qbase-omega*Ng
       +theta*E||vaux-Zdiff/(2E)||^2
       -theta||Zdiff||^2/(4E)
       +(theta*h/E)<Jvec,rR>-tau*k||A11||^2.     (6.7)

The auxiliary null direction leaves a genuine physical coupling:

 Jvec=L01+b*A7+L10
                    +[1+k-h*(J-k)/J]A11.       (6.8)

This is not identically zero under the physical update equations. For
example, assign a common nonzero formal vector G to EVERY physical
gradient, including the terminal, and also to p0. Then

 L01=L10=t*G, Zdiff=h*G,
 Jvec=h*k*G/J, W_*=0, P_sq=0,
 Qbase=0, Ng=0.                                (6.9)

The signed coefficients in (4.7) sum to0, which checks Qbase=0 exactly.
On this same formal physical direction take rR=sigma*G and rL=alpha*sigma*G,
where sigma is an arbitrary real scalar. Equations(6.6),(6.9) give

                         Qpair=tau*k*(sigma-1)||G||^2.       (6.10)

In particular sigma=0 makes the WHOLE quadratic remainder strictly negative.
No uncomputed eigenvalue, numerical solve, or isolated coefficient sign is
being used. This is an explicit vector assignment in its full formal space.
It does not obey the additional projection constraints and is NOT offered
as a legal H4 adversary. Those constraints are precisely the rows being
used to form a sufficient conic/PSD identity; PSD is required before their
signs are used and therefore must hold on this formal direction too.

The obstruction is stronger than failure at the one chosen tau. Keep any
theta>0 and any omega>=0, and let tau>=0 vary while retaining the same
three row combinations, C_rows, and canonical initial mixture. If tau is
below the threshold in (6.4), the auxiliary-only formal direction has
negative Qpair. If tau is at or above it, then tau>0; the direction

             g0=...=g15=p0=G!=0, rL=rR=0        (6.11)

gives Qpair=-tau*k||G||^2<0, independent of omega. Thus no tau works in
THIS family. This conclusion concerns the whole specified matrix, while
leaving arbitrary redistributions of old row weights or of N_* outside scope.

The failure also explains why a cost-free recursion cannot be inferred:
inserting cross-sibling cycles while freezing the initial-maximizer mixture
requires positive auxiliary control, but that control consumes a direction
on which every retained physical square and the fixed final square vanish.
An attachment involving the actual initial/terminal projection must change,
or a materially different redistribution of existing rows must be derived.
This statement identifies an unpaid direction; it supplies no sufficient
all-depth repair and no rate or total coefficient bound.

## 7. Mandatory all-core actual H4 check

Take D0=[-1,1], x=1/100. Put Qc=a^2*beta. From a>1/3, beta>9/10 and
a<u0, one has 1/10<Qc<1/5. Every physical core multiplier has magnitude
a,beta,k or K; a prefix has at most two factors k and one K, so its
magnitude is less than (5/2)^2*7=175/4<100. Induction in the fifteen
physical steps keeps every query and terminal strictly in(-1,1).
All auxiliary denominators are at least1, so their projections are also
interior. This is a symbolic guard, not an executed trajectory.

Multiplying the literal factors gives the full landmarks, including the
ACTUAL right H3,

 u=-Qc*x, z=k*Qc*x, v=-k*Qc^2*x, w=-K*v,
 uR=-Qc*w, zR=k*Qc*w, y=-k*Qc^2*w
                              =-K*(k*Qc^2)^2*x.             (7.1)

All physical gradients equal their input. The auxiliary samples are
p0=x/M, rL=z/E, rO=w/D, rR=zR/J, q=y, qL=v/eta.
EVERY normal row on these samples vanishes, including Ng,Na,Nc,N_*,
all the rows in C_rows, and the normal parts of every retained remainder.
For a core H2 of input X its energy is (1-C^2*Qc^2)X^2, so the four
energies in (4.1)-(4.2) use respectively X=x,z,w,zR. They have not been
set to zero or deleted.

For clarity the entire right H3 identity specializes to

 G_R=w^2/D-y^2
    =V10/D+V11/J+B_r/D+RPr+RRr,
 B_r=C^2*uR^2-(zR+ell*zR/J)^2,
 D*RPr=ell^2*zR^2/J^2,
 J*RRr=t^2*y^2.                                (7.2)

These follow respectively from (3.5),(2.5); q=y is essential in the last
formula. The complete matched identity and its paired form therefore both
reduce to

             M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]=x^2-M*y^2=Qpair.   (7.3)

It is strictly positive. Indeed k*Qc^2<1/10, K<7 and
M=1+2(2s+1+K)<71, so M[y/x]^2<3479/10000<1.
This checks the complete identity with all normal rows zero; a negative
isolated Xi neither describes nor refutes (7.3).

## 8. Mandatory inherited cap/cap actual H4 check

Take the ONE fixed body D0=[-r,1], r=k/C, and input x=C. The first H2
has points C-T>=1 at prefix mass T in[0,t] and constant gradient1,
ending at u=1. Its original inner bridge gives z=-k. The second H2 has
points -k+T*r<=-r and gradient-r, ending at v=-r. Hence

 V00=V01=0, P00=P01=0, w=K*r.                  (8.1)

There is no replacement right child. Define the scalar clamp
P_r(X)=max(-r,min(X,1)) and T_l(X)=X-l*P_r(X). The ACTUAL remaining
queries and terminal, on this same body and original word, are

 x9=T_A(w), x10=T_B(x9), uR=T_A(x10),
 A11=P_r(uR), zR=uR-(1+k)*A11,
 x13=T_A(zR), x14=T_B(x13), y=T_A(x14), q=P_r(y). (8.2)

These symbolic formulas specify the entire right history without an assumed
face pattern. They have not been executed by a mathematical program.
All the right H2 energies and every right remainder remain

 V10=w^2-(uR+t*A11)^2,
 V11=zR^2-(y+t*q)^2,
 B_r=Theta(C,k,J)(A11-rR)^2
       +2ell(1+C/(J+k))(uR-A11)(A11-rR)
       +2ell*C/(J+k)(zR-J*rR)(rR-A11),
 RPr=R(w,D,(w-zR+(J+ell)rR)/D),
 RRr=R(zR,J,(zR-y+C*q)/J).                      (8.3)

Their exact sum is G_R in (4.2), and is nonnegative by the jointly derived
H2 certificates, (2.5), and (3.7), regardless of the clamp choices.

The left and outer auxiliary values are interior:

 p0=C/M, rL=-k/E, rO=w/D, qL=-r/eta.            (8.4)

Here E>C, M>C, r>1/3, r<1/2, and 0<w<7/2<D.
The right auxiliary is also interior; this can be checked without choosing
its physical faces. For a step0<=l<=2 and any X,

 |X-l*P_r(X)|^2
 <=|X|^2-l(2-l)|P_r(X)|^2<=|X|^2,

using <X,P_r(X)>>=|P_r(X)|^2 from the normal to0. All three H2 steps
are in(0,2), so |uR|<=w<7/2. If uR<=-r, then
zR=uR+(1+k)r lies between -5/2 and k*r; if -r<=uR<=1, then
zR=-k*uR lies between -k and k*r; if uR>=1, then
zR=uR-(1+k) lies between -k and1/2. The bounds(3.6) thus give

 -5/2<zR<5/4, J=2C-1>10,
                 rR=zR/J in(-1/4,1/8) subset(-r,1).        (8.5)

Both auxiliary normals in (5.5) vanish on this actual cap history, hence
Na=Nsym=0. The physical and local-right combinations retain their actual
values

 Ng=(uR-A11)(A11-1),
 Nc=(uR-A11)(A11-zR/J),                         (8.6)

since u-g3=0 and zR-J*rR=0. No separate assertion that these values
vanish is needed; both are nonnegative by their original normal rows.

Here U_M(C)=C^2/M and U_D(w)=w^2/D. The complete matched and paired
identities have, respectively, the following exact specializations:

 C^2-M*U1(y)=B_i+B_o+Xi+M*G_R,
 G_R=w^2/D-U1(y)=V10/D+V11/J+B_r/D+RPr+RRr,

 C^2-M*U1(y)=C_rows+N_*+omega*Ng+tau*Nc+Qpair.   (8.7)

In particular the complete parent endpoint slack can also be written as

              C^2-(M/D)w^2+M*G_R.              (8.8)

It is strictly positive. The exact ratio for its prefix is

 (M/D)w^2/C^2=K^2*k^2*M/(C^4*D)
                         <46875/58564<1.        (8.9)

To verify the bound, h=s+1+K<D=1+2s because s>11>K, hence M/D<3;
use K<25/4, k<5/2, C>11/2. Multiplication gives
3*(25/4)^2*(5/2)^2/(11/2)^4=46875/58564.
Since G_R>=0 by its FULL retained expansion, (8.8) is positive.

Thus this diagnostic, whose two left primitive reserves are exactly zero,
does not refute H4. It is accounted for by the parent together with its
entire actual right child. The formal directions in Section6 are explicitly
different from this legal history. No large-d diagnostic is used here.

## 9. Remaining interface and resource boundary

The new exact interface is (6.6)-(6.11). The auxiliary denominator gap
E-J=2h forces a positive local allocation in this additive cross-cycle
template. With the canonical initial mixture and old primitive coefficients
held fixed, the allocation makes the whole remainder negative on a physical
common-gradient formal direction. Enlarging just that allocation cannot fix
it, and changing only the physical sibling weight cannot fix it either.

This closes only the specified pairing family. It does not forbid a
certificate that simultaneously redistributes initial-maximizer normals,
local bridge rows and cross-sibling rows. No such sufficient redistribution
has been derived in this call. No arbitrary all-d closure is assumed; every
new test above uses d=1 with the exact inherited E,J,D,M.

Mechanism1 ends with the identified obstruction. Because H4 remains unpaid,
the instructed condition for opening an all-depth rule was not met. There
is no H5/H6 casework, no claimed1+C0/m endpoint loss, and no sufficient17/16
full-class bound. The conditional full-W bridge in finding62e43bbe64d53676
is not used as a theorem or a verified fact.

Recall read TASK, the latest applicable master_guidance, local notes, the
complete prior draft5eca10879d4d07ef, relevant shared dead ends, accepted
1252b8823bb5a290 necessity, accepted04219554e41d9750 Sections10-11, and
accepted931b9653ce0f7201. Fact search found no existing H4 endpoint fact.
Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving, and
construct-counterexamples. The attempted Matlas query returned short-step
self-contracted, logistic, and unrelated multi-task results, none applicable.
Web background retrieval recovered Zhang--Jiang's concatenation paper,
https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12395. No external theorem or web snippet is used
as a proof premise. This is the previously audited projection-normal and
composition regime, not a newly imported literature mechanism.

Complete joint construction and proof are in89e46fc6f580a48b and its exact public manuscript /opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/correlated_mixed_history_20261001/mixed_history.md. All statements here remain UNVERIFIED. The function is the one static maximum of0 and three scaled finite logarithmic-arc dictionaries, followed by one Moreau envelope, in R4. epsilon=1/2, u_(i+1)=-r*u_i+sqrt(1-r^2)e_(i+1); all scales, centers and an explicit nonempty parameter domain are fixed in Sections1-2. The physical word is h=(alpha2,alpha1,2,alpha2,alpha1,1+d/a,alpha2,alpha1,2), unchanged by the repair. On its third ordinary block, the exactly proved active slopes are p=c*u0 and q=d*u2. Set v=p-q, D=||v||^2, eta_star=d*(d-c*r^2)/D and g_bar=q+eta_star*v. The fixed amplitude satisfies d>c/r^2, so eta_star-1=c*(d*r^2-c)/D>0. At x6 the actual old-cap weight eta0 is in(0,1). After steps alpha2,alpha1 the actual coefficient is eta2=eta_star+(eta0-eta_star)*(1-alpha2)*(1-alpha1), still in(0,1), as proved against EVERY one of the ten fixed planes. Their heights are positive. At the next physical step2, continuation on that face would require eta_next=2*eta_star-eta2>1, outside the gradient segment[p,q]. The actual new point is x9=C1+d*(-epsilon*u1-zeta*u2)+R(d), zeta=(1-alpha2)*(1-alpha1), with R(d) explicitly bounded independently of d. Section5 proves the retained stage1 endpoint a*w1 is the UNIQUE active plane at y9=x9-a*w1; its gradient has negative u0 component, whereas EVERY point of[p,q] has positive u0 component. Thus even clipping to p is incorrect. Its positive height and value are H9=a*d*L_end+a<w1,R(d)>-a^2*omega^2 and F9=H9+a^2*omega^2/2, where w1=epsilon*u1-u2, omega=||w1|| and L_end=zeta*(1+epsilon*r)-epsilon*(epsilon+r)>0. Section6 proves all directed rows and closest initial radius R0 exactly. This counterexample is to the specified finite carrier cone, not an impossibility of correlated mixed constructions or a minimax upper bound.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001367 -->
# Saturated-cut certificates retain a signed terminal-interpolation credit

Status: self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or
fact_submit was used. The full initial-distance objective theorem is not
being restricted here: the statement concerns the explicitly specified
intrinsic primitive energy. The only accepted background used is the
necessity of the interpolation inequalities in fact 1252b8823bb5a290.

## 1. Definitions and an exact identity

Work at smoothness one. On a GD trajectory for a list h of sum s and
capacity C=1+s, write g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), and

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0,
  V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2.

Take two lists a,b of capacities A,D, respectively, and set

  T=A+D+r,  r>0,  rT=AD,
  kappa=(T/D)^2=(A/r)^2,
  theta=1-1/kappa.

In particular 0<r<min(A,D), kappa>1 and 0<theta<1. The joined
schedule is h=(a,1+r,b). Denote its bridge query by L and the tail
queries by j=0,...,m; z=m is the final query. Tail indices in Q always
mean the corresponding original joined indices. Define

  J_b=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,z),
  E_theta(b)=V_b-theta J_b,
  C_cross=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z).

The exact identity, including every value term, is

  V_h = V_a + kappa E_theta(b) - (kappa-1) C_cross.       (1)

All coefficients in -C_cross are nonnegative on actual inequalities
-Q. Thus universal nonnegativity of V_a and E_theta(b) is a sufficient
condition for universal nonnegativity of V_h. It is not necessary here
to assume V_b>=0. Whether this genuinely enlarges the allowable tail
class is a separate question.

Here is a direct audit of (1). Put p=g_L, q=g_z, and
H=sum_(j<m)b_j g_j. Then x_tail,0=x_L-(1+r)p and x_z=x_tail,0-H.
For C=C_cross+J_b the value part is

  C_f=2sum_(j<m)b_j f_j+(r-D)f_L+(2-D-r)f_z.

Expand each Q and use
2sum_j b_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)b_i g_i>=||H||^2-sum_j b_j^2||g_j||^2.
The gradient part reduces exactly to

  C_g=sum_(j<m)b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2
            +[(D-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(D+r)||q||^2/2.

All mixed p,H,q products cancel. The identities

  (kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r),
  kappa r^2=A^2,  kappa D^2=T^2

then show V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)C coefficient by coefficient:
the f_L coefficient is 2(1+r), the f_z coefficient is -2(T-1),
the p-square coefficient is -r(1+r), and the q-square coefficient
is -T(T-1). Substituting kappa theta=kappa-1 proves (1).

## 2. Full intrinsic certificates force the bridge coefficients

For this part assume the entries of a and b are strictly positive;
either list may be empty. Suppose an explicit intrinsic certificate is
given:

  M_h=V_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij,
  lambda_ij>=0,

where values cancel and M_h is PSD in the full independent formal
gradient vector. Allowing the optimum index and x_0 initially does not
enlarge this class. Indeed V_h has no x_0-square coefficient, so PSD
forces the x_0 row to vanish. Its cross coefficients are -lambda_i,star,
which forces every lambda_i,star=0. Summing the value-flow equalities
then gives sum_i lambda_star,i=0, so these multipliers also vanish.
This argument relies on the absence of an initial-distance term and
does NOT apply to a full objective certificate.

There are two explicit equality trajectories for V_h. A scalar Huber
trajectory staying on one affine ray has every gradient equal to 1,
all finite Q zero, and V_h=0. A second trajectory uses the derivative

  f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)),  eta=r/D,

starting at A. The prefix gradient is 1, its last query is 1, the
bridge lands at -r, and the tail gradient is -eta until its endpoint
-eta. Every incoming optimum residual is zero. Distance telescoping
gives V_h=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0.

For completeness, if R_i is a prefix query's remaining prefix sum
and R_j is a tail query's remaining tail sum, this second trajectory
has

  Q_(prefix i,tail j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j,
  Q_(tail j,prefix i)=-(1+eta)R_i.                       (2)

Every within-block Q is zero. Since M_h>=0 and all lambda Q<=0,
equality of V_h forces multiplier complementarity and M_h to vanish
on each equality gradient vector. Those vectors span the two block
indicators e_A,e_B, so both indicators lie in the kernel of M_h.

By (2), the only potentially nonzero cross multipliers are

  nu_i=lambda_(prefix i,z),  mu_j=lambda_(tail j,L).

For a prefix index i<L, the corresponding row of M_h e_B is
-D nu_i/2. Thus nu_i=0 for every i<L. Write nu=nu_L.
For a nonterminal tail index j, the corresponding row of M_h e_A
is [r mu_j-nu b_j]/2; at z it is [r mu_z-nu]/2.
Consequently

  mu_j=(nu/r)b_j  (j<m),  mu_z=nu/r.

The sum of the prefix value-flow equations is
sum_j mu_j-nu=2(A+r). Therefore all cross multipliers are fixed:

  lambda_(j,L)=(kappa-1)b_j (j<m),
  lambda_(z,L)=kappa-1,
  lambda_(L,z)=r(kappa-1),                              (3)

and every other cross multiplier is zero. The mixed gradient block
of the full matrix vanishes, not merely its value on equality data.

## 3. Exact cone interface and the sign that cannot be discarded

Let lambda^A,lambda^B be the two within-block multiplier arrays of
the given full certificate. Define a signed tail array by

  lambda_hat_ij=lambda^B_ij/kappa
                 -theta b_i * 1_(i<m,j=z).             (4)

Combining (1) with (3) yields the full block-diagonal identity

  M_h = M_a + kappa M_b,
  M_a=V_a+sum lambda^A_ij Q_ij,
  M_b=V_b+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij.                       (5)

Each summand has its own value cancellation, since its values are in
a disjoint block. Full PSD is equivalent to PSD of both summands.
The prefix multipliers are nonnegative. The exact tail constraints are

  lambda_hat_ij>=0 except possibly i<m,j=z,
  lambda_hat_(i,z)>=-theta b_i.                        (6)

Conversely, any prefix intrinsic certificate and any PSD tail identity
with the value cancellation and bounds (6) give a nonnegative full
certificate by (3),(4). Equivalently, the tail has an ordinary
nonnegative certificate for E_theta(b), not necessarily for V_b.

Thus direct restriction to an ordinary primitive tail certificate would
require the stronger bounds

  lambda^B_(i,z)>=(kappa-1)b_i.                        (7)

They are not implied for each full certificate. The next exact example
proves this, including full nonnegativity and PSD.

## 4. Exact full certificate violating the proposed coefficient floor

Use the empty prefix A=1, tail b=(1,1) of capacity D=3, and

  r=sqrt(7)-2, T=sqrt(7)+2, kappa=(11+4sqrt(7))/9.

The full schedule is h=(sqrt(7)-1,1,1). Use original indices 0,1,2,3,
where 0 is the bridge query and 1,2,3 are the tail queries. Set

  lambda_10=lambda_20=lambda_30=kappa-1,
  lambda_03=r(kappa-1),
  lambda_12=5kappa/2, lambda_21=17kappa/4,
  lambda_23=37kappa/4-1, lambda_32=12kappa,
  lambda_13=3kappa/4-1,

with every unlisted multiplier zero. All are nonnegative, since
kappa>4/3. Direct substitution, or (1) and the audited tail identity
below, gives

  V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
   =kappa { ||(g_1-g_2)-(g_2-g_3)/8||^2
                         +(255/64)||g_2-g_3||^2 } >=0. (8)

For the tail alone, write u=g_0-g_1 and v=g_1-g_2 using local indices.
Its signed multiplier list is

  lambda_hat_01=5/2, lambda_hat_10=17/4,
  lambda_hat_12=33/4, lambda_hat_21=12,
  lambda_hat_02=-1/4, lambda_hat_20=0.

The value coefficients cancel, and expansion gives

  V_(1,1)+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij
      =||u||^2-<u,v>/4+4||v||^2
      =||u-v/8||^2+(255/64)||v||^2.

Nevertheless lambda_13=(kappa-1)-kappa/4 is strictly below the
proposed floor kappa-1. The restricted terminal coefficient is -1/4.
This refutes the inference of (7) from full nonnegativity and PSD.

There IS another ordinary primitive certificate for this tail:
lambda_01=2,lambda_10=4,lambda_12=8,lambda_21=12, all other
multipliers zero, gives ||u||^2+4||v||^2. Accordingly this example
does not disprove existence of a suitable re-selection, arbitrary-tail
heredity, or the original asymptotic minimax problem. It identifies
the exact missing sign assertion rather than replacing it with PSD.

## 5. A particular nonprimitive tail cannot exploit this credit

The previously considered b=(17/10,2,6/5) fails even E_theta for
every 0<=theta<=1. Here is a self-contained witness.
Put m=7/34, ell=21/85. Define the continuous derivative

  f'(x)=x                  for x<=m,
         m                 for m<=x<=m+ell,
         x-ell             for x>=m+ell,

and integrate with f(0)=0. Its derivative is nondecreasing and
1-Lipschitz, so f is convex differentiable 1-smooth with minimizer 0.
Starting at x_0=106/85 gives

  (x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(106/85,-77/170,77/170,7/34),
  (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(1,-77/170,7/34,7/34).

The endpoint is core: x_3=g_3=m, f_3=m^2/2. Define
I_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Direct scalar evaluation gives

  I_0=ell(1-m)=567/2890, I_1=I_2=I_3=0,
  sum_(i<3)b_i I_i=567/1700,
  V_b=-13581/23120.

At a core endpoint, expansion gives Q_(i,3)=-I_i. Thus

  E_theta(b)=-13581/23120+theta*567/1700
           <= -29349/115600 <0.                       (9)

The value of V_b can also be checked without integrating every term:
distance telescoping gives
V_b=x_0^2-D^2g_3^2-2sum b_i I_i, with D=59/10.
This proves (9) exactly.

Consequently this specific tail cannot acquire a valid E_theta
certificate at any saturated join parameter. A rank-one coefficient
matching attempt on this same fixed tail also failed, but (9) is the
stronger actual-function obstruction. Neither statement excludes other
nonprimitive tails. No numerical schedule screen was reopened.

## 6. Complete two-bridge identity with retained earlier anchors

The identity also provides a closed, explicitly signed value state
through two bridges. Let a_1,a_2 be any nonnegative physical prefix
lists of capacities A_1,A_2. Starting with the physical tail b of
capacity D_0, define

  H_1=(a_1,1+r_1,b), D_1=A_1+D_0+r_1, r_1 D_1=A_1 D_0,
  H_2=(a_2,1+r_2,H_1), D_2=A_2+D_1+r_2, r_2 D_2=A_2 D_1,
  kappa_1=(D_1/D_0)^2, kappa_2=(D_2/D_1)^2,
  K=kappa_1 kappa_2=(D_2/D_0)^2, theta_2=1-1/K.

Let L_1,L_2 be the respective bridge query indices in the final
original indexing and z the final query. For each physical step in
a_1 or a_2, its gradient/value index is also its original index in H_2.
Define the following sums of actual inequalities, without collapsing
any old query or replacing it by a new anchor:

  C_1=sum_(j in b steps)b_j Q_(j,L_1)+Q_(z,L_1)+r_1 Q_(L_1,z),
  C_2=sum_(i in H_1 steps)(H_1)_i Q_(i,L_2)
                                      +Q_(z,L_2)+r_2 Q_(L_2,z),
  J_left1=sum_(i in a_1 steps)(a_1)_i Q_(i,z)
                                      +(1+r_1)Q_(L_1,z).

Then the complete identity is

  V_(H_2)=V_(a_2)+kappa_2 V_(a_1)+K E_theta2(b)
       -kappa_2(kappa_1-1)C_1-(kappa_2-1)C_2
       -(kappa_2-1)J_left1.                            (10)

Every coefficient displayed on -Q is nonnegative. In particular the
earlier a_1 queries still have their actual outgoing edges to z, and
every query inside H_1 has its actual edge to L_2. These are not removed
by the unqueried-terminal theorem.

To verify (10) algebraically, apply the audited expansion in Section 1
at each cut. The weighted incoming-terminal sum for H_1 is exactly
J_b+J_left1. Substitution gives a J_b coefficient
kappa_2(kappa_1-1)+(kappa_2-1)=K-1=K theta_2; all other terms are
those displayed in (10). This derivation is part of this same candidate
proof, not an invocation of an unverified finding as a theorem.

Consequently, if explicit nonnegative intrinsic certificates are given
for a_1 and a_2, and an explicit nonnegative certificate is given for
E_theta2(b), (10) assembles an intrinsic certificate for H_2. Its PSD
remainder is the sum of the three input PSD remainders with scales
1,kappa_2,K. All original actual multipliers are sums of nonnegative
numbers. No positive remainder is presumed for a benchmark block.

## 7. Final objective certificate, including initial distance

Here is the exact final conversion for any intrinsic certificate
produced by (10), and in particular for the example below. Write H=H_2,
let its terminal query be n, its capacity be D=1+sum_(i<n)H_i, and let

  M_H=V_H+sum_(i!=j<=n)lambda_ij Q_ij >=0

be the assembled PSD identity, with all lambda_ij>=0. Define

  t=(-1+sqrt(8D+1))/4, beta=1+t,
  gamma=1/(2t+1), q=gamma^2,
  x_(n+1)=x_n-beta g_n.

The arithmetic identities are t=gamma D, 1-gamma=2qD, and
q=1/[1+2(sum H_i+beta)]. The accepted terminal-square parameterization
in fact 1252b8823bb5a290(c) applies with terminal probabilities
alpha_star=gamma, alpha_n=1-gamma, and all other alpha_i=0.
Give the queried prefix the following additional optimum multipliers:

  lambda_pre_(i,star)=2q H_i  (i<n),
  lambda_pre_(n,star)=2q(D+1),

and multiply every intrinsic lambda_ij by q. All these coefficients
are nonnegative and their flows are exactly the required 2alpha_i.
There are no optimum-outgoing multipliers in this particular
constructed certificate; none is dropped from an arbitrary certificate.
The full identity supplied by the accepted parameterization is

  q||x_0||^2-2f_(n+1)
    +q sum_(i!=j<=n)lambda_ij Q_ij
    +2q sum_(i<n)H_i Q_(i,star)+2q(D+1)Q_(n,star)
    +2(1-gamma)Q_(n+1,n)+2gamma Q_(n+1,star)
  =||g_(n+1)-[gamma x_n-(t+gamma)g_n]||^2+q M_H.       (11)

The optimum is translated to x_star=0,f_star=0 in this display.
It follows that W_(n+1)(H,beta)<=q. The factor on ||x_0||^2 is retained,
so this is a certificate in the original objective normalization.

To audit its application rather than re-prove the accepted component,
the prefix remainder of that component is

  R=q||x_0||^2-2(1-gamma)(f_n-||g_n||^2/2)
       -||gamma x_n-(t+gamma)g_n||^2
       +2q sum_(i<n)H_i Q_(i,star)+2q(D+1)Q_(n,star)
       +q sum lambda_ij Q_ij.

Using t=gamma D and 1-gamma=2qD, its mixed <x_n,g_n> coefficient
cancels. Its f_n and ||g_n||^2 coefficients are respectively
-2q(D-1) and -qD(D-1). Distance telescoping then gives R=q M_H,
which is exactly (11). Every queried-prefix inequality is retained.

## 8. A full two-bridge calibration and its exact scope

Take b=(1,1), and take both a_1,a_2 empty. Then

  D_0=3, D_1=2+sqrt(7), r_1=sqrt(7)-2,
  D_2=[1+D_1+sqrt((1+D_1)^2+4D_1)]/2, r_2=D_2-1-D_1.

Thus H_2=(1+r_2,sqrt(7)-1,1,1). For E_theta2(b), use the signed
tail coefficients in Section 4 and add theta_2 b_i to each incoming
terminal coefficient. The only initially negative coefficient becomes
theta_2-1/4>0: already kappa_1>4/3 and K>kappa_1. All other
coefficients are nonnegative. The PSD remainder is
||u-v/8||^2+(255/64)||v||^2, because the added interpolation terms
exactly cancel the -theta_2 J_b term in E_theta2.

Equations (10),(11) therefore give a completely explicit certificate
for this five-step objective schedule after appending beta. This is a
calibration of signed coordinates with nonnegative actual multipliers,
not an improved asymptotic schedule. In particular, the physical tail
capacity 3 is already smaller than that of the legal two-step primitive
schedule (3/2,3/2), whose capacity is 4. The old tail also has an ordinary
primitive certificate, so this example does not establish strict
enlargement by the E_theta state.

The comparator's primitive property has a direct identity, so no
unverified external premise is needed: at h=(3/2,3/2), take
lambda_01=1,lambda_10=7/2,lambda_12=17/2,lambda_21=14,
lambda_02=1/2,lambda_20=1. Expanding gives V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0.
All six multipliers are positive. Replacing the capacity-3 tail by this
capacity-4 tail in the same two saturated joins strictly increases both
successive capacities, since the positive root
Omega(A,D)=[A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD)]/2 is increasing in D for A,D>0.
The terminal q in (11) strictly decreases. The displayed calibration
is therefore strictly dominated already at its own finite horizon.

The unresolved bridge toward an improvement is concrete: find an actual
tail with a nonnegative certificate for E_theta at some theta<1 and with
a capacity gain large enough to pay for the physical prefix lengths.
Neither the existence of that tail nor the required infinite-horizon
comparison with U_n^ZJ is asserted here.
