# C93: curvature costs and an exact single-region obstruction for static ridge sums

Status: **UNVERIFIED JOINT PROOF DRAFT**. Current runtime round is 1 of
the new allocation, completed baseline 23 and completed ceiling 25. This
round would bring the completed count to 24 after the runtime returns.
No numerical/model evaluation, sweep, solver, replay, installation,
subordinate agent, or verifier call was used. The original problem is open.

Final revision of the joint draft: shared finding `25f0424231f49cc6`
contained the all-samples fixed-region theorem. Section 7 now proves a
strictly broader bound with arbitrary actual terminal region, and retains
the same asymptotic ceiling. This extension is within the same runtime
round. Latest guidance `94ae9bd894051fbf` was read; all allocation and
scope restrictions remain unchanged.

Accepted mathematical predecessors used here are `1252b8823bb5a290`
(necessity of every original smooth-convex interpolation inequality),
`b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact scalar Huber/single-cut lower bound and the
original benchmark), `1fca3e5dd555cb4a` (the explicit alternating-ray
construction), and `04219554e41d9750` (the original dyadic benchmark limit).
All new assertions are proved together below. No shared unverified result
is a mathematical predecessor.

The shared ruler audit `bf591e52671f6d33` suggested the activated-product
quantity used in Section 5. Its needed estimates are rederived here, and
the new conclusion is a resolvent inequality, rather than its quadratic
objective bound. The accepted finite C59 fact `e9a55f57461f253c` and max's
shared actual31 construction `7ffb4d0516ace32f` were read for scope. Neither
is inserted as a child, used as an asymptotic premise, or replayed.

## 1. Family, definitions, and resolved scope

All vector spaces are finite-dimensional real Euclidean spaces. Matrices
are symmetric unless otherwise specified, and matrix inequalities refer
to positive semidefinite order. The identity matrix is denoted I.

For positive radii r_minus,r_plus, define the scalar asymmetric Huber
function phi by phi(0)=0 and

    phi'(s)=max(-r_minus,min(s,r_plus)).                 (1)

Thus phi(s)=s^2/2 inside [-r_minus,r_plus],
phi(s)=r_plus*s-r_plus^2/2 for s>=r_plus, and
phi(s)=-r_minus*s-r_minus^2/2 for s<=-r_minus.
Both joins include their boundaries, where the values and derivatives agree.

Consider ONE STATIC function

    F(x)=x^T A0 x/2 + sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j phi_j(u_j^T x), (2)

where r is any nonnegative integer, A0>=0, every u_j is a unit vector,
alpha_j>0, and every phi_j has its own positive radii. Assume

    H0=A0+sum_j alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.                 (3)

The number of ridges, dimension, matrices, directions, and both radii may
depend arbitrarily on the horizon. The ridges need not be orthogonal.
The quadratic part need not commute with any ridge matrix.

For a fixed finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), set

    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, K=1/(1+2S),
    P_h(t)=product_(i<n)(1-h_i*t),
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i), 0<=i<n.                (4)

The normalized payoff is

    V=2F(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2,                     (5)

and initial points in the minimizer set are omitted.

A **fixed-region run** means that, for EACH ridge separately, all samples
x_0,...,x_n, INCLUDING the actual unqueried terminal sample, belong to one
of that ridge's three closed intervals: its core, its positive outer ray,
or its negative outer ray. Different ridges may choose different regions.
At a shared boundary either compatible region may be chosen. No condition
is imposed on signs or correlations of the full gradients.

The main quantitative conclusion of this joint draft is:

* If P_h(t)^2 <= 1/(1+2S*t) for every t in [0,1], every fixed-region run
  of (2) has V<=K. This includes all true normalization costs.
* On the ORIGINAL diagnostic H_m defined in Section 5, for every m>=128,
  that polynomial inequality holds, and the supremum of V over the entire
  fixed-region class is EXACTLY K_n(H_m)=1/(1+2 sum H_m).
* More broadly, if only the QUERIED samples x_0,...,x_(n-1) share a fixed
  region and the actual terminal sample is unrestricted, then
  K <= sup V <= 1/[1+2S-2(1+a)] on that same H_m. This larger class is
  asymptotically Huber-tight as well; a terminal region change alone
  cannot produce a fixed positive relative improvement.
* Both classes' exact original-benchmark ratios tend to Z_1/(2e)<4/5. Here
  Z_1=lim_(m->infinity)(1+sqrt(2))^(-m) B_(2^m-1), as in accepted
  `04219554e41d9750`, and e is the exponential of 1.

This closes the fixed-queried-region subclass with arbitrary quadratic
coupling and any number of subunit-curvature ridges, allowing any actual
terminal region. It does NOT close trajectories that change a ridge
region within the queried prefix. Section 9 gives a genuinely coupled static example
with two physical sign changes and actual noncommuting region visits.
It refutes dropping the fixed-region premise even on a word satisfying
the same polynomial inequality. Its payoff is explicitly compared with
the stronger already accepted scalar construction and is NOT presented
as new lower-bound strength.

## 2. Global legality, minimizers, and every original pair

Each phi_j is differentiable, convex, nonnegative, and zero only at zero.
For any x,z, monotonicity and the Lipschitz bound in (1) provide numbers
d_j in [0,alpha_j] such that

    grad F(x)-grad F(z)
      =[A0+sum_j d_j u_j u_j^T](x-z).

If u_j^T(x-z)=0 its contribution is zero and d_j may be taken as zero.
The displayed matrix lies between zero and H0, and hence between zero
and I. Its operator norm is at most 1. This proves global 1-smoothness;
convexity also follows directly from the sum in (2).

Conversely, all radii are positive, so a ball about zero lies in every
core. In that ball F(x)=x^T H0 x/2. Thus global 1-smoothness forces (3).
Condition (3) is exact for this centered model, not merely a sufficient
trace bound.

The minimizer set is precisely the subspace

    N=ker(A0) intersect intersection_j u_j^perp.        (6)

Indeed every summand of (2) is nonnegative, and their simultaneous
vanishing is exactly (6). Let z_0 be the orthogonal projection of x_0
onto N and replace every x_i by x_i-z_0. The objective, gradient, ridge
coordinates, GD recursion, and fixed-region condition are unchanged,
because all gradients lie in N^perp. The initial squared norm after
this replacement equals the TRUE squared distance in (5). We therefore
work below with x_0 in N^perp and denominator ||x_0||^2.

The pair convention is the original one:

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    f_i=F(x_i), g_i=grad F(x_i), x_star=g_star=f_star=0.

Every Q_ij is nonpositive, including both terminal directions and both
optimum directions. To spell out the accepted necessity argument from
`1252b8823bb5a290`, fix i and define
J(z)=F(z)-<g_i,z>. Convexity and grad J(x_i)=0 make x_i a global
minimizer of J. Smoothness gives
J(x_j-grad J(x_j))<=J(x_j)-||grad J(x_j)||^2/2.
The minimum J(x_i) is at most this value. Rearranging gives Q_ij<=0.
This one argument checks every ordered pair of the actual trajectory;
no subset of remote, zero-step, optimum, or terminal rows is discarded.

## 3. A sharp curvature price, including shifted ridge cores

This section treats a broader geometry than (2): allow an additional
linear term and shifts in every ridge argument, and let each scalar
convex ridge have a nonempty open interval where its second derivative
is the constant alpha_j>0. Assume the whole sum is globally 1-smooth
and its quadratic Hessian A0 is positive semidefinite.

At a point in one ridge core, second differences in an arbitrary
direction z, using nonnegativity of the second differences of all
other convex terms, give

    A0+alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.                         (7)

For two NONPARALLEL directions u,v, their shifted open core slabs
intersect. In fact the map x -> (u^T x,v^T x) is surjective onto R^2:
on span(u,v) its matrix has determinant 1-<u,v>^2>0. Thus any desired
pair of interior core arguments can be achieved simultaneously.
At such a point the same second-difference argument gives

    A0+alpha u u^T+beta v v^T <= I.                    (8)

These arguments require no differentiability of other summands at that
point. For sufficiently small real t the two selected core terms have
second differences exactly t^2 alpha<u,z>^2 and t^2 beta<v,z>^2,
while 1-smoothness bounds the total symmetric second difference by
t^2||z||^2.

Writing rho=<u,v>, the largest eigenvalue of
alpha uu^T+beta vv^T on their span is

    [alpha+beta+sqrt((alpha-beta)^2+4alpha beta rho^2)]/2.

Consequently (8) implies alpha,beta<=1 and the sharp necessary bound

    alpha beta rho^2 <= (1-alpha)(1-beta).             (9)

To obtain (9), square the nonnegative inequality
sqrt((alpha-beta)^2+4alpha beta rho^2)<=2-alpha-beta
and cancel. With A0=0 it is also sufficient for this pair's matrix to
be bounded by I, so this numerical curvature price is sharp.

There is a sharp bound on coupling to A0 as well. For one ridge let
s=1-alpha and write A0 relative to u and u^perp as

    A0 = [ a  b^T ; b  C ].

If b is nonzero, put v=b/||b|| and c=v^T C v. Positivity of A0 and
of I-A0-alpha uu^T, restricted to span(u,v), gives

    ||b||^2<=a c,  ||b||^2<=(s-a)(1-c),
    0<=a<=s, 0<=c<=1.

If a<=s(1-c), the first bound is at most s c(1-c). If
a>=s(1-c), the second bound is at most that same quantity. Hence

    ||(I-uu^T)A0 u|| <= sqrt(1-alpha)/2.              (10)

The case b=0 already satisfies this. Equality is attained in dimension
two by u=e_1 and

    A_s=(1/2)[s,sqrt(s);sqrt(s),1], alpha=1-s,
    0<s<1.                                          (11)

Both A_s and I-A_s-alpha e_1e_1^T are rank-one positive semidefinite
matrices. This is an ACTUAL available coupling, not a forbidden one.

In particular a ridge of full individual curvature alpha=1 satisfies
A0 u=0: (7) gives u^T A0 u=0, and positive semidefiniteness implies
A0 u=0. Any two nonparallel full-curvature ridge directions are
orthogonal by (9). Parallel shifted ridge terms must be grouped into
one scalar function; they need not have overlapping cores.

It follows that a sum consisting entirely of full-curvature convex
ridges plus a PSD quadratic, with any shifts and any linear term,
separates orthogonally into scalar ridge groups and an independent
quadratic on their orthogonal complement. If its minimum is attained,
its minimizer set is the Cartesian product of the scalar minimizer
sets and the quadratic minimizer affine subspace. Squared distance to
that product is the sum of squared component distances. GD separates
in the same coordinates. For every h the true-distance payoff is
therefore a weighted average of the actual scalar-group payoffs and
quadratic eigenmode payoffs

    t * product_(i<n)(1-h_i*t)^2, 0<=t<=1.             (12)

A component starting in its minimizer set stays there and contributes
zero to both numerator and denominator. This proves that rotating or
adding such full-curvature ridges cannot yield a genuinely coupled
multidimensional improvement over the best component on the SAME word.
It does not bound arbitrary scalar multi-return profiles by K_n(h).

## 4. Exact terminal algebra and the fixed-region bound

Return to the centered model (2). For a fixed-region run absorb all
always-core ridges into the quadratic matrix and write

    A=A0+sum_(j in Core) alpha_j u_j u_j^T,
    b=sum_(j in Outer) epsilon_j q_j u_j,
    D0=sum_(j in Outer) q_j^2/alpha_j,                 (13)

where epsilon_j is +1 or -1 for its chosen outer ray, and
q_j=alpha_j times its radius on that ray. Every actual sample satisfies

    f_i=x_i^T A x_i/2+b^T x_i-D0/2,  g_i=A x_i+b.    (14)

Condition (3) becomes

    A+sum_(j in Outer) alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.         (15)

Let L=I-A. For every vector z, scalar Cauchy--Schwarz and (15) give

    (b^T z)^2 <= D0 * z^T L z.                       (16)

Consequently b is orthogonal to ker L. Define L^dagger by reciprocating
its positive eigenvalues and assigning zero to its zero eigenvalues.
Putting z=L^dagger b in (16), or treating b=0 separately, proves

    D0 >= b^T L^dagger b.                            (17)

Thus the intercept cost required by global smoothness is retained even
when many nonorthogonal ridge forces add in the same direction.

Define polynomials (with their continuous values at t=0)

    R_h(t)=(1-P_h(t))/t, R_h(0)=S,
    T_h(t)=(1-P_h(t)^2)/t, T_h(0)=2S.

Unrolling g_i=A x_i+b yields

    x_n=P_h(A)x_0-R_h(A)b.                           (18)

This identity is also proved inductively using
R_(prefix+h)(t)=(1-h*t)R_prefix(t)+h. Substituting (18) into (14),
and using I-A R_h(A)=P_h(A), gives the EXACT identity

    2f_n=x_0^T A P_h(A)^2 x_0
           +2b^T P_h(A)^2 x_0-D0-b^T T_h(A)b.        (19)

No radius, value, forcing term, or terminal quantity has been removed.

Assume now the polynomial condition

    P_h(t)^2 <= 1/(1+2S*t), 0<=t<=1.                 (20)

Diagonalize A orthogonally. For one eigenvalue t in (0,1), abbreviate
p=P_h(t)^2 and

    d=1/(1-t)+(1-p)/t.

By (17), its contribution to the right side of (19) is bounded by

    t p X^2+2p X B-d B^2,                            (21)

where X,B are the coordinates of x_0,b on this eigenvector. We claim
(21)<=K X^2. First d>0 by (20), and
t p<=t/(1+2S*t)<=K. The determinant needed to complete the square is

    (K-t p)d-p^2
       =[K-p(K+t(1-K))]/[t(1-t)] >=0,               (22)

because K/[K+t(1-K)]=1/(1+2S*t). Thus the two-variable quadratic
K X^2-(21) is positive semidefinite.

At t=0, p=1, and its contribution is at most
2XB-(1+2S)B^2<=K X^2 by completing a square. At t=1, (16) gives
B=0, and the contribution is P_h(1)^2 X^2<=K X^2 by (20).
Summing all eigenvector inequalities proves 2f_n<=K||x_0||^2.
Section 2 identified that norm with the true closest-minimizer distance.
This proves the first quantitative claim in Section 1, including zero
eigenvalues, unit eigenvalues, empty outer sets, and zero physical steps.

Equality is attained by the actual scalar global Huber witness for ANY
nonnegative word: take A0=0, one unit ridge of curvature 1, both radii
q=1/(1+2S), and initial point x_0=1. All iterates are
x_i=1-q sum_(t<i)h_t >=(1+S)/(1+2S)>=q. The fixed positive outer
region condition includes the terminal point. Direct substitution gives
2F(x_n)=1/(1+2S), the initial true distance is 1, and the minimizer is
unique. Hence whenever (20) holds the restricted supremum is exactly K.

## 5. The resolvent inequality on the ORIGINAL H_m, with all curvatures

Fix an integer m>=128, and keep the following enclosing-horizon
parameters fixed in EVERY child word:

    sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

The actual physical word is h=H_m, of length n=2^m-1. Write
S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, r_c=c-2, and A_mass=a/r_c. Counting the
occurrences of each bridge gives

    M_j=A_mass*c^j+(1-A_mass)*2^j,
    P_j(t)=product_(l=0)^(j-1)(1-d_l*t)^(2^(j-l-1)).   (23)

Here P_j is the polynomial of the complete physical word H_j; P_0=1.

The following bounds follow from squaring the rational comparisons
7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7 and using m>=128:

    12/5<c<49/20, 3/8<a<sigma-2<c-2<9/20,
    127/128<=ac<1, 0<A_mass<1, c*r_c<5/4.            (24)

For the upper c bound, c<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20.
For the lower a bound, a>(127/128)/(49/20)>3/8. The upper bound on
a follows from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)sigma]<1/sigma=sigma-2.
In particular (23) gives M_j<=c^j and

    t*S_j >= a*c^j*t/r_c-t.                          (25)

We prove (20) for this exact h. At t=0 it is equality. Fix t in (0,1].
If every d_l*t<1 for 0<=l<m, all factors are nonnegative and
1-z<=exp(-z) gives

    P_m(t)^2 <= exp(-2S_m*t) <= 1/(1+2S_m*t).        (26)

If a factor is zero the required conclusion is immediate. Otherwise
let j be the first level with d_j*t>=1. Set

    v_l=a*c^l*t, Y_l=c*v_l*|P_l(t)|.

If j=0 then Y_0=ac*t<=1. If j>=1, all factors of P_j are on their
nonnegative side, and t<1/d_0<8/11. From (25),

    Y_j<=c*v_j*exp(-v_j/r_c+t)
         <=c*r_c*exp(t-1)<1.                         (27)

The middle bound maximizes v exp(-v/r_c) over v>=0, with maximum
r_c/e. The last inequality uses c*r_c<5/4 and
exp(3/11)>1+3/11=14/11>5/4.

For every l>=j, the exact physical recursion in (23) gives

    Y_(l+1)=[1-(1-t)/v_l]*Y_l^2 <=Y_l^2<=1.          (28)

The bracket is between zero and one, since d_l*t>=1 and t<=1.
Consequently, with z=a*c^m*t,

    P_m(t)^2 <= 1/(c^2*z^2).                         (29)

Activation implies t>=1/d_(m-1). Since m>=3, (24) implies

    z>=c*[a*c^(m-1)]/[1+a*c^(m-1)]
       >(12/5)*(54/25)/(1+54/25)=648/395>3/2.        (30)

Also S_m<c^m by (23). Therefore

    (1+2S_m*t)P_m(t)^2
       <=[1+(16/3)z]/[(144/25)z^2]
       <=25/36<1.                                  (31)

For the final inequality the function z^(-2)+(16/3)z^(-1) decreases
for z>0, and its value at z=3/2 gives 25/36 after multiplication by
25/144. Equations (26) and (31) cover EVERY t in [0,1]. In particular
there was no curvature grid, schedule replay, or fixed-curvature premise.

## 6. Same-word scalar comparison and the ORIGINAL benchmark

We also rederive that the best accepted Huber/single-cut value on this
word is the global Huber value, so the comparison is on the SAME word.
For a cut at level j its squared numerator is (a*c^j)^2. Holding the
total S_m and bridge d_j fixed, the denominator is

    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_m-d_j-P)],

where P is the actual preceding mass. Its logarithm has strictly
negative second derivative in the interior of the allowed mass range.
Thus its minimum over the interval between the first and last occurrence
is at an endpoint. The palindrome H_m makes the following mass at its
first occurrence at least S_(m-1), and its preceding mass is S_j. At
the last occurrence the preceding mass is at least S_(m-1), and the
following mass is S_j. Put M=M_(m-1).

From (23), a*c^j<=r_c*M_j, M_j<=M, and M_m<=c*M. The first-occurrence
cut is at most r_c^2/(2M-1). The last-occurrence cut is at most

    r_c^2*M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)] <= r_c^2/(2M-1),

because x^2/(2x-1) is nondecreasing for x>=1. Finally c<5/2,
r_c<1/2, and M>=1 give

    r_c^2(2M_m-1) <=(5M-1)/4 <=2M-1.

Every cut in accepted `b98bb807d514e80d` is therefore no larger than
1/(2M_m-1). Its global Huber term attains that value. Hence

    sup_fixed-region V = K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).         (32)

For the limit, divide (23), at j=m, by sigma^m. Since a/(c-2)->1,
(1+1/m)^m->e, and (2/sigma)^m->0, one gets

    M_m/sigma^m -> e.

The accepted ray theorem `04219554e41d9750` supplies
B_(2^m-1)/sigma^m -> Z_1. Thus the exact restricted ratio has limit

    B_(2^m-1)*sup_fixed-region V -> Z_1/(2e).         (33)

For an explicit strict gap, its accepted upper enclosure at b=1,j=0
gives Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2. The accepted original recurrence gives
B_1=4. Therefore Z_1<=41209/10000. The positive exponential series
gives e>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3, whence

    Z_1/(2e)<123627/160000<4/5.                      (34)

No fit, substituted recurrence, internal capacity, or scalar radius
of a higher-dimensional witness is used in this comparison.

## 7. Release the terminal region, retaining the original last step

Continue with exactly the full-horizon parameters of Section 5, and put

    ell=d_0=1+a, S_pre=S_m-ell,
    h_pre=H_m with its last physical entry ell deleted.

This is a deletion in the SAME word with the SAME m,a,c. It is not a
smaller ruler with recomputed parameters. Its polynomial is

    P_pre(t)=P_m(t)/(1-ell*t),                        (35)

where the right side is interpreted as polynomial division. We prove

    P_pre(t)^2 <= 1/(1+2S_pre*t), 0<=t<=1.            (36)

At t=0 this is equality. If any factor is zero, P_pre is zero too:
the factor 1-ell*t has multiplicity 2^(m-1)-1>=1 after deletion,
and all other level factors retain their positive multiplicities.
If there is no activated level d_j*t>=1, the exponential proof (26)
applies directly to the truncated word with its exact sum S_pre.

We may therefore assume positive t, no zero factors, and an activated
level. Define q=|1-ell*t|>0 and z=a*c^m*t. Section 5 supplied

    (1+2S_m*t)/(c^2*z^2)<=25/36,
    Y_(l+1)<=Y_l^2 after the first activated level.    (37)

If t<=1/(10ell), then q>=9/10 and (29),(37) give

    (1+2S_pre*t)P_pre(t)^2
       <=(25/36)/(9/10)^2=625/729<1.                (38)

For the remaining t>1/(10ell), the first activated level j is at most
5. Indeed ell<3/2, hence t>1/15; also
c^4>(12/5)^4>25 and c>2, so d_5>1+(3/8)*50=79/4>15.

First suppose 1<=j<=5. The proof of (27) has the uniform strengthening

    Y_j < (441/400)*(11/14)=4851/5600<7/8.           (39)

All factors in P_j are nonnegative and at most one, and one is q, so
|P_j|<=q. First activation also gives
v_j=c*a*c^(j-1)*t<c, since d_(j-1)*t<1. Thus Y_j<=c^2*q.
For positive M,C,q and integer k>=1, the elementary inequality

    min(M,Cq)^k/q <= C*M^(k-1)                      (40)

follows by treating Cq<=M and Cq>=M separately. Applying it with
M=7/8,C=c^2,k=2^(m-j), and iterating (28), yields

    Y_m/q <= c^2*(7/8)^(2^(m-j)-1).

Here k>=32, since m>=128 and j<=5. Squaring, using (37), and using
(7/8)^8<1/2 gives

    (1+2S_pre*t)P_pre(t)^2
       <=(25/36)c^4*(7/8)^(2(k-1))
       <(25/36)(625/16)/64=15625/36864<1.            (41)

For completeness, (7/8)^8<1/2 follows, for example, from
5*7^4=12005<12288=3*8^4: its fourth power is less than 3/5,
so its eighth power is less than 9/25. Since 2(k-1)>=62>=48,
six such eighth-power bounds suffice in (41).

It remains to treat first activation j=0. Put delta=ac=1-1/m.
Then Y_0=delta*t<=delta, and (28) gives Y_1<=delta^2. Directly,
Y_1=c^2*a*t*q=c*delta*t*q<(5/2)q. Apply (40) with
M=delta^2,C=5/2,k=2^(m-1). It gives

    Y_m/q <=(5/2)delta^(2^m-2).

As 2^(m+1)-4>=4m for m>=4, delta<=exp(-1/m) and e>2 imply

    (1+2S_pre*t)P_pre(t)^2
       <=(25/36)(25/4)delta^(2^(m+1)-4)
       <=(625/144)exp(-4)<625/2304<1.                (42)

All cases in (36) are now proved, including curvatures arbitrarily
close to the removed factor's root. No division by a vanishing factor
or curvature-dependent positive lower bound on q was assumed.

Consider an actual run of (2) whose queried samples x_0,...,x_(n-1)
share fixed regions, while x_n may lie anywhere. Section 4 applied to
the exact prefix h_pre, with its last sample x_(n-1), and (36), give

    2F(x_(n-1)) <= ||x_0||^2/(1+2S_pre).

The ORIGINAL last step ell lies in (1,3/2). Global 1-smoothness yields

    F(x_n)<=F(x_(n-1))-ell*(1-ell/2)||g_(n-1)||^2
            <=F(x_(n-1)).                            (43)

The true initial norm is retained as in Section 2. Thus the larger
class with arbitrary actual terminal region satisfies

    1/(1+2S_m) <= sup_fixed-queried-region V
                 <=1/(1+2S_m-2ell).                 (44)

The lower bound is the same actual global Huber witness. Since ell is
bounded and S_m tends to infinity, its ratio to that Huber value tends
to one. Equations (33)-(34) therefore hold for this larger class too.
The terminal is an actual sample of the same static function, and both
of its original interpolation directions remain legal by Section 2.

## 8. The coupled fixed-region class is nonempty on every word

Here is a fully specified genuinely coupled instance satisfying its
region hypothesis for ANY finite nonnegative h. It is included to rule
out a merely vacuous obstruction. Work in R^2 and put

    u=e_1, w=(e_1+e_2)/sqrt(2),
    eta=1/[8(1+S)], A0=eta*w*w^T,
    alpha=1/2, q=eta, r_minus=r_plus=q/alpha,
    F(x)=x^T A0 x/2+alpha*phi(u^T x), x_0=e_1.       (45)

The matrices A0 and uu^T do not commute. Also
A0+alpha uu^T <=(eta+alpha)I<=5I/8<I. The minimizer is unique zero,
since the two distinct directions w and u span R^2.

To check the actual gates without numerical evaluation, first run the
explicit affine recursion z_(i+1)=(I-h_i A0)z_i-h_i q u, z_0=e_1.
Every factor I-h_i A0 has norm at most one, because
0<=h_i eta<=S/[8(1+S)]<1. Consequently
||z_i||<=1+q sum_(t<i)h_t<=9/8. Its displacement from e_1 satisfies

    ||z_i-e_1||<=sum_(t<i)h_t(eta*9/8+q)<17/64.

Thus u^T z_i>47/64, whereas r_plus=1/[4(1+S)]<=1/4.
Every sample including z_n is strictly in the positive outer region.
It follows inductively that z_i IS the actual GD run of the single
function (45). Section 2 supplies all original pairs and true radius 1.
The payoff need not equal (32); Section 4 shows that on H_m it cannot
exceed (32). This is not a scalar copy hidden behind a coordinate change:
its actual outer Hessian A0 and central Hessian A0+alpha uu^T do not
commute, although this particular run only visits the outer region.

## 9. A static multiple-sign escape, and its honest payoff comparison

The next example attacks the fragile extension that the polynomial
condition alone might bound ALL trajectories of (2) by the global Huber
value. It preserves that condition and releases the fixed-region premise.
It also shows that the curvature rigidity at alpha=1 is not valid for
arbitrarily small positive slack. It is a scope example, not a new
competitive lower bound on H_m or on arbitrary words.

Take the fixed physical word

    h=(1,4,1,4,1), S=11, K=1/23.                    (46)

Its polynomial is P_h(t)=(1-t)^3(1-4t)^2. For 0<=t<=1/4, all factors
are nonnegative, so P_h(t)^2<=exp(-22t)<=1/(1+22t). For t>=1/4 put
z=1-t in [0,3/4]. Then P_h(t)=z^3(3-4z)^2. Its derivative as a
function of z is z^2(3-4z)(9-20z), and the maximum is

    (9/20)^3*(6/5)^2=6561/50000<1/7.

It follows that (1+22t)P_h(t)^2<=23/49<1 on this second interval.
Thus (20) holds on ALL of [0,1] for the same word (46).

Let phi have radii r_minus=3/4 and r_plus=1/2. Fix ANY
0<epsilon<=1/1000000 before the run, put s=epsilon^2, and define

    F_epsilon(x,y)=(epsilon*x+y)^2/4+(1-s)*phi(x),
    (x_0,y_0)=(5/4,0).                              (47)

Its quadratic is A_s from (11), its ridge direction is e_1, and its
ridge curvature is 1-s. Thus it is globally convex and 1-smooth. Its
unique minimizer is (0,0), giving true initial squared distance 25/16.
Its outer and central Hessians are

    A_s=(1/2)[s,epsilon;epsilon,1],
    C_s=A_s+(1-s)e_1e_1^T.

They do not commute for epsilon>0. Both are PSD and bounded by I, since
I-C_s=(1/2)(epsilon,-1)(epsilon,-1)^T.

At epsilon=0, the exact first-coordinate trajectory on (46) is

    5/4, 3/4, -5/4, -1/2, 3/2, 1,                  (48)

and the second coordinate stays zero. The corresponding first gradient
coordinates are

    1/2, 1/2, -3/4, -1/2, 1/2, 1/2.

Its ridge regions are positive outer, positive outer, negative outer,
core, positive outer, positive outer. Every listed point has distance
at least 1/4 from the boundary of its specified open region.

Here is a uniform TEXTUAL perturbation bound establishing those SAME
actual region visits for every epsilon in the specified interval.
The matrix change A_s-A_0 has norm at most epsilon, and |phi'|<=3/4.
At every unperturbed sample, whose norm is at most 3/2, this gives

    ||grad F_epsilon(z_i^0)-grad F_0(z_i^0)||<=3epsilon.

Let E_i=||z_i^epsilon-z_i^0|| for the actual GD runs, and put
D_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t). Global 1-smoothness gives
E_(i+1)<=(1+h_i)E_i+3epsilon*h_i, and hence

    E_i<=3epsilon(D_i-1)<=597epsilon<1/1000.          (49)

The product at n=5 is exactly 2*5*2*5*2=200. Equation (49) is a
five-step analytic perturbation estimate, not a numerical replay.
It preserves every strict gate in (48). In particular the actual run
visits A_s,C_s,A_s at queried indices 2,3,4. It has TWO actual sign
changes in both the first position coordinate and first gradient
coordinate, with gradient signs +,+,-,-,+,+.

The correlations are not constant, and the gradients are not collinear.
For the first two actual gradients, which use the positive outer region,
direct substitution gives

    g_0=(1/2+s/8,5epsilon/8),
    g_1=((1-s)(1/2+s/16),epsilon(1-s)/16),
    det(g_0,g_1)=-epsilon(1-s)(9+s)/32 !=0.           (50)

Their inner product is positive. The error of any actual gradient from
its unperturbed counterpart is at most E_i+3epsilon<=600epsilon.
Thus <g_1,g_2><0: its unperturbed value is -3/8, while the absolute
change is at most (5/4)(600epsilon)+(600epsilon)^2<1/100.
This proves distinct normalized correlations as well as noncollinearity.

All original ordered Q rows, including the actual terminal rows, follow
from Section 2 applied to (47). No Hessian is chosen online.

The payoff comparison must keep the true radius. At z_5^0=(1,0),

    F_epsilon(1,0)=3/8-s/8,
    grad F_epsilon(1,0)=(1/2,epsilon/2).

This gradient has norm less than one. Convexity, smoothness and (49)
therefore give |F_epsilon(z_5^epsilon)-3/8|<1/100. Since the true
squared radius is 25/16,

    9/20 < V_epsilon=(32/25)F_epsilon(z_5^epsilon)<1/2. (51)

For example the stronger bounds from 3/8 plus or minus 1/100 are
2336/5000<V_epsilon<2464/5000, which imply (51).
This strictly exceeds the global Huber value 1/23 despite (20), so
dropping the fixed-region premise of Section 4 is refuted.

On this SAME word the exact accepted single-cut maximum is

    K_5(h)=max(1/23,9/52,3/49)=9/52<9/20.            (52)

Nevertheless the stronger accepted scalar construction
`1fca3e5dd555cb4a` already gives 73/48>1/2. Its two checkpoints are
4 and 4, with all three ordinary masses equal to 1. The fact's exact
parameters are m_0=1/2,m_1=3/4,O_1=1/2,z_term=7/4,e_term=1/4,
and q_opt=7/12. Feasibility is z_term>=2m_0=1, and its payoff is

    2e_term+z_term^2/3=1/2+49/48=73/48.              (53)

Thus (47) is an actual nonlinear, noncommuting, multiple-sign scope
example with a checked quantitative gap over the single-cut calibration.
It is DOMINATED by an already accepted scalar witness and is not new
lower-bound strength. The word (46) is not H_m and supplies neither the
arbitrary-h nor the non-dyadic quantifiers of the project goal.

## 10. Interface left for the next authorized round

The fixed-region mechanism on the original H_m is exactly Huber-tight,
even with an arbitrary number of centered ridges, arbitrary dimension,
subunit ridge curvatures, and noncommuting quadratic coupling. The
larger class with only the queried samples in fixed regions and an
arbitrary actual terminal region is asymptotically Huber-tight. The
full-curvature relay is additionally forced to split into scalar groups.
These are separate scoped exclusions; neither excludes all static ridge
sums.

The explicit escape (47) shows the remaining mechanism is real: a
subunit ridge can visit its core between opposite outer visits, while
the background quadratic generates changing correlations. What remains
missing is a fixed static construction with gates changing DURING THE
QUERIED PREFIX on ORIGINAL H_m, with a
terminal value exceeding its accepted scalar baseline, with all region
visits and true distance controlled uniformly. The matrices and affine
forcing are fixed by (47), not freely selectable at each bridge. There
is no authorized numerical search or verifier call for that step.

For max's actual31 state, this result supplies a price only if a proposed
realization is specifically a centered Huber-ridge sum of (2). No claim
is made that its conjugate interpolant has that representation. Its
positive endpoint state, changed values, residuals, and minimizer hull
cannot be inserted into (19) without proving the representation and
the fixed-region hypothesis. C59 likewise is not claimed to belong to
this family, and its fixed H6 parameters were never replaced by those
of another enclosing horizon.

Literature context only: Aravkin, Burke, and Pillonetto, arXiv:1301.4566v2,
Remark 4 (Huber scalar example) and Remark 5 (sum representation), were
read from the exact PDF. The downloaded PDF is retained in downloads/.
Local extraction failed because pdftotext and Python PDF readers were
absent; the web PDF extractor supplied the relevant text. No installation
was made. Those remarks are not external mathematical premises: (1),
its pieces, every matrix bound, and all dynamics above are derived here.

# max67: an asymmetric exact-copy transport and its full-objective obstruction

Status: complete joint analytical draft, UNVERIFIED. There is no new fact,
formal submission, unrestricted objective upper bound, or actual lower
witness. The original minimax problem remains OPEN.

This round tests one specific asymmetric transport. It copies the right
child's finite interpolation coefficients, scales its optimum attachments
separately, and permits arbitrary position-dependent corrections throughout
the left block. Its sole repair adds forward cross-block mass and makes the
backward transport affine in the right child's chronological position.

The outcome is a full-original-space PSD obstruction, not just a failed
bridge inequality. For every actual enclosing m>=1536, every sign-valid,
value-cancelling completion in the specified family would require

                  q_cert*(1+2 sum H_m) > 11/10.                 (0)

The same conclusion covers the unrepaired rule. With the original benchmark,
any sequence of PSD levels within this family would satisfy

       liminf_m B_(2^m-1)*q_cert,m > 3619/3600 > 1.             (1)

Thus this particular rule cannot supply the required improvement at
unbounded true horizons. It does not exclude different terminal destinations,
a different right optimum scale, corrections to the right finite block,
non-affine transport, optimum-outgoing coefficients at a larger objective
level, or general asymmetric full certificates.

## 1. Exact physical words, verified inputs, and the viability requirement

Fix one enclosing integer m. Throughout the entire hierarchy use

 lambda_s=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4m),
 c=lambda_s*(1+1/(4m)), a=beta_word/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

Every occurrence of a child uses these SAME parameters. The number of
physical updates in H_m is n_m=2^m-1. The terminal gradient is a formal
interpolation variable; it supplies no extra update.

The accepted mathematical inputs are fact1252b8823bb5a290, for the original
objective cone; fact04219554e41d9750, for the unconditional original benchmark
and its certified interval table; and fact5fd396eba75651e7, for the stated
Huber/benchmark comparison on these exact words. No conditional seed clause
of fact042 is used. The two numerical/analytical findings242f8d453ac26183
and dd4045b39aa0c257 are provenance and awareness, not correctness premises.

Before attempting a recursive PSD proof, a prospective whole-word level
q_cert=chi/(1+2 sum H_m) must be checked at the original B_n scale. The
accepted inequality B_(n_m)/(1+2 sum H_m)<237/250 would suffice with a
uniform chi<250/237. Multiplying a fixed loss greater than one at every
depth does not meet this condition. For example (101/100)^m>=1+m/100,
so an assertion of a one-percent loss at each level cannot be replaced
by an assertion of a one-percent whole-word loss. The obstruction below
is stronger: even the loss needed at the final join is too large at the
actual limiting B_n scale.

All new calculations in this document are symbolic textual derivations.
No schedule, recurrence, matrix, spectrum, fitted coefficient, or benchmark
has been evaluated by a mathematical program in this round.

## 2. Original cone and an exact full matrix formula

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For independent formal vectors
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n), define x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t. All distinct ordered
original interpolation rows remain in the model:

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Let lambda_ij be their nonnegative multipliers. Values cancel in

 M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij*Q_ij               (2)

exactly when out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n) at every finite i. A valid upper
certificate requires PSD on the ENTIRE original formal space, as specified
in fact1252. In the tested family lambda_(star,i)=0. Write
nu_i=lambda_(i,star), and z_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t. Once flow cancels values,
the complete quadratic, with every original gradient retained, is

 M(q)=q||x_0||^2-sum_i nu_i<g_i,x_0>
      +sum_i nu_i[-<g_i,z_i>+||g_i||^2/2]
      +sum_(i!=j finite)lambda_ij
           [<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].                 (3)

Thus its full matrix is [[q,-nu^T/2],[-nu/2,C]], where C acts on
all n+1 finite gradient coordinates. If h_n=0, its entries are

 C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(j>i)lambda_ij,
 2C_ij=-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)lambda_iv
          +h_i*(nu_j+sum_(v<=i)lambda_jv), i<j.             (4)

The sums in (4) are over finite labels; out_i includes nu_i. To verify
the diagonal, the squared gradient differences contribute half the incident
mass, which is out_i-1_(i=n) by flow. A forward position term based at i
contributes -h_i*lambda_ij. For i<j, squared differences contribute
-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji) to the cross coefficient. Position terms based at i
with destination after j contribute -h_j times their multipliers; those
based at j with destination at or before i, including the optimum,
contribute h_i times their multipliers. This proves (3)-(4) directly.

Zeros assigned below are restrictions on this candidate's coefficients,
not assertions that any original interpolation row is redundant.

## 3. One asymmetric transport; exactly one affine repair

All quantities introduced in this section are local to the displayed join;
in particular its gradient matrix C is distinct from the benchmark C_n.
Consider a parent (H_j,d,H_j), where k=2^j-1, j>=1, and d=1+a*c^j.
Let the child steps be u_0,...,u_(k-1), with

 p_i=sum_(v<i)u_v, 0<=i<=k, A=p_k>0,
 t=A+d, S=2A+d, D=1+2S, D_child=1+2A.

Left finite labels are L_l=l, 0<=l<=k; L_k is the physical bridge
query. Right finite labels are R_i=k+1+i, 0<=i<=k. The actual parent
terminal is R_k=2k+1. Their chronological masses are p_l and t+p_i.

Take ANY nonnegative reference finite array K_ij (i!=j, 0<=i,j<=k), K_ii=0,
and nonnegative optimum attachment vector mu_i satisfying

 sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+mu_i=2*1_(i=k).                    (5)

Summing (5) gives sum_i mu_i=2. No PSD assumption, objective bound,
rank, or child oracle is used in the obstruction. Incoming child-terminal
coefficients in K may be present. In particular the ensuing argument is
stronger than one limited to already certified children.

Set

 theta=(3A+d)/(2S), s=1-theta=(A+d)/(2S).                 (6)

The right finite block is an EXACT copy and its optimum attachments have
the separate scale theta:

 lambda_(R_i,R_v)=K_iv, lambda_(R_i,star)=theta*mu_i.     (7)

This exact-copy condition and (6) are new candidate restrictions, not
conclusions drawn from the floating-point fits.

The base rule is kappa=0. Its ONLY repair is

                         kappa=s/(2d).                  (8)

For either choice, define the complete backward transport by

 lambda_(R_i,L_0)=mu_i*[s-kappa*d*(A-p_i)/A],
 lambda_(R_i,L_k)=mu_i*kappa*t*(A-p_i)/A,
 lambda_(R_i,L_l)=0 for 0<l<k.                           (9)

Retain the forward direction, with arbitrary nonnegative multipliers
lambda_(L_l,R_i) whose column totals are

 F_i=sum_l lambda_(L_l,R_i)=kappa*mu_i*(A-p_i).           (10)

Both directions are therefore explicitly present in the repaired rule.
If mu_i>0 and i<k, F_i>0; the repaired backward coefficient to L_0 is
strictly positive too. At the original terminal, p_k=A, so F_k=0,
lambda_(R_k,L_k)=0 and lambda_(R_k,L_0)=s*mu_k.

ALL left finite rows lambda_(L_l,L_v), and ALL left optimum attachments
nu_(L_l), may be chosen independently of any scalar child-copy law,
with arbitrary dependence on position, m and q. The obstruction grants
every nonnegative such choice for which full original flow holds. This
includes adding nonzero incoming edges at the bridge query L_k. There
is no assumption that it remains an unqueried child terminal.

Here is an explicit full nonnegative flow completion, showing that these
prescribed rows are not merely a hypothetical feasible-set description.
Put

 E=sum_i mu_i*(A-p_i), 0<=E<=2A.

Send every forward F_i from L_k; set all left finite pairs to zero; set

 nu_(L_0)=2s-kappa*d*E/A, nu_(L_k)=kappa*d*E/A,
 nu_(L_l)=0 for 0<l<k.                                  (11)

Every optimum-outgoing coefficient is zero. Together (7),(9)-(11) specify
every original multiplier. The theorem below also covers all the more
general left corrections and forward distributions just described.

### Exact signs and value cancellation

The inequalities 0<theta<1, s>0, d>0 and 0<=p_i<=A imply all the claimed
signs. In the repaired rule,

 s-kappa*d*(A-p_i)/A=s*(A+p_i)/(2A)>=s/2.

In (11), 0<=kappa*d*E/A<=s, so nu_(L_0)>=s>0 and nu_(L_k)>=0.
The base has nu_(L_0)=2s, nu_(L_k)=0. All other coefficients are
nonnegative multiples of mu_i or entries of K, or are explicitly zero.

The backward outgoing total at R_i is

 B_i=mu_i*[s+kappa*(A-p_i)]=s*mu_i+F_i.                 (12)

By (5), the right value flow is
(2*1_(i=k)-mu_i)+theta*mu_i+B_i-F_i=2*1_(i=k).
At L_0, the incoming backward sum equals the first quantity in (11).
At L_k, incoming backward minus outgoing forward is
kappa*(t/A-1)*E=kappa*d*E/A, equal to its optimum attachment.
All other left flows vanish in the explicit completion. Thus every
finite f_i cancels in (2), including f_n; the total optimum incoming
mass is 2. No interpolation row or value variable is silently omitted.

### Why these coefficients were tested

The base routes the right terminal's backward correction to the actual
initial left sample. If an input child has exact Huber contact and mu_k>0,
its terminal row equation is sum_v K_kv*(A-p_v)=A*mu_k.
For the parent, that row at exact Huber contact would require
S*theta*mu_k=A*mu_k+S*s*mu_k. Solving gives (6). Thus theta is derived
from this chosen terminal attachment in the original identity. It is
not a fitted numerical constant.

The base's right-node flow permits no forward mass. The sole repair
allows such mass to increase linearly with the remaining right mass
A-p_i and distributes backward mass between the original endpoints
L_0,L_k. Formula (9) has the required mass (12) and first moment
kappa*t*(A-p_i), which interpolates the two endpoint contact conditions.
Its interior contact defect is calculated explicitly in Section 9.
The largest kappa allowed by the displayed L_0 sign is s/d; (8) uses
half of this exact cap to leave that multiplier strictly positive.
This is one analytic repair, not a scan over coefficient choices.

The initial-point terminal destination is a substantive restriction of
the tested law. It need not match the numerical optimizers. In particular
this draft does not claim that all asymmetric recursions must use (6).

## 4. Feasible starting arrays and the recursion's actual scope

For H_0, the only nonzero multiplier lambda_(0,star)=2 at q=1 gives
M=||x_0-g_0||^2. For the actual H_1=(b), b=1+a, use

 lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1,

with every other original coefficient zero. Direct expansion gives

 M(1/(1+2b))
   =1/(1+2b)*||x_0-(1+2b)(g_0+g_1)/2||^2
       +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4.                         (13)

For the physical family, a<1/lambda_s=sqrt(2)-1, so
1<b<sqrt(2)<3/2; (13) is PSD on all three formal coordinates.
This base is part of the joint derivation, not a separately promoted fact.

Equations (5),(7),(9)-(11) define an actual nonnegative value-flow
recursion from this base for every later child, always with the enclosing
m fixed. They do NOT define a PSD recursion: that is precisely what the
full-space test below refutes. No new stronger child invariant is presumed,
and no virtual child oracle or primitive-capacity result replaces a parent
calculation. The full proof of failure even grants arbitrary arrays (5)
and arbitrary sign-valid left corrections at the final join.

## 5. The first full-space PSD obstruction

Evaluate the entire original quadratic on the following original-coordinate
linear subspace, in one real dimension:

 g_(L_l)=0 for every 0<=l<=k,
 g_(R_i)=v for every 0<=i<=k,
 x_0=x.                                                   (14)

The terminal gradient remains present and equals v in this TEST subspace;
no equality constraint has been imposed on the certificate. A PSD matrix
must remain nonnegative on every such subspace. The original substitutions
give x_(L_l)=x and x_(R_i)=x-p_i*v, including the terminal.
These formal test vectors are not asserted to satisfy interpolation or to
be an actual convex-function trajectory; they test the full dual matrix.

Every left finite quadratic and left optimum quadratic vanishes on (14),
irrespective of how large or position-dependent its coefficient is. The
right finite block contributes

 sum_(i,l)K_il*(p_i-p_l)*v^2
       =[2A-sum_i mu_i*p_i]*v^2,                         (15)

where (15) is just (5) multiplied by p_i and summed, not a use of
child PSD or an independent-gradient assumption about actual iterates.
The right optimum rows contribute
-2theta*x*v+theta*sum_i mu_i*(p_i+1/2)*v^2.
The backward rows contribute sum_i B_i*(p_i+1/2)*v^2;
the forward rows contribute sum_i F_i*v^2/2. Inserting (12) proves
the exact whole-parent identity

 M(q)|_(14)=q*x^2-2theta*x*v+Gamma*v^2,
 Gamma=1+2A+kappa*sum_i mu_i*(A-p_i)*(1+p_i).             (16)

In particular (16) is linked explicitly to ALL original rows and to the
original (n+2)-coordinate matrix (3)-(4). It is not an autonomous bridge
inequality, a relaxed child's bound, or a virtual matrix of lower order.

For q>0, set v=1 and x=theta/q. This is an explicit full-space direction,
whose quadratic value is

                         Gamma-theta^2/q.              (17)

If q<0, x alone already gives a negative direction. If q=0, the nonzero
coefficient -2theta*x*v in (16) does. Hence any PSD level in this law
is positive and necessarily satisfies q>=theta^2/Gamma.

## 6. A loss larger than 11/10, including the forward repair

For 0<=p<=A, completing a scalar square gives

 (A-p)(1+p)=(A+1)^2/4-[p-(A-1)/2]^2<=(A+1)^2/4.

Since sum mu_i=2, the repaired Gamma satisfies

 Gamma<=1+2A+s*(A+1)^2/(4d)=:Gamma_up.                   (18)

The unrepaired Gamma=1+2A is no larger, so it is enough to treat (18).
Suppose

                    A>=1024, 2/5<=r=d/A<=1/2.          (19)

Then theta=(3+r)/(2(2+r)), s=(1+r)/(2(2+r))<=3/10 and

 Gamma_up=A*[2+s/(4r)]+1+s/(2r)+s/(4rA).

The coefficient of A in 10D*theta^2-11Gamma_up is

 P(r)/[8r(2+r)],
 P(r)=40r^3+64r^2-3r-11.

For r=2/5+w with w>=0, direct symbolic expansion is

 P(r)=3/5+(337/5)w+112w^2+40w^3>=3/5.

Also 8r(2+r)<=10. Therefore

 10D*theta^2-11Gamma_up
   >=3A/50+10theta^2-11[1+s/(2r)+s/(4rA)]>0.           (20)

For the last strict inequality, s/(2r)<=3/8,
s/(4rA)<=3/(16*1024), so the bracket is less than 3/2;
3A/50>=3*1024/50>33/2. Every inequality is exact.

It follows that D*theta^2>(11/10)*Gamma. Consequently for every
chi=qD<=11/10 with q>0, (17) is strictly negative. This proves (0)
under (19). It applies even if all unspecified left multipliers and the
forward distribution are allowed to depend arbitrarily on q and m.
Such choices cannot change (16). If their signs or value flow fail,
they are already disqualified before this PSD test.

This is a restriction on certificate LEVELS IN THIS LAW. It is neither
a lower bound on unrestricted W nor an assertion that the law becomes
PSD at any larger level.

## 7. The unchanged physical hierarchy satisfies (19)

For the actual final join let j=m-1. Counting the occurrences of each
physical step, or proving the same identity by symbolic induction, gives

 A=2^j-1+a*(c^j-2^j)/(c-2), d=1+a*c^j.                 (21)

For m>=1536, put u=1/(4m). Since sqrt(2)<10/7, u<=1/256 gives
c<(17/7)(257/256)=4369/1792<49/20<5/2. Since sqrt(2)>7/5,
c>12/5. Also beta_word>=3/4, hence a>3/10.

Write z=(2/c)^j. Because c>12/5, z<(5/6)^j. The inequality
(5/6)^4=625/1296<1/2 and j>=24 give z<1/64.
Using (21), d>a*c^j, and 1/(c-2)<1/(lambda_s-2)=lambda_s,

 A/d < 1/(c-2)+z/a
       <17/7+5/96<5/2.                                (22)

Conversely, dropping the positive term 2^j-1 from (21) gives

 A>(d-1)*(1-z)/(c-2)
      >(20/9)*(63/64)*(d-1)=(35/16)*(d-1)>2d.          (23)

Here d>12 follows from a>3/10, c>2 and j>=6. Equations (22)-(23)
give 2/5<d/A<1/2. Finally every physical child step exceeds one, so
A>2^j-1>=2^11-1>1024. This proves (19) for EVERY m>=1536 without
replacing a child by a newly parameterized hierarchy or executing a
recurrence. Therefore (0) holds at the required true horizons.

## 8. Comparison with the ORIGINAL benchmark

The accepted unconditional ray theorem in fact042 states

 Z_1=lim_m lambda_s^(-m)*B_(2^m-1),
 Z_1>=lambda_s^(-j)*B_(2^j-1) for every j>=0.

Section 8 of that fact certifies every interval in the public fixed table
research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv, whose SHA256 is
2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
The row 1023 was read in this round, not recomputed. With

 D80=1208925819614629174706176,
 L1023=19205851554343006584447327614,

the accepted interval gives D80*B_1023>=L1023>15850*D80.
For an explicit integer comparison, the last difference is
44377313451134165354438014>0. Also
lambda_s^10=3363+2378sqrt(2)<6727, since sqrt(2)<99/70.
Thus the displayed accepted enclosure and elementary comparisons give

 Z_1>15850/6727>47/20,                                 (24)

where 20*15850-47*6727=831>0. No new DP, interval generation, numerical
root, arithmetic replay, or certificate checker was run.

For completeness, the exponential series and k!>=2*3^(k-2), k>=2,
give

 exp(1/4)<1+1/4+(1/32)/(1-1/12)=113/88<9/7.            (25)

There is strictness because the factorial inequality is strict from k=4.
Accepted fact5fd gives, for the unchanged tau=1/4 physical words,

 B_(n_m)/(1+2 sum H_m) -> Z_1/[2exp(1/4)]>329/360.     (26)

If there were a sequence of full PSD levels q_cert,m in the tested
family, (0) and (26) would imply

 liminf_m B_(n_m)*q_cert,m
    >=(11/10)*Z_1/[2exp(1/4)]>3619/3600>1.

The same statement holds along any unbounded subsequence of such levels.
This proves (1) and rules out a NEGATIVE-alternative certificate through
this law. It does not lower-bound W_(n_m)(H_m): a different multiplier
family could certify a smaller value.

## 9. Why an affine transport also loses exact Huber contact

This additional identity diagnoses the repair, without being a premise
of Sections 5-8. Suppose the reference child really has Huber contact
and mu_k>0. A Huber-tight full parent must also have contact. Indeed
take Hub(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1 and Hub(x)=|x|-1/2 otherwise, and its
actual trajectory from x_0=D. Every x_i=D-P_i>=S+1>1, so all finite
g_i=1 and f_i=x_i-1/2. Its terminal numerator 2f_n is D and its initial
square is D^2;
every finite Q_ij and every Q_(i,star) is zero, and
Q_(star,i)=1-x_i<0. Nonnegative multipliers and PSD at q=1/D force
optimum-outgoing multipliers to vanish and M(D,1,...,1)=0. A PSD
quadratic with zero value at a vector annihilates that vector: apply
it to that vector plus an arbitrary scalar multiple of another vector.

Writing P_i for original chronological mass, expansion of (3) gives
the finite-coordinate contact equations

 (2S-P_i)*nu_i=2h_i+sum_(v finite)lambda_iv*(P_i-P_v).  (27)

More explicitly, if e is the all-ones gradient vector, differentiating
(3) at equal gradients gives
2(Ce)_i=2h_i+(1+P_i)*nu_i+sum_v lambda_iv*(P_i-P_v).
In the position terms, full finite value flow makes the sum of the
position-derivative contributions 2h_i; the remaining derivative based
at i is its displayed outgoing drift. The norm terms contribute nu_i.
The gradient part of M(D,e)=0 is 2Ce=D*nu, which yields (27).

For the right finite copy (7), the child version of (27) subtracts out.
Let backward coefficients more generally have the form
lambda_(R_i,L_l)=mu_i*(b_l+c_l*p_i), with every actual coefficient
nonnegative. Permit arbitrary nonnegative forward coefficients, and
require their terminal column to be zero. Put
B0=sum b_l, B1=sum c_l, M0=sum p_l*b_l, M1=sum p_l*c_l.
Right flow gives F_i/mu_i=B0+B1*p_i-s at every mu_i>0.
The right equations (27) require

 (t+p_i)*(B0+B1*p_i)-(M0+M1*p_i)
       =theta*t-s*(2A-p_i).                            (28)

If there are at least three distinct positions with mu_i>0, equality
of the two degree-at-most-two polynomials at those positions forces
B1=0. At the terminal, zero forward inflow and mu_k>0 then give
B0=s. Thus every F_i=0. At mu_i=0, flow and the backward formula
also force F_i=0. Nonnegativity makes EVERY forward coefficient zero.

Consequently an affine backward source profile cannot retain genuine
forward mass while simultaneously preserving this exact finite copy,
a single right attachment scale, and Huber contact. This is scoped to
the stated law; it does not follow from the approximate numerical fits.

For the explicit repair (9), the backward first moment is
kappa*t*(A-p_i). At the endpoints p_i=0,A the intended right equation
is matched. At an interior position its drift exceeds the required drift
by exactly kappa*mu_i*p_i*(A-p_i). This provides the concrete interior
contact defect. The result of Sections 5-8 is stronger for the selected
theta,kappa: raising q within a small whole-word budget cannot repair
the full PSD failure, even if the contact requirement is abandoned.
